INTRODUCTION

WHAT IS BITCOIN?

The world has advanced in the last few decades. What was once just a fantasy has become reality. What was once just a thought, a pipe dream, has become a true industry worth billions. The world - and especially the world of business and financial institutions has changed before our very eyes in just several short decades.

Perhaps the biggest catalyst for growth in nearly every industry has been the internet. The worldwide web has changed almost everything it has touched and has created massive fortune and massive billionaires in its wake. And while the world of many businesses has transformed over the years, the scope, power, and functionality of the internet have changed too. When the internet was first launched for average consumers, it was a slow and unfocused prospect. People logged online to chat with others, buy a few items, read the daily news.

Now the internet has exploded into an entire system that lives and dies on its own. It has its own heartbeat, its own life force. The internet is more than the few popular sites that made their name with it back in the 1990s. Now the internet is a behemoth of business, pleasure, and life in general.

With the vast expansion of the internet and its powers, a lot of focus has been paid to the might of all things digital. Digital is a word that not many people were familiar with at the beginning of the 90s. It seemed like something out of a science fiction film. Now everything has become digitized. Your bank account, your tax returns, and paycheck are digital via your financial institution and your workplace, your personality and information about yourself are digital thanks to multiple social media sites, your family life is digital because of webcam apps and other forms of communication. Shopping has become digitized, as has watching a movie or listening to music. What was once a buzzword that few people truly understood has now become something so commonplace that even a grade-schooler knows what it means.

Something else has become digitized in the years since the internet exploded; the way we do business. It isn’t just that you can buy books and movies online anymore. Instead, any and all things related to business now can be done via the web. You can buy cars, houses, clothing, digital and physical media. You can book plane trips, pay for a doctor’s visit, create a new cell phone plan. And it isn’t just about paying for things either. You can create and run a business solely through your computer. No longer are storefront, brick-and-mortar stores necessary for some.

Loads of businesses have found great success as online-only stores, servicing customers who live all over the globe. In fact, there are many massive companies that thrive by only being found on the web. Think of business behemoths such as Uber, DoorDash, Facebook - these are companies and corporations that truly could not exist before the internet. They are also businesses that, for all intents and purposes, do not exist outside the walls of your phone or computer. They have headquarters and services that exist in the real world, of course. But the heart and soul of these companies exist in a digital form.

The entire world has changed since it was all digitized, but nothing has changed more than the ways we spend and earn money. So, it is no surprise that money itself is becoming digitized. But what does that mean? How can we take something as traditional, physical, and important as currency and turn it into a digital item? Are there risks involved? Is it truly functional? Will it work?

These are the questions that were asked - and answered - by the Bitcoin and cryptocurrency revolution. Bitcoin was the first digital currency, existing solely on the internet without a physical, real-world counterpart you can hold in your hands. Bitcoin is unlike any other form of banking or money. It is not like when you use a debit or credit card to make purchases. It is not like walking into a bank. Bitcoin is completely different, revolutionary in ways that could have never been predicted before the invention and rapid growth of the internet.

But what is Bitcoin? What is it and how does it work? Where did it come from? How does it exist? How is it maintained? How is it kept safe? Most importantly, is it safe and what is next for Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency? These questions plague proponents of Bitcoin as it finds more and more popularity. As Bitcoin and other forms of cryptocurrency ballooned into a massively successful and valuable form of payment, it has raised many questions among those who don’t quite understand it. Questions remain, suspicions too. But those who support Bitcoin stand strongly behind it and are confident that it is the future of monetary systems that need a great shake-up.

There was a time when no one understood the internet nor knew of its true potential. Those who use Bitcoin think the same is true for cryptocurrency. They think that there will come a day when everyone who wants to can use Bitcoin for any sort of spending. They think that this paradigm shift will radically change the way we bank, earn, and spend. Are they right? Or are the critics correct? The battle rages on.

This book will tell you everything you need to know about Bitcoin and cryptocurrency. It will investigate and reveal its origins and its beginnings. It will tell you how Bitcoin started as an idea and transformed into something so much more. This book will teach you how the system works, why it works the way it does, and what lies ahead for it. This book will also tell you the dangers and controversies surrounding Bitcoin and why some people are so hesitant to jump on board. This book will examine all facets of Bitcoin and the digital revolution of cryptocurrency.

You will see that the journey and story of Bitcoin isn’t just about cryptocurrency. It isn’t just about a new way of spending, earning, and trading. No, Bitcoin is about so much more and speaks to larger issues, concerns, and hesitations that people have about the modern monetary system. Someone once called Bitcoin the largest form of peaceful protest and there is much truth to that. To understand Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency and why they attract so many people, you must comprehend why they exist and what drove their creators to make them.

The study of Bitcoin is the study of a world in great transition, from what once was to what will be. Like so many things that have ended up being massively monumental and successful, it started in the midst of a great crisis.

WHAT IS BITCOIN?

Before we get into the origin, history, success, and future of Bitcoin, we need to summarize what it is. As with many things related to the internet, a clear definition of Bitcoin can be a bit confusing especially if you do not have much experience with it. But to truly understand the importance of Bitcoin, you must truly understand what it is, how it is, and why it is.

So, what is Bitcoin? Essentially it is best to think of Bitcoin as a digital currency. When we say digital currency, we mean that Bitcoin is a form of payment and money that you can only invest in, trade, and use online. It is a form of payment that does not exist outside of the internet, you will not find paper or coin that is tied to Bitcoin. It is only online.

That is one of the biggest misconceptions about Bitcoin - there is no physical coin. Many people grasp the concept that Bitcoin is a digital currency and started on the internet. But many have a hard time understanding that you will never hold a Bitcoin in your hand. You will never walk into a store and put a Bitcoin on the counter to purchase goods. It simply does not work that way. Bitcoin is not seen, it is never printed on paper or exchanged like a typical dollar. It truly does only exist in the realms of the internet.

That is hard for many people to comprehend. If you cannot hold Bitcoin, if you cannot freely hand it to employees or vendors, does that mean it exists? How can something that you never see or hold have value? Scores of people have trouble with the concept of Bitcoin being a fully digital currency. Understandably, most people want money to be something they can hold in their hands and therefore protect. Bitcoin doesn’t have that feature and that frightens many.

Bitcoin also doesn’t use banks, which is another feature that scares many away. You do not take your digital Bitcoin to a bank and place it in a checking or savings account. Instead, your Bitcoin investment exists online among millions of other coins. That makes many think that there is no way that Bitcoin is secure. In reality, it is ultimately more secure than traditional currency. In fact, the chance of someone stealing your Bitcoin is slim. It is so slim in fact that some people feel it is impossible.

Bitcoin is operated by a decentralized authority. That means that no major bank overlooks how Bitcoin is run. It is run by the people who own it, the system of checks and balances is operated solely by those who invest and own Bitcoin. A decentralized system also means that no governmental body has control over Bitcoin, which is one of its most attractive - and frightening - features for others.

When you put your money into a bank account, you are given insurance that nothing bad will happen to it. The work of a bank is regulated and overseen by the federal government, who have instituted a series of insurance policies and actions to make sure that citizens can trust and use banks without them losing a fortune. It wasn’t always that way. In fact, there was a time when banks were much less regulated and if they went out of business, your money could disappear entirely without any chance of it returning.

Nowadays, banks are monitored and insured by the government, making sure that your investments and savings are safe. However, that can only be achieved because of the vast power of the federal government. Without that sort of interaction and supervision, nothing is guaranteed. Since Bitcoin is not watched over and protected by the government, technically there is a chance that your Bitcoin share could be stolen or lost. Though, as stated before, the possibility of that is smaller than small. Still, the fact that it could happen is reason enough to keep many away from investing in Bitcoin.

But without the supervision of the government also comes great advantages too. Bitcoin is not held to the same standards that typical, traditional currency is. It is protected in its own, revolutionary ways. It is also easier to trade, easier to make, easier to invest. It also has lower transaction fees and isn’t tied to the restrictions associated with modern banking systems.

Additionally, doing away with the federal government and banking institutions has moved Bitcoin further and further away from institutions that many people have grown uncomfortable with. There are scores of people who have felt burned, lied to, and hurt by the banking systems of the last few decades. This system led to much financial pain and loss, especially during the Great Recession crisis of the late 2000s. When people saw these giant banking corporations breaking rules and rewarding themselves before receiving massive bailouts from the federal government, they decided that enough was enough. Since then, there have been millions of people looking to get away from that system. Bitcoin and cryptocurrency have given them an opportunity to do just that.

While Bitcoin and cryptocurrency have created new ways to earn, save, spend, and invest, there are certain aspects about them that feel at home in the modern monetary systems they broke off from. For instance, investing in Bitcoin and other cryptos is a lot like investing in stocks on the stock market. In fact, many people have become attracted to investing in crypto for that very purpose alone. The cryptocurrency market looks and feels a lot like the stock market. People have put money into certain cryptos, like they do certain stocks, and have watched their fortunes rise, fall, then rise again. This has created a wild, exciting way for people to make money.

Bitcoin is a way for people to break off from the system they are tired of, and it is also a way for people to make some serious money. Like the American Dollar or any other form of currency, the value of Bitcoin and its peer cryptocurrencies has gone up, down, and up again a lot since its inception. Purchasing a share of Bitcoin at the right time has made many people very, very wealthy.

It is wild to think that a form of digital currency could change the way we purchase and sell things, but it has. People use Bitcoin to buy many different products and services. Some major online retailers have gotten in on the act, allowing Bitcoin to be used as a method of payment. While other people have traded coins among themselves, creating a market that can be examined, studied, and used to one’s benefit.

Bitcoin is a digital currency you can only find online, one that doesn’t need banks or the government. But that doesn’t mean it isn’t wildly successful with great amounts of potential. Already in just a little over a decade, it has become something much, much larger than it once was. Its value has skyrocketed and people who invest in Bitcoin stand a chance to make a small - or large - fortune.

In simple terms, Bitcoin is a form of currency that is unlike the average dollar but very similar to it at the same time. It is different because it doesn’t rely on banks or governmental oversight. It plays by its own rules and is supervised by a large and growing group of people who believe in it. It is similar because it can be traded and spent to accrue products and services. For all its brand-new features and its revolutionary effect on investing, buying, and spending, it still functions a lot like regular money.

To answer the biggest question that people ask: yes, Bitcoin has value. In fact, it has a lot of value. You may never hold it, you may never put it into a bank account, but it definitely has a worth that rivals and sometimes beats the current American dollar.

IS BITCOIN REALLY SAFE?

The Bitcoin system is safe, despite what some critics might say. In fact, most of those opposed to the idea of Bitcoin just do not truly understand how it works and how certain parts of the system keep it all under the watchful eyes that are most benefited by its safety.

We will later discuss the intricacies of how the Bitcoin system works but one thing you should know is that it is run off a collection of computers, much like the very one you have at home. These computers are all over the world, furiously working away to keep things in line and in order and ultimately protected and safe from any thieves or criminals.

These computers, owned by people who believe in and invest in Bitcoin, are running the code that stores all Bitcoins. This massive, growing, impossibly intricate code is referred to as the blockchain. Think of it as a wildly complicated and complex code that is millions upon millions of characters long. Every transaction and move related to Bitcoin is stored in this code, every single one. But all the computers in this blockchain system store the code, down to every single letter and number.

It is important to note that the very people who need Bitcoin to function properly and safely are the ones looking after it. The computers mentioned are owned by Bitcoin investors and traders, the true believers who are heavily invested in the process. If the system goes awry under their watch, they are the ones who will lose their profits. That gives them even more reason to take their jobs as watchdogs seriously. As well, the way the system is created makes it nearly impossible to be hacked or compromised.

Think of the blockchain as a massive code needed to unlock a safe. If a burglar wants to break into the code, they need the code exactly as it is, with not one letter or number changed. However, to get this code they would have to visit hundreds of thousands of computers all over the world to get the code, piece it together and not make a single mistake. This is not an impossible task, it is technically something that could happen. But the likelihood of it happening is next to zero.

There is much more to know about Bitcoin, its system, and its safety but the main thing you should remember is that it is safe. In fact, in many ways, it is much safer than the way you bank now.

IN CONCLUSION

There is much to learn and know about Bitcoin. There is much to understand to properly get a solid handle on it. You need to know the history of Bitcoin and the reasons it was made. You need to understand who sees value in Bitcoin and why they have championed it for so long. You also need to know who stands in its way and why.

But you also need to understand the ins and outs of the system that allows Bitcoin to be possible. You need to see and truly comprehend the more technical aspects of Bitcoin. It is a brand-new way to make and spend money and it is complicated, different, truly unique. To understand Bitcoin as more than just a concept, you must really dive into the specifics of it and see how it works.

Bitcoin is just over ten years old, but it has already left a strong footprint on the world of monetary systems. It has already shaken things up and changed the way people do banking. How did it start? How did we get here? Most importantly, where do things go next? What lies ahead for Bitcoin and its potential?

1

THE ORIGINS AND GOALS OF BITCOIN

The world was in a very different, frightening place when Bitcoin was first created in 2008. It is hard to truly express how dangerous and volatile that time was. Unless you were there, you cannot fully grasp just how truly uncertain, unpredictable, and scary that period was for Americans and citizens all over the world.

However, it is from this truly chaotic and shocking time in global history that Bitcoin was founded. From the ashes of a severe recession came the first fully realized cryptocurrency that did not need to rely on banks, financial institutions, or even governmental oversight. It is from that crisis that Bitcoin was really born.

The financial breakdown of 2007 and 2008 didn’t create the idea of cryptocurrency. In fact, the basis for crypto has existed for decades. It was only an idea then, though. It was the thought that maybe there was another way to do business than the status quo. It was the thought that perhaps banks and other financial institutions could be avoided for something that is more democratic and self-reliant. The thought and theory had existed for a long time. But it wasn’t until the current financial system began to break apart in 2008 that the wheels began turning and Bitcoin became an actual reality.

THE FINANCIAL CRISIS OF 2008

The American and global economy was hitting new highs during the 1990s. In fact, things had never been better for many. Following a powerful recession in the late 1980s and early 1990s, the economy had turned around and things were looking bright and promising.

As the 1900s wore on, the financial prospects for many were only getting stronger and stronger. There was a lot leading to this: global trade was proving to be strong, banks had freed themselves from many financial regulations that limited their ability to lend to people, the internet was beginning to blossom into something powerful and new.

There was a time when the World Wide Web seemed to be growing and nearly multiplying in size day after day. Websites that people had never heard of were appearing and finding great success seemingly overnight. Some of the most popular sites of all time, such as Amazon and Google, were launched in those early days. Many companies were finding great fortunes thanks to the internet and things were continually looking better and better. That would end eventually, though. The dot com bubble, as it was called, would burst after a few years and many of the sites that seemed like complete slam dunks, would close their digital doors forever. Still, the economy stabilized and soldiered forward.

There was other great success being found among homeowners and folks looking for loans. For years, it was incredibly hard to get a loan from a bank. You had to have amazing credit with very little financial issues in your past. That meant that millions of Americans were out of luck when they were looking to buy a house. A bank would look at their history, decide they couldn’t offer them the capital needed, and send them away.

But things had changed and because of Washington, DC things had been made much, much easier for lenders and those looking for money from their banks.

Regulation policies had made it much simpler for banks to offer loans. In fact, many banks had nearly complete freedom when it came to who they lent money to and how much they gave them. Suddenly, in the early 2000s, it seemed that anyone who wanted a home was able to find one. Banks were making loan agreements with many people who couldn’t afford them before. People with less than stellar credit were now being given deals and amounts to pay back to their banks. People who were making less than $30,000 a year were now buying two-bedroom homes all over the country.

Was this healthy? Was this right? Was this smart? No, it wasn’t. However, the banks were making money off making money. At the same time, they had created new ways to profit off of mortgages and those who owed them. In fact, they were making money off of peoples’ debts. They were offering mortgages to people and then, when those people were missing payments, the banks were bundling those debts and selling them to other banks. Banks were essentially purchasing debts.

It was an awfully complicated system that essentially was making tons of money for banks and lending firms, even off the misfortune of others. The more misfortune that fell on new homeowners, the more money banks made. However, the system was about to collapse upon its own weight.

Those homeowners who couldn’t afford their mortgages and should have never been offered them were starting to miss their payments again and again and again. This was not just a few people who were owing money, too. There were millions upon millions of people who weren’t making their payments and were owing what would end up being trillions of dollars.

The banks, who were selling these debts to all sorts of financial institutions, started losing the money they were counting on. There were just too many people who couldn’t afford their payments and not enough capital coming in based on mortgages.

As for the other banks and institutions that were making money off the debts, they too were beginning to suffer. They had agreed to buy debt from lenders, under the assumption that the debt would eventually somehow get paid one way or another. But those in debt certainly weren’t paying and the banks that made the faulty loans weren’t paying so soon banks on top of banks on top of banks were losing more money than they could handle.

What had been created was a spider’s web of interconnected financial companies that were all buying and selling to one another, mostly customer’s debts. However, this grand fabric that tied them all together was collapsing, and it risked taking them all down with it.

This is what happened. In a grand succession of moments, the housing bubble burst in a major way. People stopped buying houses, all because of fear from what they were hearing about banks and the predatory loans that had been offered for over a decade now. At the same time, the banks and mortgage companies that were handing out loans left and right started going broke, in major ways. Some of the biggest names in Wall Street were tied up in the mess, having spent years buying and selling and trading debt as if it was a normal commodity. Multiple major companies that had existed and thrived on Wall Street for ages were suffering and they weren’t alone. As mentioned, everyone was connected. Banks, brokerages, mortgage lenders, the list goes on. And in what seemed like an instant, everyone started to face imminent demise.

This led to a major freeze on lending practices, which hurt businesses all over the globe. That led to businesses cutting back and cutting hours and laying people off. Companies couldn’t get their hands on money because banks had none to lend. Employees were being fired as some major corporations faced a recession unlike any the world had ever seen.

There was a time in the fall of 2008 when it seemed that every day brought news of another major retail chain, bank, or business failing. Some major brands that had existed for decades were going under, closing their doors forever and sending their employees home without much notice. For the first time in decades, unemployment was skyrocketing to levels that were once unheard of. Families were being kicked out of their homes at alarming rates, small villages of tents were popping up in numerous American cities. Those in power seemed afraid, unsure of what would happen next.

This all happened in the midst of an American presidential election. The two candidates, Senator John McCain of Arizona and Senator Barack Obama of Illinois were grappling with this new reality. The sitting president, George W. Bush, made it clear that whoever won in November 2008 would be faced with a crisis unlike any before it. In the end, the steadfast calm and resolve of Senator Obama would prove victorious as he was elected by a wide margin. He was sworn in under the darkest clouds any president had faced in ages. Uncertainty reigned and the president would later say there were times he was sure that the economy would crater even more, and the entire world would face shortages and troubles it hadn’t seen since the 1930s.

Despite a major change in Washington, many more massive institutions failed and went bankrupt as the recession dragged on. Due to the negligence and shifty moves made by CEOs and power makers at the top, these giant companies were facing a sudden demise. However, if they went under, they would take countless other businesses with them. The spider web was collapsing in a huge way and the United States government had a choice: let them all fall and destroy the global economy, or keep these companies propped up through the use of American tax dollars.

The government chose the latter.

When the government bailed out multiple major banks and lending firms and Wall Street icons, many people were furious. Though most Americans understood that if something drastic wasn’t done the world would have faced a financial dark age, they still were unhappy with the choice of Congress and the president. Approval ratings sank and many people felt sold out. Why were these massive companies who were breaking the rules and getting away with gluttonous, greedy things getting paid handsomely while those trying to make ends meet were losing their jobs because of something they didn’t do? Why were they being punished when other people did the crime?

Sure enough, no one was truly held accountable. Through a lot of hard work and after millions of jobs were lost and countless businesses closed, the economy finally recovered. And in the end, no one from Wall Street or the major banks was charged with a crime. Congress called people before them to testify, some lost their cushy jobs and massive paychecks, but no one went to prison and not much was changed. The culture of corruption that had led to the biggest recession and economic downturn since the 1930s was seemingly alive and well.

Throughout the chaos of the Great Recession, questions started to get raised. Many of them were concerned about the system that had led to that point. Why were those who had created the system able to profit off it? Why were citizens so reliant on the powers that be? How could it be that people could make money off making money? Why were millionaires profiting off the pain of people just trying to buy a home? Why wasn’t anyone looking out for the little guy, the small family that was promised a mortgage from a bank that knew they shouldn’t offer it? Things had run amok, and the system had collapsed, and the American citizen had suffered the most. They had been taken for a ride, subjected to the desires of criminals. However, the criminals were the ones running the banks and Wall Street.

People were angry. People were mad, not just at politicians in Washington but the entire system. Many saw corruption like they hadn’t seen before. Many saw crimes that were going unpunished. They didn’t know what to do but they did know one thing: they wanted out. They wanted no part in a financial and monetary system that was going to reward those who deserved it least.

But how would they break away? With a system such as the American and global financial one, it seemed impossible to branch off and use an alternative. It felt like the powers that held all the cards had them stacked against anyone looking to try something different. It just felt impossible to forge a new path. Using a monetary system different than the one that everyone else relied on felt like the equivalent of using a system other than the DMV to get a driver’s license. It hadn’t been done before and no one knew if or how it was possible.

There had been talk of an alternative to traditional currency and the system that used it. In certain circles of the internet, people had spoken and written about an all-digital form of money that would only be used online. It wouldn’t be printed, it couldn’t be held. It would break free from the powerful, restrictive, and corrupt system that had caused so many problems in the past. If someone were able to figure out how to create and implement a system like this, they could introduce millions to a whole new way to make, save, spend, and buy.

But how could that be done? How could anyone create a system of an all-digital currency? More importantly, how could anyone monitor it and make sure it wasn’t taken advantage of and used in all the wrong ways. No one was under the impression that only the rich and powerful on Wall Street were the ones who could grow corrupt. Great power and wealth create a desire for more and it often leads to people breaking the rules, breaking the law, and doing it all to advance their own means.

It was plain that an all-digital currency would be worthwhile if it could be practical. Yet, despite all the talk of this online currency, no one knew how to create it and make it work. All the options that presented themselves relied upon old institutions that many were trying to move away from such as banks and the government. Those who proposed a digital currency didn’t want anything to do with those powers, that was why they created this idea in the first place.

Then, in 2008, everything changed when one man - or a group of people - proposed what would become Bitcoin. With just some simple keystrokes and a few new ideas, fantasy became reality.

SATOSHI NAKAMOTO AND THE BIRTH OF BITCOIN

Although the concept of a digital currency had been toyed with before by many different people online, the major ideas and solid concepts that would eventually become Bitcoin and truly the entire cryptocurrency world were first introduced to the world on October 31, 2008. They were submitted by a person or a group of people going by the name Satoshi Nakamoto. You may be confused by the idea that Nakamoto was a person or group of people. How does that work, you may ask? The truth is no one knows. Since Nakamoto would never reveal his or her or their identity, the name remains shrouded in true mystery.

In fact, to this day no one knows who Satoshi Nakamoto is. Due to the anonymous nature of the internet, the true identity of this person or group of people who started Bitcoin may never be truly known. It is wild to assume that someone who has had such a strong impact on the world of finance and the internet may never truly be known but that is a perfect way to summarize the advantages and truly unique position the World Wide Web has. Someone can ultimately change the world and the way it operates and forever remain a question mark.

Many assume that Satoshi Nakamoto was a pseudonym used by a group of individuals who shared the single, strong idea to overhaul the banking system with the working concept of cryptocurrency. When all is said and done, it doesn’t really matter who Nakamoto was because, like most things related to Bitcoin, it isn’t about a single person but rather a community of people coming together to create something larger than themselves. That is what Nakamoto spoke for, that is what he or she represented. To this day, that name stands for the ideas behind cryptocurrency, Bitcoin, and breaking away from a corrupt system.

Whoever he or she or they were, Nakamoto started a movement with just the click of his mouse and the use of his keyboard. He created a post on a cryptography mailing list called “Bitcoin P2P e-cash paper.” From there, a link took readers to a paper called “Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System.” That paper laid out the plans and the functionality of Bitcoin. However, unlike many other online debates that had come before it, Nakamoto’s paper was written with solid concepts and hard facts. It wasn’t just some out-of-this-world idea, it was showing ways that cryptocurrency could be implanted, monitored, and run safely and effectively. This was like the creation of the wheel or the discovery of fire. It was taking an idea that had long existed but seemed just out of reach and was bringing it down to Earth.

“Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System” laid out the idea behind a digital currency that did not rely on banks or regular financial systems, just like the fans of crypto had always wanted. Nakamoto’s plan was based on a series of “coins” that could be sent from user to user across the internet, without using a single administrator to keep tabs on them. No banks, no governments, no financial powerhouses keeping tabs on the system. The groundbreaking paper detailed how these new forms of cryptocurrency would work and referred to this grand new idea as “a system for electronic transactions without relying on trust.”

WHAT IS BLOCKCHAIN AND WHY IS IT SO SAFE?

Where the paper really set itself apart from other proposed cryptocurrency ideas from the past was its creation of blockchain. To make this radical, all-digital system work, the system would need some sort of all-digital ledger to keep things in line. Without banks doing the paperwork, there stood a chance that people could game the system or otherwise not keep track of who was sending who money and how much that money was worth. It had been done before with traditional currency, why wouldn’t it happen with Bitcoin if it wasn’t kept in check?

That led to the inclusion of blockchain. What is blockchain? In the simplest terms possible, blockchain is a system of recording information related to cryptocurrency. Like a hard-bound paper book you would find in a bank, blockchain is an online ledger that is used across an entire network of computers that participate in the system. It is not owned or run by one group or person. Rather, blockchain runs itself and is a constantly updating history of all transactions. It is impossible to be tampered with and is a safe, secure, and anonymous way for the world of cryptocurrency to keep moving. Everyone who participates owns blockchain, its ownership is spread all over the world and that makes it safer than one centralized history of all the Bitcoin transactions.

Blockchain was almost as revolutionary as bitcoin and cryptocurrency itself. It was a new technology, a way to tie transactions together and turn them into a series of blocks. The way that every transaction is tied together (hence the word “chain”) means that if you change one transaction, you change them all. That means you cannot modify any block without altering the rest of the chain. This is bad news for anyone trying to hack or tweak the system for their advantage. Essentially all bitcoin users are expanding upon an incredibly long line of code every time they make a transaction. Every piece of the chain, known as hashes, relies on one another, they are all linked in ways that cannot be pulled apart. They work together in a way that makes them more secure and impossible to be tweaked after the fact. It is one long chain and simply cannot exist without another. Each piece of the chain is shared, every computer that has the blockchain code has all of it.

As stated, the blockchain is not stored in one central location, like a vault in a bank. Instead, it is distributed among all the users of the system. Everyone has a copy of this blockchain and spread and refresh it every time a new transaction is added to the network.

Blockchain is a reliable and safe way of recording any transactions. It does away with the need for external authority, that is one of the reasons why so many people were attracted to the idea. Bitcoin users did not need to report to a third party every time they made a transaction. Instead, they relied upon the ever-evolving blockchain and other bitcoin users to keep everything robust, healthy, and safe.

With just a few exceptions, this system worked like a charm. This was what people had been looking for when they first mentioned the concept of cryptocurrency. Blockchain was the missing piece that really tied everything together. There was now a plan that could be followed and a real cryptocurrency that had a blueprint for success. Blockchain did away with the worries that cryptocurrency would be unregulated and completely unsafe. Instead, it was now safer than traditional money. If implanted, this plan would rely upon the people who were going to use Bitcoin. They would be the enforcers, the vanguards, the safety that kept the entire system honest and strong.

Nakamoto’s ideas of Bitcoin and especially the inclusion of blockchain made many people all over the globe excited. Finally, written in precise detail and realistic terms, was the idea that so many people had rallied behind for years: the idea of a digitized currency that was for the people and by the people and did not rely on banks. The safety installed by blockchain made the premise even more tantalizing for so many. Proponents of digital currency would finally have a strong, tested concept that could stand on its own. They took to the critics and showed them that not only was cryptocurrency safe, but it was also possible.

And it was coming.

On January 3, 2009, the bitcoin network officially started when Nakamoto created, or “mined”, the first block of bitcoin, titled block number 0. Attached to the block was the text “The Times Jan/03/2009 Chancellor on brink of second bailout for banks.” This comment was an obvious reference to the global financial disaster that had led so many to want digital currency in the first place. It was just another reminder that Bitcoin and all cryptocurrencies were a response to a system that many felt had failed the people.

Popular programmer Hal Finley was the first person to receive any bitcoins. He downloaded the bitcoin software on the very day it was available and received 10 bitcoins from Nakamoto. That transaction, which occurred on January 12, 2009, was the first Bitcoin transaction ever. The value of these first bitcoin transactions were decided and negotiated by the people using the bitcoin forums. Again, that was proving that Bitcoin was and would always be a form of currency for the people and by the people. Even from its start, Bitcoin had a strong emphasis on being a democratic and decentralized form of payment that would always put the people first.

Bitcoin had arrived. Though its origin remained in a shadow of mystery with many not sure who truly started it, it was finally in the world. Now the true tests were laid before Bitcoin. Would it be adopted by the many who championed it before its creation? Would it be picked up by the mainstream? Would it even break through the noise of the late 2000s and have any sort of impact at all?

Bitcoin was born but its future was uncertain from the very start. In time, it would be proven again and again that its future would remain uncertain often. Where would it go next? The future would be dictated by the same power the past had been: the people who cared most about Bitcoin, on both sides of the argument. The fight for and against Bitcoin had just begun.

IN CONCLUSION

By the beginning of 2009, Bitcoin had transformed from concept to reality. It was finally in the real world after much deliberation and discussion. As you will see, the beginning stages of Bitcoin were rocky indeed. In fact, all Bitcoin’s existence has had its fair share of rocky moments. But the creation of Bitcoin was a big deal, a huge deal in fact. It was a major event for reasons beyond the obvious.

In reality, the creation and implementation of Bitcoin was a major form of protest that capped off a decade of serious change and turmoil. Though many people will see Bitcoin was a way of making money, a digital currency that stood to make a lot of people a lot of money, it was way more than that.

Bitcoin was always about so much more than money and value, even before it was fully implemented after creation. Bitcoin was already about shaking up the system and doing away with the constraints and unchecked greed that had led to multiple financial fiascos like the massive crisis that changed the world in 2008. Did people make a lot of money from Bitcoin? Yes. Did it generate a brand-new industry that has created millions of dollars for individuals and generated even more for businesses and online enterprises? Yes. But was it about the money? No, it was about changing things.

The creation of Bitcoin occurred in a very tumultuous and troubling time. Its origin cannot be ignored, and it cannot be assumed that Bitcoin was spawned in a vacuum. The events that were rocking the entire world were what caused the ultimate creation of - and interest in - Bitcoin. True experts of cryptocurrency would be mistaken to ever forget that Bitcoin was created as a response. It was the response to a system that had left millions behind, it was the response to banks and other major institutions ignoring the rules that others had to follow and breaking them to manage their own wealth. Bitcoin was a response to a government that chose to bail out troublemakers who caused such a mess while millions of hard-working citizens were left in the cold.

Bitcoin is about money, there is no doubt about that. It is a type of money after all. But the concept and ideas that drive Bitcoin are about something much larger than money. Even in the beginning that was evident. In the years ahead, it would remain an obvious facet of Bitcoin. It would be one of the aspects of cryptocurrency that would challenge critics the most and pose the biggest danger to the powers that be. Bitcoin was always a rebel concept and that would forever create enemies and champions for it.

2

HOW BITCOIN WORKS

Before we can carry on with more of the history of Bitcoin and the ways it changed the world and the way people make money, save money, invest, and trade, we need to really get into the basic tenements behind Bitcoin. In other words, we need to truly discuss how Bitcoin works. If you can understand the basic premises behind the functionality of Bitcoin, you can turn into a successful trader and investor. No one becomes a master of something without truly understanding it.

As has been mentioned multiple times, Bitcoin is a completely digital currency. It does not exist on paper, it does not exist in coin. You will never hold a Bitcoin in your hand. You will never deposit one into an ATM. Bitcoin is run completely online. Are there Bitcoin ATMs? Yes, there are, and we will get into that more later. But even those are corresponding, communicating, and collaborating with a system that is run only on the internet. Bitcoin is a digital form of money that lives solely online.

So, how exactly does that work?

WHERE DOES BITCOIN GET ITS VALUE?

One of the things that confuse people the most about Bitcoin is its worth. Where does it come from? How much is it worth? Is it worth anything? Is it worth the same amount of a dollar? More than a dollar? People are used to the American dollar and other currencies and that leads them to believe that no other currencies can have any sort of value.

But Bitcoin does have value. In fact, it has a lot of value. In late March 2021, a single Bitcoin was worth approximately $54,000. That’s right, just one Bitcoin was worth that much money. Now you may understand why so many people are drawn to Bitcoin; earning just one is worth more than some people make in an entire year.

Before we go on, you need to be reminded that not everyone buys a whole Bitcoin. In fact, most people just buy into the system by earning or buying a portion of a Bitcoin. Most people cannot spend the money to purchase an entire coin. But even if you got just a small percentage of one, that is a lot of worth right there. Most people who invest in Bitcoin only buy a small part of one whole coin. Only the wealthiest and those who spend their time crypto mining receive entire Bitcoins. Everyone else gets just a small cut of a coin, but that is more than enough for most people.

So, yes, Bitcoin does have value. More value than what most people would assume. But where does it derive that value from?

HOW DOES ANY MONEY GET ITS VALUE?

Before we discuss that, it is important that we determine where any form of currency derives its value. There are just certain things that are universal and one of them is the idea that money is going to be accepted because it has a value that is decided and agreed upon by everyone who uses it. If you walk into a store with a dollar, or yen, or peso, you know that it will be accepted. When you are shopping and you see that something costs fourteen dollars, you know exactly how to pay. Societies all over the globe have established forms of currency and rely on these currencies to remain consistent and with a stable value.

There was a time before dollars and coins when citizens would trade and sell their items and give one another beans, cocoa, gold, and other such forms of payment. If you wanted a cow, you would pay your neighbor five or six cocoa beans. Eventually, money would take the place of these items.

What currency typically needs to stand the test of time is something called store of value. Store of value is essentially a currency or commodity that can be exchanged and given away at any point without losing value. Basically, these items should be worth the same today as they will be in four, five, ten, or a hundred years.

There are not many items that have that sort of long-lasting value. Most currencies change in value over time, but slowly and relatively. That is why when you look back in the past, people were able to make extravagant purchases for a couple hundred dollars. Today, a purchase of that size would cost several thousand. That is because the value of the dollar has changed over time but in a measure and healthy way. However, certain items like some metals and gold never change. Their shelf lives are perpetual. That is why some people prefer gold and try to hold onto great amounts of it because it will still be worth a lot tomorrow, the next day, and long into the future.

America didn’t disagree with that opinion about gold. In fact, for a long-time gold was America’s standard. America was on a gold standard for generations, meaning that the dollar was redeemable for a specific weight in gold. That was changed in 1971 when President Richard Nixon gave the Federal Reserve more power to influence the rate of employment and inflation. The dollar is no longer tied to a commodity of any other.

Still, the dollar, the yen, the Swiss franc, and other currencies around the world are considered stores of value almost all the time because they have proven themselves to be ultra-reliable. There are only a few rare instances when these currencies cannot be counted upon. Thanks to major steps by the government, banks, and, frankly, everyday citizens, the American dollar and other currencies have remained steadfast and strong throughout the years. They can be relied upon; people know that they will not walk into the store tomorrow and be denied service because the dollar has suddenly become obsolete.

To be a successful currency, there must be certain qualifications met to withstand the test of time. These qualities are what determine the strength and lasting power of the Yen, Dollar, and all successful forms of currency.

One of the features that is needed is divisibility. That means that the currency can be divided into smaller incremental units. This is essential otherwise all products, big or small, would be worth the same thing. A strong currency will have the option to be split into smaller units and given away as such.

Another feature is utility. This means that citizens can rely on the currency for goods and services. You need to know that your currency will be accepted for goods and services all over. If there isn’t uniform utility that can be relied upon and trusted, people will not use it.

Durability is another asset that is essential for a successful currency. No one is going to use a form of money that is always falling apart or fades, wears, and tears. Governments put great energy into making sure their money holds up over time and will not deteriorate quickly.

It is also important that any form of currency looking to stand the test of time is not easily faked. When a currency is counterfeit, it loses value and people will quickly lose trust in it. The system can be flooded with authentic-looking fakes and the value of the currency will plummet. That is why you see so many governments around the globe going to great lengths to make sure their money has anti-counterfeit features installed. No one wants a slew of fake money filling the market.

Finally, the biggest piece of the puzzle to a successful currency: scarcity. Any currency needs to find the right balance, not too much and not too little. If there is too large a money supply in the nation, the prices of goods could spike upward and create an economic collapse. If the amount is too small, other problems could arise. You will see governments closely monitor the amount of money in the market and generate how much they are printing based on that. It is a careful science, an accurate way to control inflation and deflation and make sure scarcity exists in the exact right amount.

As you can see, Bitcoin hits some of the marks listed above perfectly. It is impossible to make a counterfeit Bitcoin, for one. It is also impossible for Bitcoin to wear out and fall apart because it just doesn’t have a physical component so it cannot ever deteriorate.

One of its most winning features is that Bitcoin is wildly and easily divisible. When you are spending an American dollar, for example, you can break it down into up to one hundred units. You could technically spend one single penny on an item, which is 1/100 of a dollar. That is as low as an American dollar goes. However, Bitcoin is far more divisible than that. You could offer someone 1/100,000,000 of a Bitcoin if you wanted and that would have legitimate value. In fact, 1/100,000,000 of a Bitcoin is a form of measurement called one “Satoshi.” That is a benefit that Bitcoin has over other currencies, it can be broken down into incredibly small - yet still manageable and profitable - units of measurement.

As things stand now, governments give their currencies value. They closely monitor their money to make sure its value remains reliable and strong. That is why a dollar today will be worth a dollar tomorrow and you won’t see much difference in the worth of that dollar until years from now, as things gradually change. The government is in control and goes to great lengths to make sure the dollar remains valuable. However, as we noted, the Bitcoin community has moved away from the traditional system, most especially anything having to do with any governmental bodies. So, no grand power like that is overseeing the value of Bitcoin.

Bitcoin’s worth and value has been driven by speculative interest. Like the stocks on Wall Street, Bitcoin has received its value from people buying in or selling their shares. That is what generates the value and worth of Bitcoin. To some, that is an attractive feature. They know that the future of Bitcoin lives and dies on how much people are interested in it. If there are more people fascinated by Bitcoin and want a part of it, they will buy a portion of Bitcoin. When that happens, the price of Bitcoin goes up. That is good news for anyone who is in the market. However, if people shy away from Bitcoin or get cold feet and sell their share, the price will plummet. Bitcoin’s worth lives and dies on the interest of buyers.

WHAT DETERMINES THE PRICE OF BITCOIN?

There are multiple factors that dictate the price and value of Bitcoin, not just interest from consumers. In fact, even though it does not always rely on the same attributes that traditional currencies do, Bitcoin has a whole series of things that determine its worth.

Firstly, the supply of Bitcoin and the market’s demand for it greatly determines how much Bitcoin is worth. We will get into the process of crypto mining later but what needs to be known is that computers and their owners are furiously working away at all hours of the day to “mine” new Bitcoin blocks. When this process is completed, a new block is made. That essentially is adding more of a supply of Bitcoin into the world. As more blocks are created, the value will fluctuate slightly. That is typical supply and demand. If the market is currently flooded with people desperate to buy Bitcoin, then this new block will be born into a high value because people will be willing to spend anything to buy it. If the demand is low, this new block’s price will be lower because the demand isn’t there.

The value of Bitcoin is heavily influenced by just how much people want it, which is unlike other currencies where the value stays remarkably steadfast in most situations. This makes Bitcoin attractive to someone who loves the thrill of being a part of a currency that is also a good. That scares others away, though. How can you really rely on a currency that has a value tied to the common person and their current interest or disinterest in it? That does not lend itself to much reliability.

Supply and demand for Bitcoin is influenced by the competition facing Bitcoin. We will later address the number of alternatives to Bitcoin, other cryptocurrency coins that are on the market and able to be bought, sold, and traded by investors. What you must know is that there are alternatives and, technically, buyers could invest in those instead of Bitcoin. Obviously, if Bitcoin loses its popularity when compared to other coins, its value will go down. However, Bitcoin has always been far-and-away the most popular form of cryptocurrency and there has yet to be an alternative to even come close to taking it down.

A crowded field of other cryptocurrency choices is good news for Bitcoin. The more competition there is in the field, the more those competing coins split the difference in terms of popularity, allowing an avenue for Bitcoin to remain the most popular and the biggest dog in the pack. Of course, a crowded field is also good for investors too, allowing them multiple ways to invest their money and gain money. This drives interest to the cryptocurrency market as a whole and helps all coins, including Bitcoin.

As mentioned, Bitcoins are mined by computers all over the globe. These computers are working day and night to crack a code that will mine and create a new Bitcoin. This coin has great value and the more of them released in the market, the more or less value Bitcoin as a whole has. The creation of a new coin does have an impact on the value of Bitcoin. Unlike other currencies, the price of creating that Bitcoin has an impact on the overall value too.

You see, it takes a lot of energy to power the machines that will mine a Bitcoin. It cannot be done on a typical laptop or computer like you have at home. Most people who mine Bitcoin are doing so on intense, custom-made computing rigs that are devoted solely to mining. These computers are working all the time and they are also using a lot of energy. In fact, people who mine have noted incredibly high power bills. It can cost thousands of dollars a month to successfully crypto mine.

The amount of money it costs to mine has an impact on how many people are mining. There will be less people doing it if it costs too much. Therefore, the price of energy in the country is a determining factor in the value of Bitcoin. Look at it this way: if the price of energy is high, the price of mining will be high too. That means less people will mine, which means creating new coins will take longer. A slowdown in creating new coins means the value of all coins will be impacted. So, there is a direct correlation between the price of energy and the value of a Bitcoin. That is certainly not something that traditional currencies ever have to worry about.

Another major factor that determines the value of Bitcoin is the government. Even though Bitcoin has long been against government regulation and does not rely on any governing body for assistance, it is still influenced by the rule of law and politicians all over the world.

As of now, most counties have not weighed in on Bitcoin. America, for example, has just said that it is legal, and people are free to do what they want with it. Other countries, such as Egypt or India, have made threatening overtures to the Bitcoin community, suggesting that it cannot be trusted and should be banned or outlawed. Some nations have in fact banned Bitcoin because the government suspects that it could rival their own currencies or because they claim it is rampant with corruption.

As things stand now, many countries are at a crossroads when it comes to Bitcoin. Do they allow it to function on its own, without any government oversight and well outside of any control but its own? Or do they put their foot down and attempt to regulate? That would be within their right. There could come a day when the government could say that Bitcoin is too risky for investors and its market is too wild and unpredictable and it needs to be regulated to keep people safe. If that happens, it would greatly impact Bitcoin.

Whenever there is talk about something related to government regulation or any sort of government involvement, this affects the value of Bitcoin. Why? Because it affects people’s confidence and trust in Bitcoin. It either scares or excites people and both of those things will impact how much people buy or sell their coins. If they suspect something is right around the bend, some sort of governmental overreach, they will react in some sort of way. Maybe they sell their Bitcoin, maybe they try to buy more before it possibly becomes harder. These sort of outside influences directly impact the feelings people have about owning Bitcoin and that of course drives the price up or down.

Bitcoins are being mined constantly but there will come a day when all the coins are mined. That may seem confusing because, as has been stressed before, there are no physical coins, there is no physical mining going on in some caves all over the world. That is true, however, it was long ago decided by the founders of Bitcoin that there would be a solid, unchangeable number of Bitcoins that would be mined. No more, no less. That number happens to be 21 million coins. Once that amount is reached, no more mining will happen, and no more new coins will be created. As of December 2020, there had been over 18 million coins mined. That is about 89% of all Bitcoins. Don’t worry though, the final Bitcoin isn’t predicted to be mined until 2140. But when that happens, the speed of mining will no longer impact the value of Bitcoin. Instead, prices of Bitcoin will be determined by other matters, such as the popularity of Bitcoin in its respective markets.

Some people say that the value of Bitcoin is going up and up because we are getting closer to that 2140 date when all coins will be mined. Obviously, that is still a long way away but when people see that more and more coins are being mined and supply is getting smaller, demand then goes up. When demand goes up, prices go up. Some people feel that the limiting number of coins waiting to be mined is having an impact on the desire of people to buy, buy, buy.

IN CONCLUSION

As you can see, the value of Bitcoin is very, very real. In fact, as you will learn, it has ballooned in worth over the years and has reached heights that were never dreamed of. Bitcoin has a value, and that value has consistently proven that it is very, very impressive.

Where does it gain its value? It gains its value in the same places that most other currencies gain theirs. It primarily gains its value from supply and demand, but other things influence the value of Bitcoin, such as the amount and speed of Bitcoins being mined, the price of production, governmental issues, and more. There are many systems at work that provide Bitcoin with a value and worth that rivals other currencies all over the world.

However, the value of Bitcoin does drastically change, more so than other currencies. This sort of volatility in the cryptocurrency market will be covered at great lengths later but it must be noted that the price and value of Bitcoin rarely stays in one position for long. If you were to look at a graph with the prices of Bitcoin since its inception, you would see wild ups and downs over the years, major highs, and major lows. To some, this is music to their ears. Some people feel that this sort of volatility is great for investors and stands to make them lots of money. To others, this is the sort of market that is best avoided. Why would you want to put so much faith, time, and effort into something that will have a radically changed value from month to month?

In other words, Bitcoin does have value and Bitcoin has real value that it receives from real factors that often drive and influence other currencies too. But its value changes - and it changes a lot. Why? That has to do with the way Bitcoin works and the way it is driven by the people and for the people. Its extreme changes have been a part of Bitcoin since the very beginning. In fact, some people will say that it is the only constant feature that has remained the same.

3

THE HISTORY OF BITCOIN

We have touched upon the origins of Bitcoin. We have spoken about why it was started, how it was started and who started it. In summation, Bitcoin was a response to a system that many people felt had grown corrupt, outdated, and cruel to the little man. Bitcoin was made to do away with the same sort of institutions that had continually ignored the everyday work and citizen and instead favored those at the top, making the rich richer while the poor grew poorer.

Bitcoin was just a promising but unreadable idea when Satoshi Nakamoto published his or her or their paper on how to create and manage and maintain Bitcoin. From there, things moved quickly, and the first coin was created on January 3, 2009.

After the creation of the first Bitcoin, the system began to come to life and live on its own, a system that would rise or fall based solely on the involvement of those championing it and participating in it.

For the first few years of Bitcoin, the value of Bitcoin remained low. In fact, it was incredibly, incredibly low. In the beginning, a single Bitcoin was valued at only $0.003. The distance it has come since then is truly astronomical.

Though the cost of Bitcoin was very low in its early days, people didn’t know how to use it. They didn’t know how to obtain it. They definitely didn’t know how to determine the value of it. Although the person or group called Satoshi Nakamoto had laid out the blueprint for Bitcoin, he didn’t stick around long to see how far it would go. In 2011, Nakamoto stepped away from Bitcoin to work on other projects. He has not been heard from since and because of his anonymous true identity, he has never been found. The creator of Bitcoin disappeared right as his baby was becoming a toddler.

The early days of Bitcoin were wild and most casual. People who could get their hands on Bitcoins would trade them or use them to buy commodities from others in the community. In May 2010, a Florida programmer named Laszlo Hanyec offered 10,000 Bitcoins in exchange for some Papa John’s pizza. A British Bitcoin fan took him up on the offer and sent two pizzas to Hanyec’s home. That date, May 22, 2010, is now referred to as Bitcoin Pizza Day. To many, it is believed to be the first time Bitcoin was ever used to make a real purchase. To be clear, the Bitcoins didn’t buy the pizzas, the British fan’s credit card did. But it was the first time that Bitcoin was used to receive goods or services. Back in 2010, 10,000 Bitcoins were worth the value of two large pizzas. Times have changed greatly since then.

Around the same time as Bitcoin Pizza Day, users online determined that they needed to establish some sort of online exchange systems if they wished to truly trade and buy Bitcoin in an easy way. They took note from systems such as the stock market and trading sites that easily allowed clients to buy, sell, and trade their individual stocks. If sites like these could be created for Bitcoin, proponents believed that you would see a great number of people looking to buy and sell and grow the popularity of Bitcoin.

Bitcoinmarket.com was launched on January 15, 2010, by a user on a Bitcoin message board. The site was rudimentary at first, but it was also an incredible feat and step forward for the future of Bitcoin. On the site, people would be able to buy Bitcoin at a price determined by users. This would be the way that people decided upon the value of Bitcoin. It would use the most basic principles of capitalism and supply and demand. If people wanted to spend more on Bitcoins, they could. If people thought it didn’t have as much value, their financial offer would express that.

This was a major moment for Bitcoin. It was the moment that things started moving forward and a true, easy-to-understand value of the new currency was founded. By simply supply and demand and speculative opinion from users, Bitcoin was gaining a true sense of worth. As mentioned, it wasn’t a lot. The early days say a single Bitcoin cost way, way less than a penny. But as time went on, the same fundamental ideas that were established at that early trading site would carry forward into others. In just a few years, there would be multiple other ways to buy and trade and speculate on Bitcoin. The value would only go up and up too. But it all started in those early days of 2010 when an anonymous user took it upon him or herself to create a site to dictate the Bitcoin market.

This was another example of Bitcoin being for the people and by the people. In the beginning, no one knew how much a Bitcoin was worth. No one knew if the man who received his pizzas and spent 10,000 Bitcoins to get them was being ripped off or receiving a great deal. No one knew if $0.003 was too high or too low for a Bitcoin. It was all up in the air; nothing was determined and nothing was certain.

But the system worked itself out, not with the help of any bank or government too. The people who believed in Bitcoin stepped forward and showed their faith in the ideas proposed just months before by creating a system that would keep the entire idea of Bitcoin alive. If it weren’t for the people, none of this would have happened. If it weren’t for the people, the future of Bitcoin would have collapsed in such uncertainty. As it stands now, the people were the ones that believed in Bitcoin and pushed it forward.

The Growing Value of Bitcoin

The value of Bitcoin continued to move upward, although slowly, in the weeks and months, and years after its creation. With an actual online exchange established and a way for consumers to decide upon an agreed-on price for the currency, Bitcoin was finding a footing with others and not just the people who had championed it since before it ever existed.

The progress that Bitcoin has made is no more apparent than when you look at its price history. Although the value of Bitcoin is always tied to how enthusiastic its buyers are, there was a time when that enthusiasm didn’t translate into a high worth. In the early days of Bitcoin, it was worth less than a penny. Over time, it progressed and was worth more than that, although not a whole lot more.

In April of 2011, just a few years into its life span, Bitcoin’s price was just $1. That was a big moment for the cryptocurrency, proving that it could compete with the American dollar. Obviously, the plan was for the value of Bitcoin to keep growing until it was worth much, much more than just a single dollar. However, some naysayers never thought it would have progressed even that far. To see it equal a dollar was a watershed moment, but only the beginning.

2011 was the first year that Bitcoin showed just how volatile it could be. A recurring theme for Bitcoin is its wild fluctuations in value. When looking at a chart of Bitcoin’s price over time, you will see very tall highs and very steep lows. It has shifted like this, up and down and up and down and up down, a lot since creation. Its volatility is definitely more extreme than almost all other currencies. That was true back in 2011, which saw Bitcoin make enormous gains…and tremendous losses too.

April 2011 saw Bitcoin reach its value of $1, which as stated was a big deal at the time. It only became a bigger deal when the price of Bitcoin kept growing and growing and growing. In fact, by June of that same year, Bitcoin was worth $32. That’s close to a 3,200% gain in just a few months. Many fans of Bitcoin were ecstatic, seeing this as a sign that cryptocurrency was catching on faster than expected and their ground-floor investment in Bitcoin was going to make them a lot of money. They had gotten in at the right time, many felt. The only direction forward was up, up, up.

But those who understand trading markets also understood that there are many peaks and valleys in trading any commodity assumed that this ascent wouldn’t last forever. Bitcoin’s price would come back down to Earth, perhaps shockingly fast. Sure enough, just a few months after its $32 high, Bitcoin dropped its value all the way down to $2 in November 2011. So, the beginning of the year saw Bitcoin worth $1, the summer of that year had it reach $32 and the end of the year had it at just $2. That was quite the wild ride for investors and a moment that stuck out like a sore thumb to those who believed that Bitcoin would only move upwards. It was a stark reminder that the value of currencies moves, sometimes quickly, and not always in your favor. For the many Bitcoin fans who were trying to invest for the first time, it was a big wake-up call that taught them an important lesson: do not always expect success and do not even count on anything as a sure thing. There are no sure things in investing, even with something you truly believe in.

2012 brought a bit more stability to the world of Bitcoin, with consistent gains that had Bitcoin reach a value of nearly $5 in May and then $13.20 by summer of that year. Bitcoin was moving in the right direction, there was no debate about that. But were there more rapid drops to come?

As the idea of cryptocurrency and especially Bitcoin caught on with more people, more online exchanges, message boards and stories were created about the concept. It was finding more popularity with people from all over the globe, people who believed that Bitcoin really was the future of currency, and it was a good idea to get in on it as close to the beginning as possible. That is why Bitcoin was seeing substantial and often reliable gains in its early years. More people were buying into Bitcoin and sticking around.

That led to 2013, which wound up being a very important and decisive year for the growing cryptocurrency and the community supporting it. For those who thought that there was going to be a year of complete smooth sailing, an unpleasant surprise was waiting for them. While 2013 did have some impressive and wildly large growth, it also had some precipitous declines too.

The year 2013 started with Bitcoin at a value of $13.40, which was just a bit higher than it was in mid-to-late 2012. This suggested that the growth of Bitcoin was now healthy, steady, something you could possibly rely on. Many had assumed that Bitcoin worked out its growing pains and was finally on a pace and speed that it would remain at. This was good news to many. And news spread fast.

But with that news came a lot of new buyers, people looking to get into the Bitcoin game. You would think that is nothing but good news. You would be right that it is good news in a sense because the more that people buy Bitcoin, the higher its value is. It is a win-win situation for everyone who is partaking in the Bitcoin market. However, it is only good news if these newcomers stick around and never pull their money out or sell their stock in Bitcoin. As long as the base of supporters and buyers grows and only grows, the price of Bitcoin will continue to remain stable and even grow. If they leave, if something scares them off and frightens away other new buyers, the coveted price is going to come crashing back down to Earth.

That is what happened later in 2013. Bitcoin’s value entered a bubble. And much like all other bubbles, it popped.

Because of its growing popularity and impressive growth, great interest in Bitcoin only blossomed and by April of 2013, Bitcoin was worth $220. That was quite the change from only a few months before and should have been a great warning to many others that Bitcoin was due for a crash. Maybe it was taken as a warning to others because the value of Bitcoin fell greatly after its high of $220. By only mid-April, it was worth just $70. That was still a lot higher than its earlier $13, but it wasn’t anywhere near its $220.

It didn’t end there. In early October 2013, Bitcoin had climbed back up to $123.20. That was a great sign, an improvement after its big fall. It gave a lot of new investors faith, and they partook in a massive rally that saw the price of Bitcoin skyrocket to $1156 by December 2013. This was Bitcoin’s highest price yet and was light years ahead of what it had started at just months ago. Sadly, it was followed by a colossal drop in value that left Bitcoin at $315 just months later.

This would be the end of just another of Bitcoin’s infamous bubbles. It would be just one of many, a time of great volatility that would scare away many but attract others to the growing potential that was Bitcoin.

The next few years would be a low for Bitcoin, with not much upward trajectory and no major causes for celebration or many positive headlines. It was around this time that Bitcoin was starting to be noticed by the mainstream media more and more. Headlines were not often that kind, citing Bitcoin as a digital currency that was only used by a select few on the internet. Most news coverage paid a lot of attention to Bitcoin’s major declines in value, not its giant gains. The media was definitely interested in Bitcoin, but they didn’t really understand it. This would begin a multi-year journey with the media that would ultimately help and hurt Bitcoin in some major ways. For every gain that Bitcoin encountered, it had its missteps and fallbacks. However, it seemed the media was only attracted to the falls, not the gains. They would also soon be attracted to stories about scandals and politicians' negative statements about Bitcoin and its future and presumed dangers. All of this would culminate in a general suspicious and paranoid feeling from the public about Bitcoin.

However, people in the know - the ones who were buying in Bitcoin - still saw nothing but potential in the young cryptocurrency. They knew that there had been a lot of negative noise about Bitcoin and that plenty of it was founded on truth. The truth was that Bitcoin was off to a rocky start. It had shown that it had the potential to be worth a lot and definitely a smart investment. But it had also shown that it could collapse in value at just a moment’s notice. Bitcoin was not for the faint of heart and was proving itself to be a volatile commodity that wasn’t for the new, inexperienced investor.

However, that was part of the problem that made Bitcoin so wildly mercurial. Because of the nature of Bitcoin, it was attracting a certain type of investor, a certain type of fan. It was pulling in people who were angry at the system and had long shied away from using it. These were not seasoned investors, people with full portfolios brimming with the latest that Wall Street had to offer. These were people who were very tuned in to the world of technology but not very in tune with the stock market or the workings of investing.

In some ways, this was great news for the future of Bitcoin. It was tapping into a fanbase that was full of great potential and a lot of money to spend. If the Bitcoin market could capitalize on these newcomers as well as people who had spent years putting their money into stocks, companies, and the like, the value of cryptocurrency could balloon more and more as the years went on. Additionally, these were people who were supporting Bitcoin not just because they believed in its worth and the future potential it had in terms of buying and selling. They were also buying into Bitcoin because they believed in it. They believed in what it stood for. In their eyes, these early adopters were pushing back against a corrupt system that was hurting millions of people and not giving anything back. Bitcoin was a way to push back against the status quo and buying into it was hurting a monetary system that was hurting them.

Having true believers on your side is always a great thing, it can only keep things more robust, healthier, and supported. However, these early investors were not used to the ups and downs of a market. Since Bitcoin was the first investment for many, it meant that the first sign of trouble was reason to sell and run. A steadfast belief in the ideas behind Bitcoin could only get it so far. If these people saw signs of real trouble, they were likely to cut and run.

In the world of investing, this cycle of brief ups and brief downs is sometimes referred to as the Gartner Hype Cycle. It shows a belief in a product, stock, or currency that is showcased by high peaks and then crashes as people become disillusioned. These bubbles show the potential, and downfalls, of any tradable products. It asks the question if a market, currency, product, or stock has the sort of strong base of buyers to keep it alive and thriving or if it will always mimic the belief (or lack thereof) of the people.

This is one of the reasons why Bitcoin was establishing itself as such a volatile buy in its early years. It was supported by people who were buying in early but also running when trouble would arise. This is a typical feature of certain stocks that bring in new, young, inexperienced buyers. Bitcoin’s base was full of them.
