What Bitcoin needed to cement itself and prove itself to other, more seasoned investors was stability. However, how did it earn that sort of stability if it was constantly showing signs of great insecurity and mistrust among its base? Only time would tell.

The feeling among many Bitcoin investors in 2014 as the first wild few years had left the value of the currency at a low and had turned many away from buying in and boosting its worth.

One of Bitcoin’s first scandals hit around that time, which was bad timing personified. Mt. Gox, which was one of the first online cryptocurrency exchanges where people could buy and sell and trade their Bitcoins, stated that it lost over 850,000 Bitcoins in a hack. That was quickly followed by them filing for bankruptcy in February 2014. This news hit the market hard and helped the price of Bitcoin tumble down 32%. It wasn’t the first time that Mt. Gox had hurt the Bitcoin market. In 2013, various reports of bad management caused Bitcoin value to fall 29%. It was those same reported bad security and poor management that would lead to the eventual hack and downfall of Mt. Gox.

However, it wasn’t all bad news for Bitcoin at that time. In fact, there were a few bright spots. Firstly, the fact that Bitcoin was capable of hitting the highs that it did showed that there really wasn’t a limit to the cryptocurrency’s potential. If it could be worth more than $1,000, it could definitely become even more valuable. While Bitcoin definitely had a lot of issues, issues that were proving themselves troublesome and constant again and again, it also had a huge upside: potential for great worth. Even though many mainstream investors had yet to buy into Bitcoin and were still hesitant to dip their toes in the water, they were definitely taking note of what Bitcoin was proving.

The days of people saying that Bitcoin could and would never be worth anything were gone. People saw that cryptocurrency could actually make people a lot of money. Now, if only it could maintain its growth. If it could pass that hurdle and show a sustained period of stability, it could bring in a lot of people with a lot of money.

Another bright spot in the early years of Bitcoin was its first vote of confidence from the world of retail. That came in January 2014, when online retailer Overstock announced it would begin accepting Bitcoin as a form of payment on their site.

This was a complete success for Bitcoin and put a lot of wind in the sails of the most diehard Bitcoin supporter. Since its inception, people had said that in order for Bitcoin to really work and really show its potential and promise, it would need to catch on in the mainstream. It would be all well and good for Bitcoin to have a loyal and vocal base of supporters but if the coin couldn’t find everyday people to buy, trade, sell, and use Bitcoin then it would never really be more than just a hobby, something that enthusiasts and only enthusiasts used.

How does something so revolutionary and different like Bitcoin catch on with more traditional investors and everyday buyers? By becoming part of the retail culture. Being accepted by Overstock was a major move in that direction.

For a long time, people had been wondering if and when any online stores would start to accept Bitcoin. In a way, it only made sense. Why wouldn’t a major website accept Bitcoin? A digital store should be the first to embrace a digital currency. It seems like the two would go hand-in-hand. However, many online businesses were slow to catch onto the Bitcoin train. Why? There were multiple reasons, but they were mostly the same as to why people were slow to invest and believe in Bitcoin in the first place. Many businesses didn’t understand Bitcoin, they didn’t know how to implement it, they didn’t trust it, they didn’t think it had value, and they didn’t know how to use it once they had it.

What many believed about businesses accepting Bitcoin was that it could be like dominoes falling. Once one business took it upon itself to accept Bitcoin, others would follow. Once one major business showed that Bitcoin was a safe and easy way to accept payment, other companies would surely do the same. Supporters of Bitcoin always believed that they just needed one company to believe in them to show the others what they were missing out.

When Overstock finally decided it would accept Bitcoin in 2014, Bitcoin fans thought it would be the watershed moment they were waiting for. They had long had their sights set on some of the biggest names in the retail industry: companies like Amazon or Target or Walmart. Overstock wasn’t at the same level as those brands but it was certainly a well-known and successful company. With them involved, many others could soon fall in line and do the same.

It wasn’t that watershed moment. While Overstock taking on Bitcoin was a big news story and one that definitely raised the profile of the up-and-coming cryptocurrency, it didn’t end with the retail world quickly jumping in line behind Bitcoin. Amazon, Target, and others were still out of reach and that left many Bitcoin fans feeling uncertain and a bit burned by their greatest desires and hopes for the future of Bitcoin. The dominoes didn’t fall like they expected. But Overstock’s inclusion of Bitcoin in their company was still a major deal and a cause for pure celebration.

Bitcoin’s Connection with Valued Investors

Overstock taking on Bitcoin was the beginning of a time of great change for Bitcoin. The original investors were sticking around and good news like the Overstock announcement helped raised its profile to the right people. While mainstream success was still a few years off, the people that needed to believe in Bitcoin were starting to really take notice. There were still many questions and a lot of uncertainty about Bitcoin, there always would be. However, some of the most seasoned investors were starting to buy into the idea behind Bitcoin.

Yet, were they buying in for the same reasons that many Bitcoin fans were? There was a new trend brewing in the world of cryptocurrency around 2014, a trend that continues to this day and leaves some fans upset and some happy.

Some of the early adopters and true believers in Bitcoin were doing it out of principle, a belief in the ideals behind Bitcoin and what it stood for. These people were strongly aligned with the premise that Bitcoin was a way to change the troubled monetary systems that left so many out in the cold. This was a way to disrupt the status quo. Not only that, Bitcoin stood a chance to be a true new currency, something that people could use to buy computers, cars, houses, and even things like groceries, clothing, and more. Many people believed in not only the idea of Bitcoin and the spirit of it but also the functionality of it.

These people wanted Bitcoin to be the future of spending. They didn’t want to destroy the American dollar or other currencies, but they did want the option of using Bitcoin - and only Bitcoin - if they so wished. That was the future they envisioned, one that allowed them to spend their Bitcoin at the store, any store, and avoid the dollar, and banks, and governments altogether.

Some of the investors that were buying into Bitcoin around 2014 and 2015 didn’t hold the same views. They saw value in Bitcoin, there was no doubt about that. But the value they saw had nothing to do with dismantling unfair policy and procedures laid out by the banks and government. No, these investors were the more developed and traditional ones that made their fortunes off the stock market. In the world of Wall Street, the bottom line is making money. While it is not always the case, usually investors in Wall Street are looking to boost the value of a stock for the sole purpose of putting more cash in their portfolio. Do these Wall Street pros believe in the companies they invest in? Are they trying to make them successful because they stand behind their products or objectives? Sometimes, but not often. More often than not, they are putting their money into the stock because they see an opportunity to ride it to more success. When they make all the money they can off a stock, they sell it. Buy low, sell high, that is an age-old rule in the stock market. You are not looking to make success for the companies you invest in, you are looking to make success for yourself.

Around 2014, 2015, and beyond, Bitcoin was starting to attract some of these investors and it started to change the reason why many people were buying Bitcoin. Where many of those who stuck with Bitcoin since the early days were doing so because they had a strong allegiance to the currency and its purpose, they also did so because they saw Bitcoin as a future currency for daily transactions and trading. These other investors saw it as a stock, a commodity they could make money off of.

This is how their procedure worked: buy into Bitcoin as you would a stock on Wall Street. Find the right time when the value is at its best and buy the desired amount. Then ride the Bitcoin market like you would ride the stock market and watch your value rise and rise. When you are ready to sell, sell your Bitcoin for a high amount, and use an online exchange to cash out and convert your Bitcoin earnings into traditional currency. It is a great way to make a load of money.

To some, this was insulting. It looked like a group of Wall Street pirated plundering the Bitcoin market to make a quick buck and then cash out. Worst of all, they were cashing out by going right back to the same banks and currencies that many people were trying to turn away from.

At the same time, this group of investors was really adding a lot of value to Bitcoin and were boosting the worth of it across the board. Everyone was enjoying the success they brought. All investors reap the benefits of Bitcoin doing well.

But it left a bad taste in many people’s mouths. They were now a part of a system that looked a lot like the ones they were trying to avoid when they first put their time and energy into Bitcoin. While these new investors were helping the overall value of Bitcoin, they were treating it solely as a market like the stock market. The notions and beliefs behind Bitcoin were being lost but doing business with and alongside these people was feeling like a necessary evil for Bitcoin’s success.

It would culminate in 2017 when the cryptocurrency would reach its newest and most impressive heights.

2017 and Beyond

2017 started in an impressive way for Bitcoin. As the year began, the price of the coin was steady around $1,000. The last few years had seen slow, progressive movement in the right direction thanks to multiple factors such as new investors, good news surrounding online retail, and a sort of stability that was enticing to people who sat on the fence about Bitcoin.

The beginning of the year was cause for celebration and a sign that perhaps Bitcoin had once again hit a solid stride, a phase in its life cycle that could last for a long time and bring much financial benefit to all involved.

Even the original early adapters to Bitcoin were feeling more comfortable working alongside some of the inside Wall Street people they initially didn’t like being associated with. They had strong feelings about Bitcoin being driven by interest from some of those who worked in the stock market, they feared they were only coming to make a quick profit, convert it into cash, and then abandon Bitcoin like an orange that had been squeezed. But as the $1,000 mark loomed, these true believers saw that perhaps the new investors were sticking around, maybe they had been won over by the power of Bitcoin and were here for the long haul.

Whoever was involved with Bitcoin in 2017 should have been prepared for a rollercoaster ride because that is exactly what they got. The dull, quiet period in Bitcoin’s life was over and the cryptocurrency was about to show just how powerful it really could be. When the year was finished, Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency would never be the same.

The first two months of 2017 found Bitcoin with a decline in value, but they were only brief drops. Soon after, the value of Bitcoin began to go up and didn’t stop going up for months. The entire year showed nothing but intense growth unlike anyone had ever seen. Spectators and investors couldn’t believe what they were seeing, the value of Bitcoin was skyrocketing and showed no signs of slowing down. By the time that December rolled around, Bitcoin was now worth $20,089. That was by far the highest market the crypto had ever reached and was legitimately astounding.

For the first time since it was created, Bitcoin was treated very favorably by the media. Many were now asking if Bitcoin was the future of money, if everyday investors should get in, if businesses should start accepting it, and more. The reaction was strong and those who had been with Bitcoin since the beginning were now seeing their early investments pay off in a major way.

Though all involved knew that the record-setting value of Bitcoin wouldn’t remain that way forever, that didn’t stop people from fawning over the cryptocurrency and remarking that it had finally reached its full potential. Even those who were most lukewarm about Bitcoin from the beginning saw just how valuable it could be in 2017. It was a true turning point for the currency. However, it wasn’t the end of controversy and decline. There would still be rough days ahead.

It was around this time that certain governments around the world really started to take note of Bitcoin. It was impossible to not notice it when it was worth so much money and creating such waves in the financial sector. But the reaction from governments was not as positive as some had hoped for. Like most new things that are hard to understand, the governments and politicians of the world looked at Bitcoin with suspicion and mistrust.

Governments didn’t know how to handle Bitcoin and they didn’t understand what it meant for them. To some, it was a welcome idea, something that could eventually stimulate the economy and boost retail sales in some new, innovative ways. However, for others it was a threat. It was a threat to the well-being of traditional currency and, more importantly, it was a threat to the status quo that decided who did and did not have power.

The purpose behind Bitcoin’s creation was not lost on those running the governments all over the world. They were aware that the cryptocurrency was started as a reaction to the financial crisis of 2008 and the way that so many in power looked away as rules were broken, pocketbooks were filled, and the hard-working everyday citizen was ignored. These powerful figures knew that Bitcoin was about changing all of that and giving the power back to the people. Obviously, that didn’t sit well with many governments all over the world. An idea like the one that created Bitcoin was dangerous for their way of being.

Others thought that Bitcoin really could disrupt financial institutions all over the world. What would happen if it really did replace the Dollar, the Yen, and other currencies? How could an economy survive with a new currency or with multiple currencies fighting for ultimate control?

It is no surprise that around 2017 and Bitcoin’s major gains, governments all over the globe started talking about the idea of creating their own cryptocurrencies. It was an obvious way for them to compete with Bitcoin as it gained popularity. It was also a way to lay the groundwork for possibly banning Bitcoin outright. If the government had its very own cryptocurrency, a national digital currency, they could pass legislation saying that only the federally recognized cryptocurrency was acceptable. That would be a death blow to Bitcoin all over the world. So, while some governments were starting to think about making their own cryptocurrencies because it was innovative and the future of money, others were doing it as a way to push Bitcoin out of the picture.

The first nation to create its own national cryptocurrency was Ecuador back in 2015. It was no surprise that right before their digital currency was launched, that nation banned all other forms of cryptocurrency. That move proved that not only did Ecuador see the potential in cryptocurrency, but it also saw the dangers to its own national currency in it. The only way to disarm the power of Bitcoin was to create a competitor and then promptly ban any competition.

Ecuador wasn’t the only nation at that time that was making strict rules related to Bitcoin. The biggest country, as it often is, was China. China has been one of the strongest superpowers on Earth for decades now and is only growing in strength and power. Plus, China sets the standard for other countries, even those who disagree with it. America, for example, responds to China’s actions. As does Russia, Britain, and others. Whatever China does, other countries take notice. It is impossible to completely ignore China’s decisions.

Being a semi-communist country, China enforces strict rules about what its citizens can and cannot use when it comes to money and purchasing and even other things such as freedom of speech. It didn’t come as a shock in 2013 when China banned all banks and financial institutions from handling Bitcoin transactions. However, 2017 saw a more drastic step taken. It was then that China banned the online cryptocurrency exchanges that were proving to be quite popular all over the globe. Without these exchanges, buying and selling cryptocurrencies was essentially impossible in China, one of the biggest countries in the world. That was of course a major blow to Bitcoin. Their insistence that Bitcoin is not a legal tender and shouldn’t be accepted hurt users even more because they understood the desire for Bitcoin in China is and was strong. For example, though Bitcoin is illegal in China, mining for them is not. And there is actually a large number of Chinese citizens who are actively crypto mining. That proves that, if given the ability, many in China would be using, selling, and trading Bitcoin.

India is another powerhouse in the world’s economy and whenever they make a major move, other nations take notice. Technically India has never outlawed or banned Bitcoin, though they have threatened to do so multiple times. However, in 2018 India banned banks and any regulated financial institutions from “dealing with or settling virtual currencies.”

Then India went even further and enacted new rules that prohibited the trade of cryptocurrencies on domestic exchanges. That sweeping regulation lasted until 2020 when the Indian Supreme Court ruled the ban was unconstitutional and forced all exchanges to reopen.

India has long toyed with becoming one of the strictest nations in the world when it comes to Bitcoin. Not only have they made it clear that they want to get rid of cryptocurrency, but they have also suggested that anyone violating set rules will face serious repercussions. In fact, leaked documents related to a possible bill that the government of India was thinking of passing suggested serious jail time for citizens who “mine, generate, hold, sell, deal in, issue, transfer, dispose of, or use cryptocurrency in the territory of India.” Essentially, they were planning to jail anyone who had anything to do with Bitcoin. That proposed bill never passed, partly due to the reaction to the leaked documents and harsh sentences for anyone breaking the rules.

But India wasn’t done with its strict stance on cryptocurrency. In 2020, new leaks showed the government of India was planning new bans on cryptocurrency trading. It is unknown how far India will go to meet those goals, and it is also unknown how strongly they will strike down upon those who violate the rules.

One thing is also clear: India isn’t against the idea of cryptocurrency, just cryptocurrency that isn’t created and run by the Indian government. In fact, India has publicly stated that it is open to exploring the idea of a national cryptocurrency and using blockchain technology. That proves that India may in fact see open cryptocurrency use in the future, just not with Bitcoin or any form of it that isn’t run by the government.

It isn’t just China and India that are cracking down hard on Bitcoin, especially since it started to show its true potential around 2017. Other nations are pushing strict rules about Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency. Russia, for example, does not specifically regulate Bitcoin but its use as a payment is illegal in that major nation. Vietnam has stated that Bitcoin is not a legitimate payment method. Other South American countries such as Bolivia and Columbia have banned the use of Bitcoin or other cryptocurrencies.

The vast majority of other countries have not come out swinging against Bitcoin - at least not yet. There stands a chance that other nations, including some as powerful and large as the United States, could decide to limit or almost ban the use of Bitcoin in their regions. They may make it harder to use Bitcoin, make it more difficult for companies to accept it, they may do a lot of things to make sure that Bitcoin is severely weakened. We will get to why they would do that later, but it must never be forgotten that Bitcoin is always at the mercy of those in power, even though it doesn’t need them to properly function.

While 2017 was a banner year for Bitcoin, the next couple of years were not as massive. The value of Bitcoin came back to Earth in a major way and was usually resting somewhere around $9,000-$10,000. By the end of 2019, Bitcoin was remaining fairly steady at about $7,000.

But then the entire world - not just the world of cryptocurrency - changed. The coronavirus pandemic came storming onto the scene in early 2020 and it radically altered how all business was done. It also showed Bitcoin’s worth to new people and caused the value of the cryptocurrency to intensify intensely.

In early 2020, nations all over the world had to shut down practically all ways of life. People worked from home, businesses had to lock their doors, restaurants had to stop seating guests, it was unlike anything the world had seen in a hundred years.

Expectedly, this sort of mass shutdown severely hurt the economy almost instantly. Unemployment shot up, companies went bankrupt, entire industries had futures that were anything but certain. The world was in economic chaos and governments had to act quickly. Most of them started economic stimulus plans, where they would send checks to citizens. This was a way for unemployed people to pay their bills and to keep some sort of life force flowing through the economy. Without this, certain doom awaited.

Something interesting happened as economies across the globe shut down: the value of Bitcoin started to come to life more and more. The year of 2020 started with Bitcoin at a value of $7,200. That was an average price, a strong number that paled in comparison to previous highs but didn’t come close to previous lows.

As the pandemic raged across the globe, it once again showed weaknesses in economies all over the world. This fed into investors’ fears about the fragility of the world economy and how easily it could slip into complete disarray. People turned away from the traditional system, just as they did during the Great Recession of 2008. As a result, renewed interest was focused on Bitcoin.

Why was the pandemic making Bitcoin such an attractive buy? For multiple reasons but mostly because it was exposing just how uncertain a global economy was. When all the nations of the world were performing well, an interconnected economy was a great thing. Every nation benefits and everyone sees the benefits of it. However, when everything is so connected, everything reliant on the same system and procedures, things can fall apart quickly. As it was with the Great Recession, all the countries of Earth were tied together. When one fell, the other fell with it.

Bitcoin proved to be so enticing during the pandemic because it was a digital currency in a world that was forced to go all-digital. It simply wasn’t possible to go into a brick-and-mortar store during the pandemic, they were all closed for safety reasons. People were forced to buy things online, from clothing to groceries to meals to much more. The entire world was now reliant on the same systems and approach that cryptocurrency had been championing for years now.

It only made sense that people were looking at Bitcoin with a new interest. In a world living online, the online currency could be king. It was also proving why working away from the traditional system could be so enticing. As economies closed and monetary systems that had been around for generations were struggling to survive, the world of Bitcoin remained safe in its online bubble. It didn’t need the system that was suffering from the COVID-19 pandemic, and it was even stronger because of it. Through sickness and health, Bitcoin was staying strong.

As the year of the lockdown continued, the value of Bitcoin continued to move upward. It started the year at around $7,000 and ended at $24,000. In just a year, Bitcoin saw an increase in value of 224%. That was an astronomical gain, and it didn’t end there. The price of Bitcoin kept moving up and up as the pandemic raged on.

As countries sent out economic relief checks to residents, the value of Bitcoin continued to improve because there was a new base of supporters who were looking to buy Bitcoin. They were using the money their governments gave them and turning it into something more reliable: cryptocurrency. For once, Bitcoin seemed more stable than the regular economy. This was doubly true as parts of the United States started suffering from cash shortages and more lockdown mandates. Uncertainty reigned and Bitcoin continued to seem more and more promising.

Bitcoin continued to reach new peaks and then surpass them. As 2021 began, Bitcoin was already at a value of $40,000 per coin. It passed that peak just days later when it hit a new high of $41,528 on January 8, 2021. It dropped a lot after that, dipping to $30,525 in just a few days, but it rebounded again and as 2021 moved forward, Bitcoin was seen reaching heights of just under $60,000 per coin. The days of being worth less than a penny were far, far behind.

In just over ten years, the value of Bitcoin has gained substantially despite many ups and downs. It is still a volatile currency, its value will see-saw from very, very high to very, very low in even a matter of days. But the fact that it can reach a high of $60,000 is incredible, something that was thought of as impossible, even among the most diehard Bitcoin fans. Bitcoin has shown its worth. It’s shown its volatility, which cannot be ignored. But it has truly proven that the sky is the limit.

And with that, renewed interest, renewed suspicion, and renewed controversies have returned.

WHY DO PEOPLE DISTRUST BITCOIN?

As you have heard time and time again, there is a level of distrust aimed at Bitcoin that is unparalleled. Many people do not understand Bitcoin and believe the most hyped and sensationalized conspiracies about it. Some people are led to believe that Bitcoin is only used by hackers, scammers, people who want to steal your hard-earned, tangible cash. Other people think that Bitcoin can easily be hacked and stolen and there is no reason to invest because it will be taken from you by some criminal computer pro. Other people think that it cannot be used anywhere, or that it has no value at all.

There are many confused and unfounded thoughts about Bitcoin and that has led to rampant speculation and skewed biases and views. However, there are others who know better. There are people who know the particulars of Bitcoin and cryptocurrency and how it works. They are realists, they understand the benefits and the disadvantages of Bitcoin. Still, they choose to rail against Bitcoin and fight tooth and nail to make sure it doesn’t find a solid footing in the modern world. Why do these people fight so strongly against Bitcoin and its potential? What are they afraid of? Why do they hate it so much? Why is there such anger directed at Bitcoin by the people who should know much, much better?

(Traditional) Money is Power

It is important to remember just how powerful and important money is. There is no way to deny that, nothing to debate about that statement. People say that money is the root of all evil and that may be true. But it is also the root of all power. And when someone has power, they want to hold onto it.

It is worth noting that someone isn’t evil just because they want to stay in control of their power and keep things the way they are. There is a strong argument to be made there. When things change too drastically or too quickly, the entire way of the world can collapse. There are people who legitimately want to hold onto the power they have gained because they truly believe that the alternative could spell doom for not just economies but the people within them. Not everyone who wants to keep the status quo strong is a money-hungry monster. People have goals, expectations, biases, and desires. It isn’t wrong to fight for what you truly believe is right.

Many people who have gained a lot of power in governments and major corporations have done so by the means of money. They have made a lot of money and spent a lot of money to get to where they are now. They have done this using fiat currencies. Fiat is a term used to describe conventional currencies, like the Dollar, Yen, etc.

The thing about fiat currencies is that they only really have value because the governments say they do. For example, the United States government has said that the dollar is the only valuable currency in the United States. If they came out one day and declared that seashells were the new, only accepted currency in America, seashells would now have value and the dollar would just be a flimsy piece of paper that meant nothing. Fiat currencies are not technically backed by any tangible assets. There is a term that you may see when reading about money. It says “backed by the full faith and credit” of the government. That means that the government believes in that currency, that is why it is valuable. It has been chosen, deemed appropriate, and that is why you use it.

As technology has advanced, that means less and less to some people. But for those in power, the fiat currency that nations have used for generations needs to stay the only, unrivaled form of payment. That is the sort of currency they have amassed, that is the sort of currency that makes the world go around, that is the sort of currency that keeps corporations thriving and the stock market booming. If fiat currencies didn’t have as much value because they had legitimate competition, the power wouldn’t be as strong. Money is power and there is only one type of money that can be recognized: the money that the powerful use. That is fiat currency.

Governments do not want new forms of currency because they do not know how to handle it. They do not know how to properly monitor it, tax it, control it. Current governments have great control over how much money is in circulation and how much it gets taxed. They can track money because they control money. If fiat currencies lost their power and more people turned to cryptocurrency, the government would not be as in control and that is something they desperately want to avoid.

Without control over currency, governments of the world would not be able to control fiscal policy, crime, the world of business.

It is not that those in power think the concept behind Bitcoin or cryptocurrency is a bad one. There are some who think the idea is revolutionary and important, there are some who truly do believe in the spirit of branching off and starting something new. However, many of these same people know that if the power is divided among those who currently have it and others, things can get messy. What will happen to the economy? What will happen to the business industry? So many things would be altered if cryptocurrency became more mainstream. Most notably, the balance of power would shift. They do not want that.

Unfortunately, that is why so many have turned to innuendo and downright conspiracies and lies about cryptocurrency. There are some politicians and industrialists who have come right out and said that they just do not want to see cryptocurrency thrive because it stands a chance to disrupt the way things are currently run. That is an honest view of things. Others have spread misinformation about Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency, saying it is easily hacked, it has no value, it will drain your bank account, and leave you in ruins.

The truth is that many of these people know the reality: that cryptocurrency isn’t easily hackable but in fact very safe. Bitcoin does have value, just not based upon the dollar or fiat currency. Lastly, it will not drain your bank account and will not leave you in ruins. It is actually less dangerous and safer than the dollar and many other forms of traditional money.

But the problem doesn’t just lie in the fact that some people do not like cryptocurrencies and Bitcoin. There is no crime there. In fact, there is nothing to stop them from hating Bitcoin and spreading falsehoods about it. The problem arises when these same people hold government office and use their power to further spread lies and push laws that will limit people’s ability to openly use and buy Bitcoin. That is when things cross over into the downright wrong.

While there are some who genuinely want what is best for consumers and buyers, there are others who do not want Bitcoin to become popular because that means they stand a chance at losing the vast amount of power they have gathered over the years. Oftentimes, you see these people spreading rumors or misinformation about Bitcoin in hopes that it will prevent the cryptocurrency from finding a new home with investors all over the globe.

These people, along with those who really do not trust the power of Bitcoin and are coming from a place of genuine care and concern, sometimes come together to pressure lawmakers to pass legislation that will stop Bitcoin in its tracks. So far, no one has been able to stop the development of Bitcoin in the most major countries, like the United States. In this country, and others, Bitcoin remains free and unregulated. But that hasn't stopped attempts to limit or outright ban it.

Confusion In the Marketplace

While some people are against cryptocurrency because of its threat to their long-held power, there are others who just cannot wrap their heads around what Bitcoin is. There are scores and scores of people all over the world who do not understand what Bitcoin and cryptocurrency is and see it as a threat to the power of the dollar and their own bank account.

While those two fears couldn't be further from the truth, you cannot blame people for not truly grasping what Bitcoin is or how it works. In fact, some could argue that this issue is the fault of those who support Bitcoin. If they want cryptocurrency to take hold across the country and across the world, they need to go to great lengths to spread the word and explain Bitcoin in terms and ways that a common, non-investor would understand. That has yet to happen, and Bitcoin has remained shrouded in mystery. Confusion creates fear and that is exactly what many people feel when talking about Bitcoin.

Can you really blame people for being in the dark about Bitcoin? It isn't the easiest concept to explain. In laymen's terms, Bitcoin is a digital currency that you can ideally use anywhere. It can be used to buy stuff or can be traded for other coins. That is an explanation that is easy to understand but the biggest question people have about Bitcoin is: why? Why does it exist? More importantly, why should it exist when the dollar does just fine? Is this a replacement for the dollar? Is this something that people will be forced to use? Is this an idea that is trying to destroy the dollar and modern financial institutions such as banks and investment companies?

The answer is no, of course. While Bitcoin can be an alternative for modern, fiat currencies, there are few supporters who want to truly do away with the dollar or other forms of traditional currencies. Yet, without a strong push to make Bitcoin understood all over the world, speculation and confusion has reigned. Now there is a common misconception that Bitcoin is used as a tool to break down the system and completely get rid of it. The truth is that it isn't trying to destroy the current system, just provide an alternative choice for people who want it. A choice that doesn't rely on banks or the current system that favors few and neglects many more.

The truth is that many people instinctively trust positions of power. They trust the institutions that have been around for a long time, that they pay for, that they are told are keeping them safe. There is nothing wrong with that. In fact, it could be argued that these institutions should be trusted...when they are used responsibly and with lots of reason and knowledge. We all know that doesn't happen nearly enough.

But a lack of knowledge about Bitcoin and cryptocurrency has led many to wholly believe what they are told by the media, by politicians, by people looking to spread misinformation. That has cultivated an entire culture that looks sideways at all things Bitcoin. They do not understand it, they do not trust it, they think that there are ulterior motives behind it. Then they are told by people they have been taught to trust that there are plenty of reasons to not trust or believe in Bitcoin. It is coming for your bank account, they are told. It is coming for your retirement fund and your local bank. It is easy to believe all of that when you are not informed of anything else.

If proponents of Bitcoin worked harder to push the truth about cryptocurrency, this would not be a roadblock they would encounter. They could have millions and millions more supporters on their side if they handled the situation strongly. They need to understand that much of the battle for the future of Bitcoin will be related to public relations. It will be about winning the hearts and minds of the common person, the person who may not be investing or buying Bitcoin right now but could stand in the way of legislation that could block it.

That is important because there have been laws passed by various governors that have limited the power of Bitcoin and all cryptocurrencies. Quietly over the last decade, numerous politicians and legislators all over America have been pushing pro- and anti-Bitcoin rules that have greatly shaped the future of the currency and have left the United States divided.

States Fight for (And Against) Bitcoin

Bitcoin was originally seen as a stateless - and nationless - digital currency that could be accepted everywhere and would have no regulations no matter where you were spending it. One of the most exciting aspects of the premise behind Bitcoin was that you could use it anywhere and use it on anything. There should be no reason why Bitcoin shouldn't be universally legal and unchecked in all jurisdictions, not just in the United States but all over the world.

However, that hasn't been the case. As stated, many countries all over the world have enacted certain rules for and against Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies. The same is true for individual countries in the United States. Since the federal government has so far tried to stay out of the cryptocurrency battle, deciding to not regulate it and let the people run it themselves, some states have stepped up to either support or push back against the rise in popularity of Bitcoin.

Some states are better than others for Bitcoin. Because of state-level laws that have been signed by numerous governors, Bitcoin is more easily used and traded and bought in some states and harder to come by and support in others.

For example, New York has long been a nemesis of Bitcoin. This isn't surprising considering how many people of power associated with Wall Street live in New York state. It would stand to reason that these powerful people would use their connections with the governor to limit the power of Bitcoin. That is exactly what has happened because New York has never been supportive of Bitcoin. In New York, all online exchanges have to disclose their entire global client base to state officials. To some, that sounds like a huge invasion of privacy. This is just an example of the ways that New York State has tried to stymie the power of Bitcoin.

New York Governor Andrew Cuomo created a Bitcoin task force to study the use and intricacies of Bitcoin so there may be changes in the future if the task force finds there is nothing to fear with Bitcoin. Supporters of cryptocurrency are hoping that's the case.

Another state that has come out swinging against cryptocurrency and Bitcoin is Rhode Island. Their senator, Sheldon Whitehouse, has long railed against Bitcoin and has said it is a way for foreign interference in America's business. He was a major supporter of state-wide legislation that heavily taxed online exchanges and all cryptocurrency transactions. This shows that it is not only Sheldon Whitehouse who thinks that Bitcoin and crypto can be a tool for foreign governments and adversaries.

Other states, such as Arizona, have created certain laws and taxes that are the opposite of welcoming for Bitcoin. This has hurt the base of supporters in those states and has created major pushback from cryptocurrency fans. It flies in the face of the ideas and purpose behind Bitcoin, which has always been about a universal currency that would not be held back by governments because it didn't need them. Bitcoin can thrive on its own without the regulation or oversight or help of any federal body of power.

But it cannot exist without them. The fact of the matter is that Bitcoin was always going to be going head-to-head with governments. There is no way around that. These strict laws being pushed by certain nations and states show that Bitcoin may exist outside the system, but it also remains inside of it in a major way too.

Thankfully, not all states are trying to limit the power and strength of Bitcoin. There are other regions in America that are actually quite supportive of cryptocurrency. For example, Colorado passed a law in 2018 that encouraged state agencies to use blockchain technology to keep its confidential records secret. That was seen as a huge sign of support for the crypto community. This was also matched by legislation that allowed cryptocurrency donations in political campaigns and a bill that made cryptocurrencies exempt from certain securities regulations. Colorado has proven that it isn't afraid of Bitcoin. In fact, it sees the value in the technology behind it.

Although it is usually known for its conservative politics that many would think could put them on the opposing side of Bitcoin and cryptocurrency, the state of Texas has actually shown itself to be an ally in the advancement of Bitcoin-related technology. That state has become a hub of crypto mining facilities, there are multiple major crypto mining locations throughout the Lone Star State. This is in part due to the cheap cost of electricity and energy in Texas, since using energy is a major part of crypto-mining and the more expensive it is, the harder the mining becomes. But Texas has in fact welcomed much of the mining companies because of the business, income, and employment it brings.

California, unsurprisingly, is another state which has tried to embrace Bitcoin and cryptocurrency. Their governor signed a law in 2014 that affirmed that "various forms of alternative currency such as digital currency" are legal payments when purchasing goods. Many of the major crypto companies in the world are located in California, such as Kraken, Ripple, Coinbase, and more.

Perhaps the most crypto-friendly state in the union would be Wyoming. The least populated state also happens to be one of the most engaging and positive when it comes to Bitcoin and cryptocurrencies. Wyoming has passed multiple laws to recognize and support cryptocurrencies. Their state legislature even created a bill that grants digital currencies the same legal status as other money. Because of their long-held support for cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, Wyoming has become home to many crypto companies and has received its fair share of praise from the community that uses them. Wyoming might not be the first place you think of when you talk crypto, but it should be. It has shown time and again that it supports the future of cryptocurrency.

Some states are in favor of Bitcoin and will do much to help advance it, others are against it and are pushing legislation that will limit it. The fight continues on, and will for some time. As long as the federal government stays out of the fray, it will be up to individual states to decide how Bitcoin and cryptocurrencies are regulated. Though that may mean it is sometimes a tough road, crypto fans are supportive of this approach because many of them do not have confidence in federal officials. They fear that crypto proves too much of a risk and danger to the federal government, so they prefer they just stay out of the arguments altogether. The more hands-off the government is, the better.

However, that doesn't mean that the United States government has never dipped their toes in the fight and limited a cryptocurrency. In fact, they have created an outright ban on at least one cryptocurrency and that decision put a spotlight on just how politics and foreign affairs can mix and potentially make a huge mess for the future of cryptocurrency.

The Petro Controversy

In early 2019, President Donald Trump signed a law that banned a cryptocurrency. That's right, the United States has outlawed one type of cryptocurrency. Why and how? And what does that mean for the future of Bitcoin and other digital currencies?

The banned crypto is called Petro, and is a state-sponsored cryptocurrency from the South American country of Venezuela. The ban came amidst an uncomfortable and strained relationship with that nation, after years of bickering and public disagreements and even talk of military intervention. The Trump administration did not get along well with Venezuela and was none too happy to sign a ban outlawing Petro.

On the surface, the ban was put in place to protest the country and its treatment of citizens and disregard for human life, that was the main reason for the law which was supported by both Democrats and Republicans. However, the signing of the law set a precedent that scared many crypto fans. It showed that an American president was not above banning forms of cryptocurrency, especially if it was for a reason they strongly believed in. If any form of cryptocurrency created controversy or happened to be associated with parties, people, or nations that the United States didn't like, a ban could easily be signed without much hesitation.

Petro was backed by Venezuelan president Nicolas Maduro, someone that President Trump did not get along well with at all. In fact, they butted heads often. Many saw the ban as a move to get under Maduro's skin. The fear is that a future cryptocurrency that proves to be very popular globally may be closely associated with a foreign country or leader that a current administration doesn't like. What then? Will the United States ban another cryptocurrency? They have proven that they could, they even have the framework for more bans in the law that prohibited Petro. All a future administration needs to do is change some of the wording and they could have a ready-made ban for any form of cryptocurrency, including Bitcoin.

Since all digital currencies can be global currencies, they all stand the chance at becoming closely associated with nations that the United States doesn't like. China is sitting out of the cryptocurrency game currently but what if they decided to create a cryptocurrency and it did incredibly well. The same goes for Russia, what would happen if that adversary to America threw their hat into the ring and created or backed or started extensively used a cryptocurrency that the world bought into? What would happen if American citizens ended up wanting it, only for it to be outlawed? The future of Bitcoin and all cryptocurrencies could be in the air if politics got involved.

One of the more fearful statements made during the Petro incident was spoken by United States Secretary of Treasury Steven Mnuchin. Mnuchin said that Bitcoin had been used to "support billions of dollars of illicit activity" such as tax evasion, illicit drugs, human trafficking, and more. Mnuchin said that the United States had no plans to outlaw or ban Bitcoin or any cryptocurrency, but they were definitely going to keep a close eye on things to make sure that everything was being done to keep the entire crypto industry legal and on the straight and narrow.

Mnuchin isn't alone in his views. Others in Washington, both Democrat and Republican, have come out swinging against Bitcoin, with claims of great crimes and dangers posed by the cryptocurrency. Senator Majority Leader Chuck Schumer said that Bitcoin was "untraceable" and was a wonderful form of currency to be used by criminals. Senator Joe Manchin of West Virginia called for a ban on Bitcoin because it "illicit[s] activity, including drug trafficking and money laundering."

This opposition and speculation and suspicion about Bitcoin from Mnuchin, Schumer, and others is not completely unfounded. The fact of the matter is that Bitcoin and cryptocurrency is used for illegal activities. There is no denying that. In 2019, it was determined that cryptocurrencies funded about $11.5 billion of illegal transactions. That is a large number, to be sure. However, that number represents just a bit more than 1% of all cryptocurrency transactions that year. So, while there are crimes associated with Bitcoin and crypto, they pale in comparison to the vast, vast majority of crypto transactions that are completely legal, safe, and of no harm to anyone.

It should also be noted that the cost of all money laundering worldwide is somewhere between $800 million and $2 trillion annually. Plus, studies have shown that the vast, vast, vast majority of all money laundering and financial crimes are committed using fiat currencies over digital ones. The fact of the matter is that most criminals use traditional forms of money to commit their crimes.

Mnuchin and others should also take note of the fact that the blockchain system of technology that runs Bitcoin makes it incredibly easy to track and find criminals and their illicit moves. The blockchain is all about keeping a public and very open record of every transaction that is made using Bitcoin. Therefore, if someone is doing something against the law with Bitcoin, it can be found easily in the blockchain. That isn't possible with traditional currencies. It is much harder to find crimes that are committed using regular money.

Mnuchin and other politicians are right when they say that Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies are used by criminals all over the world. That is sadly the truth. But that statement is only half of the truth. The whole truth is that while some are using Bitcoin for the most nefarious reasons, there are many more people who use it for the most common and mundane and legal reasons. The Bitcoin community is not full of criminals, no more than the fiat currency community is. There are bad guys in there, but there are bad guys in every form of currency, including the American dollar. The truth remains that a criminal is far more likely to use fiat currency over digital.

Some also call out the fact that Secretary Mnuchin used to work for Goldman Sachs, one of the oldest and most well-known money managers on Wall Street. Some see this as just another example of the old guard being afraid of the new, modern form of currency that threatens their vast amount of power.

Growing Fears About Bitcoin

Because of legislation and controversy, the fear around Bitcoin has only grown over the last decade with the common person. It was hard to understand Bitcoin during the best of times, but now rampant speculation, unchecked allegations, and scandalous headlines have made many people outright scared about Bitcoin.

What are the biggest fears about cryptocurrency in general, and how should they be dismantled? It all starts with confusion about it, something we have mentioned before. People are confused about Bitcoin. They are confused about the concept, they are confused about how it is bought, how it is traded, how it is stored. Frankly, many people are confused by the idea of having a sort of currency that you cannot hold in your hands or insert into an ATM. This could easily be addressed and fixed if more businesses partook in Bitcoin and made it a viable means of payment. Imagine walking into a local business, or a major retailer, and seeing a sign that said they now accepted Bitcoin. Perhaps there would be a brief summary about what Bitcoin is, how you get it, why you should use it. Little moves like that could go a long way to educating people. Just the idea of Bitcoin becoming more accessible and accepted at other locations across the world would do a lot to further people's comfort with it.

The fight for the future of Bitcoin is a public relations one and it has much to do with people's fears about it. Not only are they in the dark about how and why to use Bitcoin, they are positive that it poses a major security risk. Isn't Bitcoin only used by hackers, they think. That is what the media and so many lawmakers say. But the truth is that Bitcoin is not only used by hackers. Are there hackers looking to steal Bitcoins and break into online exchanges? Yes, unfortunately there are. But how many times have major banks, credit card companies, and other corporations been the victims of major hacks that have exposed millions of customers and billions of dollars? Sadly, hacking and online criminals are a common part of the world today. Unless you want to store all of your money, fiat and digital, under your mattress at home then you cannot completely avoid the risks that come with using any sort of financial system.

The truth is that the blockchain technology that powers Bitcoin is incredibly safe and used accountability and the latest cutting-edge techniques to keep everything reliable and secure. There should be more education about that, told in the simplest terms possible. If people truly grasped just what blockchain did and how it makes hacking and steal Bitcoin next to impossible, they would understand that the security risks they are told about are not nearly as prevalent as people think. Is Bitcoin 100% safe with no risk of exposure or danger? No, it isn't but even the most diehard Bitcoin supporter doesn't say that it is. However, it is safer than most other currencies and it is far, far safer than some naysayers claim it to be.

Another reason people are afraid of Bitcoin is because they are told that only criminals use it and it is some sort of front for human trafficking, tax evasion, and all sorts of nefarious activities. No one wants to be a part of something criminal, no one wants to be involved in something that could get them in legal problems or create any sort of jeopardy for their livelihood. There is no easier way to scare someone into avoiding something than by telling them they are about to break the law.

The truth is that Bitcoin and cryptocurrency have been used by people who are doing something illegal or immoral, there is no doubt about that. Of course, the same could be said about credit cards, or the dollar, or the yen, or any number of currencies. Criminals use any sort of payment method they can get their hands on. It is not the type of payment that creates crime, it is the criminal. Cryptocurrency is not an illegal form of currency, it does not encourage people to do something illegal. It is not somehow easier to do illegal things because of cryptocurrency.

The fact of the matter is that using a cryptocurrency is an anonymous act, it is impossible to track who is spending the money. The blockchain does not give an identity to those spending Bitcoin. It is reasonable to think that some people would be attracted to that anonymity, the ability to stay totally secret during a transaction. That would welcome people to sell drugs or traffic humans or do some sort of other awful things, right? If there was no way for the police to find out who they were, why wouldn't they use Bitcoin?

But the blockchain technology is still a good way to keep tabs on people, even though it doesn't reveal someone's true name. It does give great details about the amount of money spent and other specifics. All of those things could be used in a court of law. So, Bitcoin isn't as friendly to criminals as some might think.

The idea that Bitcoin is used only by those breaking the law has been a rumor since it was created. It isn't true, it never has been true, it has been said by those who don't want Bitcoin to be successful and are trying to make all sorts of claims to stop people from showing interest in it. They say that Bitcoin is only used by those breaking the law, but they ignore the fact that the vast, vast majority of people partaking in Bitcoin transactions are doing so legally and ethically. The people claiming that Bitcoin is only used by lawbreakers also conveniently ignore the fact that criminals use all sorts of currencies. If you truly do not want to use a form of payment that is also used by those breaking the law, you might as well avoid all forms of currencies.

The growing fears about Bitcoin and cryptocurrency as a whole has been one of the largest hurdles that crypto proponents need to battle against. For multiple reasons, Bitcoin seems more suspicious than just about any other form of payment. This is partly because it is so new, it is also because it is a digital-only currency and there are still great suspicions about the internet even though it has been around for decades now. The other major reason people are still fearful about Bitcoin and cryptocurrency is because there are some pushing these fearful ideas for their own gains. Misinformation is a great way to keep the growth of Bitcoin stalled and there are some people who have that as their main goal.

In Conclusion

Bitcoin has come a long way from its creation back in 2008. It started with nearly no value and no recognition across the country or the world. Today, it is a household entity that mostly everyone has some knowledge about.

But the truth remains that not enough people truly understand Bitcoin. The truth remains that many people think that it is a danger to them and their banks and their futures. Many people have fallen in line with politicians and people in power who are dead-set on stopping Bitcoin in its tracks. This has created an environment that is holding Bitcoin back from its full potential, even as it has made great leaps and bounds over the last few years.

Bitcoin has reached new highs that no one could have dreamed of when it first launched. Thanks to the horrific COVID-19 pandemic, the cryptocurrency has shown more of its potential and has proven why the idea of a digital currency isn't a bad thing. It has also shown why an interconnected global economy can end in ruins and can pose many great dangers that people can avoid if they rely on cryptocurrency.

For all of its progress, Bitcoin still has a long way to go. It does have some hidden dangers and controversies that need to be addressed and it needs to find a way to prosper in states that are run by officials who have no interest in supporting it. Now more than ever, Bitcoin needs allies to fight for it and plainly and honestly state its case and prove its worth. Even though billions and billions of dollars have been made thanks to Bitcoin, the battle for its future is not done. Not by a long shot.

4

HOW & WHEN TO BUY BITCOIN

You can learn a lot about Bitcoin. You can study the history, know the ins and outs, get a mental handle on the marketplace and how it works and why it operates the way it does. You can do all of these things and be something of a Bitcoin expert. But you don't really know Bitcoin, you can't really say that you're really acquainted with Bitcoin until you have some skin in the game and actually try to invest in the Bitcoin market.

Investing, buying, trading, selling. These are all terms you have likely heard a million times, especially if you have ever invested in any other market, like the stock market. These are terms that seem easy to understand. In a sense, they are. But they are more complicated than their base description leads on. There is a lot of subtlety and nuance to each of those words and they all must be understood before you can really make a name for yourself in the Bitcoin market. Once you have a decent grasp of those concepts, you can move forward with becoming an investor.

But it isn't as easy as walking into a local brokerage firm and telling them you want to invest in Bitcoin. Bitcoin is a market that works outside of current monetary systems. It is a market that is independent, volatile, wildly unique, and sometimes daunting. There are multiple ways to dip your toes into the waters of Bitcoin and when you do, you are mostly on your own. There will not be a financial advisor to hold your hand and guide you through the journey. That is why it is doubly important that you know what you are doing before you do it. That is why it is so vital that you understand not just how to invest but why to invest. You should truly comprehend what to be looking for in the Bitcoin market. You need to know how Bitcoin moves and reacts to changes in the world and other markets. You need to also get comfortable with other cryptocurrencies.

That's right, there is far more than just Bitcoin you can buy, trade, sell, and invest in. Bitcoin was the first coin in the system, the biggest name in the game. Bitcoin remains the most powerful and popular form of cryptocurrency in the world and is unrivaled in terms of its size and fame. But it is no longer alone. Over the years, the world has seen an introduction of what are referred to as altcoins. What is an altcoin? It is an alternative coin and is a form of cryptocurrency that isn't Bitcoin. No altcoin comes close to the popularity or worth of Bitcoin, they are all considered the little brothers and sister of Bitcoin. However, there are now hundreds and hundreds of altcoins to buy and sell and trade. These coins are also able to be bought on online exchanges and add to a robust market of cryptocurrency. They allow more options for you when you are looking to invest. Do you want to find a new altcoin that may soon become popular and make you a lot of money? Now you can do that, thanks to the many altcoin options in the marketplace. Even fans of Bitcoin will invest in other coins too. Altcoins have become a wonderfully important part of the cryptocurrency industry. They add a lot of value to people's pocketbooks, and they allow options for investors.

Studying altcoins, understanding the market, being well acquainted with Bitcoin - all of these are important steps before you begin investing, buying, trading, and selling in the crypto world. Once you feel comfortable with all of these aspects, buying into the crypto market will feel much easier. It will be second nature and you will soon be a natural.

HOW TO BUY BITCOIN

Before you can buy Bitcoin, or any altcoin, you need to make a big decision: which online exchange are you going to sign up for.

This is a necessary and fundamental step that needs to be finished in order to do anything with cryptocurrency. Online exchanges are the only way to buy yourself a piece of the crypto pie. Thankfully, it is now easy to sign up for and use an online exchange.

Online exchanges were first started in the early days of Bitcoin and were originally created primarily as a way to understand how much worth there was in Bitcoin. It is wild to think about the fact that when it was first created, people didn't know the value of Bitcoin. Was it worth a penny, a dollar, hundreds, or millions? It was all left up in the air, even those who were radically in favor of Bitcoin and what it stood for didn't quite know how much value it had.

The online exchanges was the most democratic and honest way for the marketplace to decide how much Bitcoin was valued at. Let the people choose, that was the concept. If they wanted to buy in for a Bitcoin and spend $100 to get one, that would be the worth. If they wanted to spend a whole lot more or a whole lot less, that would be the worth. It was the best way to dictate just how much Bitcoin cost and it created an entire marketplace ecosystem that would live, thrive, and die on its own, without the oversight of anybody but the people who used it.

Since then, online exchanges have become a major part of buying into Bitcoin. They are they who hold the keys to the kingdom. To use one, you need to sign up and submit your banking information to make your first investment. From there, you will be able to buy your first form of cryptocurrency, whether it be Bitcoin or any other sort of altcoin.

You should remember this about altcoins, however. Not all online exchanges allow you to buy into them. While most if not all online exchanges will provide you with the ability to buy Bitcoin, they do not all allow you the choice to invest in altcoin. However, some of the exchanges allow you to trade Bitcoin for altcoin. Therefore, if you sign up for an exchange that doesn't allow you to buy altcoin, you may be able to buy Bitcoin and then trade that Bitcoin for your altcoin. There are usually workarounds if the site you choose doesn't initially allow you to find the altcoin of your choice.

Once you have access to and purchase your first Bitcoin or cryptocurrency, you should take a moment to congratulate yourself. You are now officially a part of one of the most exciting and promising markets in the world. There is great potential ahead of you, chances for great gains. There are dangers too, of course. So, you need to be cautious and careful moving forward. The easiest way to lose your money and the fastest way to get burnt out on Bitcoin is to invest wildly and not be smart about your moves.

What To Remember Before Buying Bitcoin

As you have already suspected, buying Bitcoin is unlike buying anything else in any other market. It has great potential, great risk, and is maintained inside a system that is self-regulated and cutting-edge. Different is a word people use often when talking about buying Bitcoin and that is a great way to describe it. Buying Bitcoin is different, but sometimes its difference is what makes it so special.

However, before you make the dive and start buying - then trading - Bitcoin, you need to know a few basics. If you really want to be a part of this growing and exciting field of investors, you have to be on the same page as them. Anyone who invests in any product or company with great potential and promise will tell you that you need to know the lay of the land before you make your first buy, you need to know what you are getting yourself into. If you think it is as easy as buying Bitcoin and then watching your fortune grow, you are sadly mistaken. No, there is much that needs to be known before your first buy-in. The more you know, the stronger your future - and wallet - will be.

What are a few things that all Bitcoin experts will tell you to keep in mind before you make a purchase? Here are the biggest tips to keep in mind as you venture further into the world of cryptocurrency.

Bitcoin is decentralized. This is a point that gets brought up often, it is one of the most attractive features of Bitcoin to many new investors. Bitcoin is a completely decentralized system. That means that unlike many other traditional currencies Bitcoin does not have a central bank or government controlling it. Its supply and system runs on its own, independent of the sort of oversight that you see when it comes to other markets and currencies. That means that there is something of a Wild West feel to the world of cryptocurrency. It feels like there are no rules but that isn't the truth. In fact, just the opposite is true: Bitcoin and all cryptocurrency trading is greatly regulated - just not by the usual suspects. It isn't a bank or government that keeps everything and everyone in line. Instead, it is the people who run the show when it comes to Bitcoin.

Bitcoin is (mostly) anonymous. Unlike writing a check or using your debit card to make a transaction in person, using Bitcoin is a mostly anonymous process. Your identity is not known when you are paying with Bitcoin. It is mostly anonymous because you and your purchases aren't completely unknown to the system. Instead, your transactions are tied to a digital wallet with a specific ID number that doesn’t have your personal information. This is an important aspect of making a purchase with Bitcoin. This highly complex and lengthy code is proof of purchase and is necessary because it becomes part of the blockchain. Without this code, there is no evidence of your transaction. This is definitive proof that your digital currency traded hands and was given and accepted above board. It also keeps things safe too. While it is technically possible for hackers or the federal government to figure out who is who and which digital ID number correlates to a buyer, it is unlikely and would require more work than is almost humanly possible. Bitcoin is essentially an anonymous form of currency, and your purchases and transactions will remain secret unless you want to share them.

Bitcoin is completely digital. Some people believe that they will decide to invest in Bitcoin, put their money down to buy some and then receive a hard copy of their investment in the mail. That isn’t going to happen, not now and not ever. Bitcoin is a completely digital currency. Are there Bitcoin ATMs that exist in the real world? Yes, there are. However, there are no physical Bitcoins or Bitcoin bills. You are not going to put this currency in your pocket or place it on a store counter like you would other currencies, it exists purely online in a complicated and safe system of computers spread all over the globe. Bitcoin does not work like typical, well-known currencies. Instead, all of your Bitcoin worth is stored online, a digital currency that you will keep tabs on and read about but never physically hold. It is vastly different from all other forms of currency and that takes some adjustment for people who want and desire the tactile and tangible feel of money in their hands.

Bitcoin isn’t just for buying. Most people get involved with Bitcoin so they can buy and trade it like they would commodities on many different markets, like stocks. However, the Bitcoin market is a bit different than the stock market because you are able to do more than simply buy and trade cryptocurrency. You are also able to “mine” it to make more money. What is mining? We will get into that more later, but the basics are that Bitcoin mining is a process that allows you or your computer to run through a series of complicated codes to encrypt and unlock the next step in the blockchain. If you do that, you are rewarded with your very own Bitcoin which is worth an enormous sum. The process is long and not for the faint of heart, but it is one of the reasons some people begin their journey with Bitcoin.

Bitcoin is volatile. Here is something that you would be smart to remind yourself of often. This is perhaps one of the biggest shocks to people when they begin to invest in Bitcoin. They are not ready for the extreme ups and downs associated with the market. It can be surprising and, frankly, shocking. For some, it's enough to scare them away from putting their money into Bitcoin altogether. One of the things you will hear again and again is how wildly the Bitcoin market fluctuates. There is no way around that, that is the honest truth. If you are looking for a stable market, one with few surprises, you might want to look somewhere else but Bitcoin's instability is one of its most reliable aspects. Since its inception, Bitcoin has always had vicious swings in value and that has scared away many investors looking to get into the game. If you are aware and comfortable with that, you can move forward investing in Bitcoin knowing that you will stick through it whether things are thick or thin. But if you scare easily and do not want to see the value of your investment rise and plummet at seemingly random times, Bitcoin may end up being too stressful for you.

Those are just a few things to remember and to keep in the back of your mind at all times. There is much more to learn about all forms of cryptocurrency, including Bitcoin. But with these basic concepts and ideas in mind, you can make the smart moves needed when you are trying to find success in the cryptocurrency market. Knowledge is power, especially when investing.

But none of this matters until you have Bitcoin in your (digital) pocket. So, the next question, perhaps one of the biggest ones, is related to where to buy your Bitcoin. Where are you going to go to make your first purchase and enter the volatile but promising world of Bitcoin investing. As you can imagine, you are not allowed to just walk into a bank or brokerage firm and invest like you would with other markets. No, getting into the Bitcoin market is a bit more complicated - but also a bit more streamlined at the same time.

Like all things Bitcoin, the easiest way to invest involves the internet.

THE DIFFERENT ONLINE EXCHANGES

Online exchanges are the best, easiest, most official, and safest way to make your first Bitcoin investment. In fact, to many it is the only way to invest. As mentioned, investors didn't always have a lot of choice when they were deciding upon an online exchange. In the early days of Bitcoin, you really only had one or two options. That availability has opened up considerably and now you have many different avenues you can go down when you are looking to make your first investment. More options are good news, but it is important you find the online exchange that is right for you.

The most important thing to remember about all of these online exchanges is that they are all safe and value your time, money, safety, and peace of mind. The online exchange market has ballooned into an enormous and ever-growing industry, and they all know you now have many choices to pick from. They want to offer you something that their competitors cannot. But one thing they all want to give you is an experience that makes Bitcoin investing less frightening. So many people are afraid to start their investing journey because they feel that it is too overwhelming, they don't know where to start. The online exchanges listed want to add a lot of ease to the experience of investing, buying, and trading cryptocurrencies.

Whichever online exchange you end up with, it is important that before you sign up you read up on all details about each site. Pay special attention to the exchange rates, the coins you can and cannot invest in, the selection of altcoins, and more. You will likely spend a large amount of time on the exchange you set upon, so you need to find one you are comfortable with.

Coinbase

If we are talking about online exchanges, it is best we start with the biggest name in the game. Here is Coinbase, the most popular online cryptocurrency exchange in the world. It is definitely the most widely-known exchange around and is the go-to for many people, the gold standard of cryptocurrency exchanges. For both newcomers and long-term investors, Coinbase has set the bar high for its competitors.

So, what is it about Coinbase that attracts so many? What makes this online exchange so special and popular among all different types of investors? The site has proven itself as a safe and easy place to start your Bitcoin investing career. It only charges fees of $.99 to $2.99 depending on the value of your purchase. It also has a wide variety of coins to choose from, including some of the best altcoins on the market. Remember, the ability to buy altcoin is one of the most attractive features for some people looking to make their name in the world of cryptocurrency.

Here is one of the most enticing things about this online exchange: Coinbase offers insured wallets for investors. That means if something happens to your investment, Coinbase has you covered. That coverage doesn’t apply to all sorts of mishaps, of course. Restrictions definitely apply. But it is a sign that Coinbase takes all forms of crypto - and their customers’ satisfaction and safety - seriously. An insured wallet is a big step in making people feel much more comfortable buying into the world of cryptocurrency without fear of losing all of the money they put in.

Coinbase also has something called Coinbase Pro, an upgraded version of their system that has a different fee structure and more options when it comes to data and personalization. Coinbase Pro is just another reason why this site has become so popular and is definitely here to stay. They repeatedly show that they take Bitcoin investing seriously and continually upgrade the customer experience to reflect that.

eToro

eToro is another incredibly popular cryptocurrency online exchange that has millions of clients and is found in over 100 countries. Based in Israel, eToro first started by only offering Bitcoin trading but eventually opened up to accept more forms of crypto. It now has plenty of options for the most seasoned pro or a brand-new person who is just figuring out the world of crypto.

What are the biggest benefits of this site and how has it stood out among its competitors? Firstly, eToro is an easy-to-use site that has a lot of helpful features that make investing and trading painless and simple to understand. Those are two of the biggest upsides to eToro because many people have difficulty navigating through investing in crypto. eToro makes it easy as can be and will leave you feeling like you really have a deep and solid understanding of things. eToro also has innovative and unique ideas like a copy-trading technique. What is copy-trading? That allows you to literally replicate the position of other traders in the network. That may not seem very exciting or original, but it is quite helpful if you know other people on the site who have found the success you are looking for. Why not just copy their positions? There is nothing wrong with that! If you become a fan of a certain investor or want to mimic their success, the copy-trading feature will let you do just that. It is as easy as copying and pasting on a computer - only this could result in you having a lot more money.

eToro allows you to try your hand at more than just cryptocurrency. You can also buy and trade stocks, currencies, commodities, and more. You will notice that a lot of online exchanges are starting to allow their clients to buy other commodities as a way to integrate Bitcoin with stocks and give them a true sense of normalcy.

Binance

Binance was founded in 2017. Since then, it has built an impressive and loyal following of Bitcoin and cryptocurrency investors who have found great success with this online exchange and sing its praises.
