Once that miner encrypts that chunk of information or block, they can share it across the internet with other miners. Once it is verifying, it is added to the existing chain. As for the computer and the user who solved the puzzle, they are rewarded with their very own Bitcoins.

That’s right, mining rewards you with Bitcoins. The number of coins you are given has changed over time. When mining was first started back in 2009, a successful miner would be rewarded with a whopping 50 coins for every block they verified and encrypted.

Since then, the reward total has been halved three times and as of May 2020, you will be given 6.25 Bitcoins if you successfully mine a block. Considering the price of Bitcoin is about $17,000 per Bitcoin, you can make hundreds of thousands of dollars for completing a block.

As you can imagine, a pay-out that high won’t be rewarded unless the work is hard. Mining is hard. Mining is very hard. It requires a pretty powerful computing system, a lot of time and electricity, and incredible dedication. But there are many chances to make money since the Bitcoin network aims to have one block produced every 10 minutes. However, the more people and computers competing, the less of a chance you have to be the one to win.

This is a complicated process but what it comes down to is this: crypto mining is an attempt from computers to guess a number. Through powerful computing power, this number is guessed again and again until someone gets it right. This requires a computer that will compute quickly again and again in an attempt to crack the code. The reward is massive but so is the workload.

Some people have sunk a lot of time and energy into data mining. It is not unheard of for people to spend hundreds if not thousands of dollars to create computer systems solely used for mining. It is also not unheard of for multiple people to come together and work as one unit, using a massive system of computers that are trying to mine and encrypt and succeed. That makes a lot of sense because when the pay-out is hundreds of thousands of dollars, you can certainly stand to split it.

You could make a boatload of money if you are a crypto miner and you hit it big. The prize is worth thousands of dollars. However, it comes with a major risk too. You could spend all that money and devote all that time and end up with nothing at all. There is no sure thing when you are a miner, you are not guaranteed to ever hit it big even if you sink thousands of dollars into it.

Are you an expert with computers and have a lot of time and money to spend? If so, crypto mining might be right for you. Otherwise, you may want to spend your energy on investing in crypto rather than encrypting and cracking it. You stand a much better chance at making money that way.

SO YOU WANT TO BUILD A MINING RIG

If you have decided that you are going to take a chance and spend the time and money to build your very own cryptocurrency mining computer there is a lot to do, and you need to get started as soon as possible. You have looked at the information, done the research, and determined that you could make a pretty penny if you have your own computer doing the work for you.

It sure is a lot of work but it can and will pay off if done correctly. You stand to earn a huge profit if you have a mining rig at home, digging away at code and unlocking and encrypting blockchain.

But this isn’t like buying a normal computer. You cannot walk into any retail or computer store and just tell them that you want to buy a mining rig. No, this takes a lot more work and you definitely need to know what you are doing if you want to earn your Bitcoin reward.

It is time to figure out just what it takes to buy and build your mining computer. Great profit might await you if you follow these steps, have patience, and do not expect immediate success overnight.

But before we get into what you need and the steps you will have to take, you need to be reminded again that you could make a lot of money by crypto mining, but it is no sure thing. There is a chance that you might not ever make a dime off of mining. Do not walk into the world of crypto mining expecting a windfall of cash. Not now, maybe not ever. If it pays out, it pays out well. But there is no promise that it ever will pay out.

TYPES OF MINING RIGS

What is a cryptocurrency mining rig? Basically, it is a computer. But it is not a computer like the one you have at home. No, a mining rig is a computer that has a whole lot of graphic cards but lacks a monitor. Inside the computer case are GPU cards, a motherboard, a cooling system and more. It is referred to as a rig instead of a computer because it is so unlike the computers that most people are used to.

And how does it work? The rig is hooked up to the internet, of course, and the blockchain network that it needs. The blockchain network works on its own 24 hours a day and is powered by the graphic cards in the computers connected to the network.

Mining rigs often work on their own once they get up and running. They take up more space than a normal computer because of some of the protruding items included in them so people often place mining rigs in garages or places that do not stick out too much. While you can hook a monitor up to a mining rig and use it as a regular computer at times, most people only use them to mine. Remember, mining can be a full-time job and can work 24 hours a day.

What are the types of mining rigs you can make? There are three vastly different choices when you are building your rig.

ASIC

First up is the ASIC rig. ASIC stands for Application-Specific Integrated Circuits. These are specially made devices created with one sole purpose in mind. This is the strongest, most powerful choice to go with when you are wanting to start a career in crypto mining. They are more than just computers, that is important to keep in mind. An ASIC rig is a tool made for one thing and one thing only. In this case, that one thing is mining. This is a dedicated piece of machinery built to get the job done.

Creating or buying an ASIC is hard because they are so good at what they do. Their impressive performance skills means that a lot of people looking to get into mining want an ASIC. That means they are hard to come by. While eventually getting your hands on an ASIC is worth it and probably your best bet at a successful career in mining, it is going to take a lot of work to find one. You will have to rely on third-party sellers or a very, very, very savvy tech friend who can make one for you. Remember, this won’t resemble the sort of personal computer you use at home to surf the web or play video games on. This is a completely different beast.

There is some controversy surrounding ASIC builds too. These incredibly powerful devices have been accused of toying and messing with other mining devices on the network and that has caused them to be subject to a lot of disdain and finger-wagging. You need to keep that in mind when you create or buy an ASIC, especially if you care about the well-being of other miners on the network. Still, this is the most robust and impressive computer on the market when it comes to mining.

GPU

The GPU approach to mining is a great way to go if you want to mine and, say, watch Netflix in your spare time or play games with your friends online. They can be more than just a mining rig, unlike an ASIC build. That makes them exceedingly popular and also rather easy to create and maintain.

A GPU rig is a favorite for someone looking to build their own computer. While it is still a difficult task and requires that you have a good deal of computer knowledge, it isn’t impossible and isn’t nearly as daunting as even shopping for an ASIC.

There are two types of GPU rigs: simple and dedicated miners. You can guess what a dedicated miner is. That is a GPU computer that is made to only mine. This is the right path to take if you are looking to dive headfirst into crypto mining and want to devote a lot of time to unlocking and encrypting the blockchain.

But what if you want to share your computer with the others in your house? What if you are an avid World of Warcraft player or use your computer for homework? Then you will want the simple miner, the second type of GPU computer. This is a device that you can use to mine and do other typical things that a personal computer does. The ability to choose between these two options is a great incentive for people looking to try their hands at mining.

What about downsides? There is a major one when it comes to buying or making a GPU computer. That is the price. Creating or buying one of these devices can be quite expensive. That is because a GPU heavily relies on a graphic card to function, unlike the ASIC model. Graphic cards can get very pricy, especially if you are looking for a high-end one that will really get the job done.

It isn’t just the graphic cards that ends up impacting your wallet. Your GPU will need a lot of bells and whistles, a few add-ons to keep it running in top form. You will need cooling devices and a strong electricity system. And if your GPU hits a bump in the road and needs repairs, you will have to spend a lot of money to get that done effectively.

Despite its setbacks and challenges, the GPU is a wonderful way to start your mining career and can serve more than one purpose. That makes the hefty price tag feel a bit more worth it.

CPU

CPU mining is the third and, at a time, the most popular way to mine. Why is CPU mining so prevalent in the crypto community? Honestly, because it is an incredibly simple way to mine. It is also rather inexpensive. But when we say it is inexpensive, we mean building the device itself doesn’t cost as much as alternatives. Upkeep and other factors of CPU mining do cost a lot and can be a huge headache as you move further in your mining career.

When we speak of the simplicity of CPU mining, we are talking about the quick set-up required. If you have a suitable computer, you can start crypto mining right now. Using the CPU method, you can just download an app on your CPU device and get to mining almost immediately. This is a very enticing and promising reason for many and why scores of people tend to start CPU mining over buying or creating a GPU or ASIC rig. CPU mining takes way less effort and the process to get started doesn’t take much time at all.

But CPU mining has its drawbacks, and they are related to pricing. While the creation of a CPU rig isn’t very expensive (in fact, you might already have a CPU rig and not even know it) the upkeep prices are frustrating and high. For one, using a CPU rig is going to make your electric bills skyrocket. One of the biggest complaints about mining is the cost of electricity once you get started and, sure enough, a CPU rig is going to hog up a lot of your power and put a strain on your local grid. This isn’t nearly as simple as a typical computer, you are not simply flipping a switch and turning on a monitor. The system and process that a CPU rig goes through to mine is long and complicated and lengthy too. It will constantly be chugging along and sucking up a lot of power. You will see the price on your monthly statement shoot up after little time at all. That alone is enough reason to scare a lot of people away.

But the electricity bill isn’t the only thing that is going to hurt your wallet. No, the price of cooling your system and maintaining it costs just as much as the power running your rig. These machines need to be tended to often and a lot can go wrong with them. When something does go awry, you are going to step in and put down a pretty penny to get it fixed. Altogether, these rigs are going to suck a lot of money out of your bank account. It will all be worth it if you successfully mine a block and get the payout from it. If not, you are going to be spending a lot of money with very little reward.

These three types of rigs all have their own benefits and their own downsides too. The most common drawback for all three models is the price. Whether it be with maintenance, repairs, electricity, or something else, mining on one of these computers sure does cost a lot of money. There is a lot that can go wrong and each misstep is going to drain your funds. Maintaining one of these rigs is almost more expensive than having a small child.

You need to look at buying and running a mining rig like it is a business. It is going to cost a lot of money, yes, but so does successfully owning a company. You are going to need to invest great sums and hope that you are rewarded at the end of the process with something that pays you back. We have spoken of just how much money you can make with mining, you could generate a fortune if you find luck in the field. If you are thinking of mining as a hobby, it is going to be a ridiculously expensive one. But if you are approaching mining like it is a career or business, the hefty price tag will be a bit more palatable.

HOW MUCH WILL A MINING RIG COST?

We have spoken a lot about how expensive crypto mining is. We have made no secret of the fact that it is going to cost you a pretty penny and may not pay out for a long time, if ever.

But how much money are we talking about? Just how hefty is the price tag related to crypto mining? How much money will it set you back? Is it really worth it?

The short answer is no, it probably isn’t worth it for most people. The long answer, however, is that it could be worth it if you do it the right way and are comfortable spending a lot of cash. This isn’t some hobby you pick up at your convenience, it is something you will truly have to dedicate a lot of time and energy to.

When it comes to building your computer, you are going to be putting up a large chunk of change to create a rig that can do the job right. The graphic card needed for a CPU mining rig is at least $700. Some cost quite a bit more than that too, it depends on what you are comfortable using. Remember, if you cut corners and go cheap with any part of your rig it will cost you in the long run and will most definitely affect your computer’s ability to successfully mine. It is hard to spend money like this, but it is even harder to do all this work for absolutely nothing while other computers race by you in terms of power.

The graphic card alone will hit your bank account hard. From there, the other components aren’t much cheaper. There are examples of people building a crypto mining rig for around $3,000 but that is the bare minimum of what you can spend, and that sort of computer will not be very promising or deliver many chances for success.

On the other hand, some people have spent more than $10,000 on their rigs. That is more expensive than many cars. Even then, there is no guarantee for success. You can spend thousands upon thousands of dollars on a rig and still walk away empty-handed. Are your chances improved based on how much money you spend? Yes, they are. But do not think that spending $10,000 will automatically mean you will be raking in the Bitcoin.

The price of the computer isn’t the only thing you will consider when getting into mining. Remember the electricity you will be using to mine. The total there can add up and add up fast and can send some people straight into bankruptcy court.

We all have electricity bills and are quite familiar with the average price of them. You know that during the height of winter and summer, your power bill is going to skyrocket because of the consumption needed to keep you and your family warm or cool. But even the highest bill you’ve come across pales in comparison to what you will experience if you start crypto mining at home.

Research has shown that the cheapest electricity cost for mining a single Bitcoin is more than $3,000. That is an astronomically high number and it’s on the lower end of the spectrum. You will have to spend about $3,000 to mine a single coin in the cheapest states in the nation. If you happen to live in a state with higher power prices, you could be dropping $6,000 to mine your Bitcoin. That quote, on top of the price of creating and maintaining your rig, is really hard to swallow. It is especially hard to accept if you aren’t getting anything in return for all that cash spent. At that rate, it could take weeks or months to get a return on your investment and that is only if your rig works perfectly and is lucky enough to mine a coin.

You can see the risk and reward associated with crypto mining when it comes to how much you will spend. The cost of building and maintaining your rig alone is enough to scare many people away from attempting to make money this way. But when you remember the value of your rewarded Bitcoin, it sounds more promising. Just remember that like all things related to cryptocurrency, your success isn’t guaranteed and it is very unlikely that it will happen quickly, if at all.

IS MINING LEGAL?

Crypto mining, like cryptocurrency as a whole, isn’t legal all over the globe. While most nations allow crypto like Bitcoin and other altcoins, some countries do not allow it. If you live in places such as Egypt, Ecuador, Nepal, Pakistan, and Morocco, you will not be able to purchase or trade cryptocurrency and you will definitely not be able to mine.

However, in other countries you can buy and mine freely. That doesn’t mean it will always be that way though. As cryptocurrency becomes more popular and disrupts the traditional monetary system, more and more people in power like politicians have come forward pushing back against it and hoping to limit or outright restrict it. If that happens in the country you live in, your ability to mine as well as buy crypto will become a thing of the past and could put you in serious legal jeopardy.

This is one of the other risks of crypto mining. The crypto landscape is changing all the time, for better and for worse. It is not only the value of coins that remains volatile. The opinion and future outlook for cryptocurrency also always feel in flux. There is a chance that in the months and years ahead, some nation will successfully ban crypto and the ability to buy, trade, and mine coins.

This unpredictably is a huge drawback to mining. Imagine spending thousands upon thousands of dollars on mining. Imagine actually starting to make a profit off of it. Now imagine it becomes banned in your region and you are now effectively breaking the law. All of that money, all of that time, all of that energy - down the drain. That is the risk you run by participating in an industry that is still seen as new and different and a bit suspicious by those in power.

It is important to note that if you live in a country that hasn’t limited or banned cryptocurrency then you are doing nothing wrong by crypto mining. You are free to do so and should feel no shame about doing so. But you do need to consider the political and economic environment you live in. If there is a chance that cryptocurrency could be banned where you live, you should proceed with caution before you start to put time and money into becoming a crypto miner.

IN CONCLUSION

As we have discussed, crypto mining is complicated. In fact, complicated doesn't rightfully sum up just how complex and hard it is. Some people feel that crypto mining might be as easy as playing a video game. Some people assume that with crypto mining, all you need to do is turn on your computer, let it start mining, and leave it alone until it is practically printing money.

That isn't the case. Crypto mining is not an easy affair. It requires a lot of knowledge about computing, Bitcoin, and all things technical. It also requires a lot of money, forethought, and electricity. Crypto mining is way harder than people think it would be.

But it can pay off in some major, major ways. Cryptocurrency is essential, a part of the cryptocurrency puzzle that needs to happen because it keeps the Bitcoin ecosystem alive and keeps the entire marketplace moving. Bitcoin needs crypto mining to survive and crypto mining needs dedicated and knowledgeable people to work.

Crypto mining is proof that the Bitcoin system is more than meets the eye and has a lot of machinery working right below the surface. It is also proof that the system is still as it always has been: run by the people and for the people.

THE FUTURE OF CRYPTOCURRENCY AND BITCOIN

When Bitcoin began in the late 2000s, few people believed it was capable of the goals it has achieved. Many people knew that Bitcoin had promise but they didn't know if the world and its inhabitants were ready for it.

Others were confident that they were. And why shouldn't they be? Why shouldn't the global economy be prepared for a digital currency that only exists online? Why shouldn't the people be prepared and willing to break away from a system that has neglected them and led so many to ruin? Why couldn't Bitcoin break the cycle and change the status quo and start something entirely new, entirely modern, and entirely safe and efficient?

The thing about Bitcoin is that it is currency with a purpose and that has driven it from the very beginning. Those who truly believe in Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency believe in it for reasons that are far deeper and more important than financial value. People know that Bitcoin is a complete rejection of a system that has grown stale and corrupt. They also know that Bitcoin has the chance to level the playing field, help businesses and consumers alike, and can truly add equality and fairness into a massive system that hasn't had either for so long.

But even with all that great promise, there are questions about the longevity of Bitcoin. What lies ahead for the prized cryptocurrency? What successes and roadblocks await it? From the very beginning, some have been asking the same question: is Bitcoin here to stay?

THE DANGERS AHEAD FOR BITCOIN

Bitcoin has found massive success repeatedly since its creation. In fact, it has earned so much worth and value for those who invest that it has become one of the hottest commodities even for seasoned traders who live and breathe on Wall Street. Bitcoin has reached some heights that few saw coming and has done it all from humble beginnings. When Bitcoin first launched, an entire coin was worth less than a penny. Now it is worth thousands upon thousands of dollars.

With such great climbs in worth, you would think there is no chance of anything getting in the way of Bitcoin's amazing future. You would be wrong.

What stands in the way of Bitcoin is people. It is a currency that is driven by people, led by people, for the people. However, its only enemy is also people. It is true that if Bitcoin falls apart, it will be because of people who want it to fail.

There are some in power who do not like that Bitcoin aims to disrupt the status quo and create more equality across the financial system. These people will do whatever they can to hold onto their power and make sure that Bitcoin doesn't allow others to climb the ranks of success and stand higher on the ladder of financial freedom.

Remember, money is power. Those with great power almost always have great sums of money too. The money they hold isn't digital currency, either. It is traditional, fiat currency and Bitcoin stands a chance to devalue the worth of that fiat currency. That change just cannot stand with some. They cannot allow that to happen. They will fight as hard as they can to make sure it does not happen.

How will they block the growing success of Bitcoin? By using their own power of the power of those closest to them to stop Bitcoin in its tracks. All across the world, the rich and powerful have grown cozy and comfortable with many politicians and governmental officials who can do much damage to Bitcoin and its strength. If push came to shove, many of those officials would look out for themselves and their allies and would push forward legislation or restricting rules that would greatly harm Bitcoin. You have seen cases of this already in certain states in America and certain countries all over the world. Make no mistake, there are people looking to outright ban and eliminate Bitcoin simply because its success stands at odds with their own.

People could lead to the downfall of Bitcoin for another reason: ignorance. If not enough people know of the value, worth, and power of Bitcoin and how it can help everyday citizens, then they will not fight to keep it around. Bitcoin is facing a public relations crisis. Too few people know what it does, how it works, why it can benefit them. Many are confused by Bitcoin or suspicious of it and its cause and purpose. These people could be some of the biggest proponents of Bitcoin and could go a long way to help it remain powerful and valuable. These people could help turn Bitcoin into the long-lasting and greatly beneficial currency that it should be. But as long as they don't know the great benefits of Bitcoin, they will not fight on its behalf.

Again, that is why it is so obvious that Bitcoin is facing a public relations crisis. Those who truly care for and understand Bitcoin need to make it a point to educate whoever they can and create a larger chorus of those singing the praises of cryptocurrency. There needs to be a mass educational program that is created at the grassroots with the sole purpose of informing any and everyone about Bitcoin and what it can do for everyone. If that is implemented and successfully carried out, it can go a long way to making Bitcoin stronger and more successful. The people need to be informed. Multiple instances have shown that the more they know about Bitcoin, the more they like it. Education is key.

THE POSSIBILITIES FOR THE FUTURE OF BITCOIN

Regardless of the issues standing in its way, the future of Bitcoin is bright. In fact, many would say that now is the time with the greatest potential for Bitcoin and all forms of cryptocurrency. The entire industry stands at a crossing in the road and the future is more promising than ever before. But what does the future of Bitcoin look like?

It is hard to tell exactly what is ahead for Bitcoin, that is one of the problems with such possibilities. Things could go in any number of directions. But most agree that the most potential lies in the realm of retail. If Bitcoin is able to make significant and constant gains among retailers, both online and brick-and-mortar, the next decade of cryptocurrency could be its biggest and most impressive.

Right now, the options are vast and much more than they used to be but there is still a lot of room for growth. Fans of Bitcoin know that they need to make in-roads with some big companies to help further the growth of Bitcoin.

Imagine if you are able to walk into some of the biggest companies in the world - places like Target or Walmart - and pay with your Bitcoin wallet. Imagine how many possibilities that would open up. There wouldn't be possibilities for only you the consumer, but also the business that is accepting this new form of currency. It really is a win-win situation for everyone involved.

Yet, there is resistance among many large companies who aren't yet sold on the idea of Bitcoin in their stores. Why? Some are hesitant because they don't see the value in cryptocurrency. Others are hesitant because they are planning to possibly launch their own cryptocurrency in the future. In fact, some have suggested that one of the reasons why Amazon, the biggest online retailer in the world, isn't accepting Bitcoin is because it is planning to launch its own form of digital currency in the next few years. If Amazon did implement such an idea, it would both prove the value of cryptocurrency and also damage the power of Bitcoin in some major ways.

Bitcoin has proven its value, it has long ago proven just how strong and powerful it can be. Now it needs to connect with regular, everyday consumers in ways it has failed to do so far. The people have always been the future of Bitcoin and that is still true.

Ever since its creation, people have always said that the future for Bitcoin is promising. They have always claimed that the best is yet to come and that the future has never been so exciting.

While Bitcoin has yet to truly break through in some of the ways that have been teased, the truth is that it has had an incredibly successful and exciting life cycle so far. It is also true that the best is yet to come. Bitcoin has shown repeatedly that it is a digital currency unlike any other and has all the ability to truly promise on its purpose: to create a revolutionary currency for the people and by the people, free of the restraints that had led to so many issues in the past. There have been some serious bumps in the road, potholes that have proven to be more difficult than expected. Yet, Bitcoin has remained steadfast and committed to its core foundation since the very beginning. The people supporting Bitcoin have also remained committed and driven to support the cause and idea behind Bitcoin. As more people learn about Bitcoin and are truly educated on its potential, the more fans it has. That is why, once again, the future of Bitcoin is so bright.

It first got its fair share of spotlight around 2016 after a Bitcoin halving. This drove the price of Bitcoin up and this generated a lot of interest. People got in on the action a little too late and end up getting shortchanged as the price for Bitcoin went down from there. Soon after, cryptocurrency faded into obscurity and has not been heard from outside the crypto circle since.

Interest renewed when Bitcoin underwent another halving in 2020. Its price continues to climb since then and people started to look. Thanks to the whole Covid pandemic that plagues the Earth, people are forced into the confines of their own homes. Most people lost their jobs, and they turned to alternative sources of income. Among them are cryptocurrency trading and investment.

At the same time, people are unhappy with how their governments are handling the pandemic, especially with how they allocated taxpayer’s money. Much of it goes into bailing out mega-corporations that do their best to minimize tax and care little about their employees. Instead of saving small businesses, some governments put money toward rescuing large corporations that can take the hit.

This, coupled with the Great Depression a long time ago, made people wary of the government. The way they see it is that their money they have been working hard for is not in their total control. They start to see how governments are favoring major financial institutions rather than the people who made everything possible in the first place.

And so, some people are looking for ways to gain back control over their own wealth. And as luck would have it, there is a solution within the cryptocurrency and its underlying blockchain technology.

Since 2016, cryptocurrency has seen significant development. It is surprising to see how much progress the people in the crypto circle can achieve in just a few years. With over a thousand cryptocurrencies to choose from and a vast range of use cases for blockchain technology, it is clear that cryptocurrencies are here to stay.

It is still a very young industry, but it is quickly gaining traction. Tesla started accepting Bitcoin as a payment method. PayPal is rolling out features that allow users to access cryptocurrencies on their platforms. Many merchants are now accepting cryptocurrency payments as well. People are starting to see the potential behind cryptocurrency and they are jumping on the train once more.

Some people are skeptical about the second coming of cryptocurrency, however. If the 2016 halving and subsequent crash is anything to go by, then what makes this halving any different? This time, large companies are getting into the industry. This is significant because their participation means that cryptocurrency stands a better chance of gaining universal recognition. In other words, cryptocurrency is here to stay.

Of course, you might decide to hang back and see how everything plays out first before you get into the game. But keep in mind that the people who bought Bitcoin when you need 10,000 of it to buy a pizza are now swimming in cash. There are a lot of opportunities to make a ton of profit for early adopters.

1

ANSWERING THE 7 WHS OF CRYPTOCURRENCIES

To start things off, we need to establish the groundwork by understanding what cryptocurrencies are and what you can do with them. We will go over the basics of cryptocurrencies in this chapter. Even if you had no idea of cryptocurrencies before, this chapter should equip you with everything you need to understand them.

WHAT IS CRYPTOCURRENCY

At a glance, cryptocurrency is just like any other digital currencies for your online banks. You are partly right. Cryptocurrencies do not have a physical presence. The entire system is completely online, supported by the users.

You see, cryptocurrencies are within decentralized networks. These networks use blockchain technology. Blockchain is a peer-to-peer (P2P) distributed ledger system that is upheld by the user’s computers. More on its function and inner workings later.

Cryptocurrency is classified as a virtual currency by the United States government and it is secured by cryptography. In other words, the major cryptocurrencies are virtually impossible to replicate or counterfeit.

The thing that sets cryptocurrencies apart from other fiat currencies, or government-issued currencies, is the fact that these do not usually come from any government or financial institution. While certain governments and banks are experimenting with cryptocurrencies of their own, the original and major cryptocurrencies do not come from any central authorities. Instead, they come from the users.

This defining feature is the main point of contention nowadays between crypto supporters and skeptics. Cryptocurrencies are technically immune to government interference or manipulation, but some argue that this lack of control is dangerous, to say the least.

The name “cryptocurrency” is derived from the encryption techniques implemented to protect the entire network from hackers. Blockchain technology powers the entire system, which is why you often hear the two terms used together. Think of it this way. Cryptocurrency is the money that you send and receive whereas blockchain technology is the entire financial system that allows the transaction to happen.

Since cryptocurrencies are independent of government and financial institutions, there have been concerns surrounding the subject. After all, the latest 2020 Bitcoin halving and its subsequent price surge gave many people the impression that Bitcoin is the digital gold. There is a lot of money here, so you can bet that many people are interested in the coins.

Many financial experts believe that blockchain and other relevant technology will ultimately revolutionize how transactions work. But their applications reach far beyond just financial technology. Blockchain is an information technology. In other words, it gives you a new way to store information. Sure, the technology is not new. It just implements technologies differently and that happens with a very secure and effective method. This technology has the potential to change how information is stored and disrupts many industries such as healthcare, law, and finance.

Cryptocurrencies have been bashed many times in the past and the arguments against them are largely the same. They always point to the potential use for illegal activities, volatility in the exchange rate, and the vulnerabilities of the underlying infrastructure, especially the cryptocurrency exchanges where cryptocurrencies can be bought and sold. While the blockchain network itself is virtually impenetrable, the crypto exchanges are particularly vulnerable and that is where most of the money is at.

Despite all of this, cryptocurrencies are praised for their transparency, divisibility, resistance to inflation, and portability.

TYPES OF CRYPTOCURRENCY

The first-ever cryptocurrency was Bitcoin, which kickstarted it all. Bitcoin is still the most valuable and popular cryptocurrency today. This is closely followed by Ethereum, Bitcoin Cash, Litecoin, etc. Altcoin is a blanket term for all alternative cryptocurrencies besides Bitcoin.

Not all cryptocurrencies are created with the same purpose in mind. While Bitcoin is created to become a medium of transaction, Ethereum is also used to fund startups with their Initial Coin Offering (ICO) events. Investing your Ether into ICO is a viable way to make more money, and we will talk more about this later.

Bitcoin was born in 2009 developed by one Satoshi Nakamoto. It is not known who this person is or if it is a group of people, but we do know that their cryptocurrency is digital gold. As of March 2021, there were over 18 million Bitcoins in circulation. This means that the total market cap of Bitcoin is around $927 billion. The total market cap of all cryptocurrencies in existence is about $1.5 trillion, and Bitcoin holds over 60% of that share.

Advantages

Cryptocurrency makes transactions fast and cheap. This is possible because of the blockchain network which allows fund transfer between two parties without the need for an intermediary. For a normal bank transfer, your bank would verify the transaction before sending your money off to the other party. The way it works now is people are still required at the helm, especially when a large amount of money is being transferred internationally. This means higher transaction costs and more time needed to verify the transaction.

Cryptocurrency does not have such a tedious system. Sure, the transaction still needs to be verified but this job falls onto the miners, a common term when one discusses cryptocurrency. These miners contribute their computing power to verify transactions and they get paid a small amount of cryptocurrency for their work. The thing is that they get paid similar amounts regardless of how much money is being sent. The fee on crypto transactions is minuscule compared to the fees from your bank or other financial institutions.

Plus, the blockchain network allows for transactions to occur within minutes. Bitcoin takes about nine to ten minutes to verify, and other cryptocurrencies can do the job faster because the developers optimized their network.

Disadvantages

That said, the biggest perceived disadvantage for cryptocurrency is its anonymity. You see, users on the blockchain network do not use their real names. They use pseudonyms or usernames, and tracking down any specific user is a challenging task even for the United States government.

This blanket of anonymity means that criminals can engage in illegal activities without the government’s knowledge. They might use it to launder money or avoid tax. On the flip side, there have been cases where people would send their life savings into the blockchain network by buying up a cryptocurrency, and then move out of their dangerous country, only to get back their riches. That way, they don’t have to risk all their wealth by carrying it with them.

Certain journalists and whistleblower websites and outlets require an alternative source of income since they may be denied financial services by major financial institutions. WikiLeaks is one such website. These websites are located on the Dark Web, where anonymity is guaranteed. That means, cryptocurrency benefits whistleblowers and activists living under repressive governments.

It is also worth mentioning that the best way to stop crime would be to give up all forms of privacy. If the government knows everything, they can stop illegal activities long before it happens. But would you want to live in such a society? Evidently, any government should not have this much power. In fact, the NSA scandal with Edward Snowden illustrates just that.

The case with the TOR browser that allows you to access the Dark Web shows why anonymity has to be accessible for everyone or no one at all. The TOR browser is intended to be used by United States officials. It allows them to remain anonymous as they conduct their investigation. However, if only they have access to the technology, the website’s host would know that an FBI agent is checking out their website if they see an anonymous user. So, the U.S. government is forced to release the technology to the public.

And that is just the sad truth of anonymity. Either no one has it at all or everyone has to have it if we want anonymity to work. Unfortunately, that also includes criminals. That said, not all cryptocurrencies are equally private.

For instance, Bitcoin is not a good option for illegal businesses. The way the blockchain network is set up allows digital forensic analysts to track down criminals. This happened with the Silk Road website in the Dark Web, which was basically Amazon for drugs.

Special Considerations

The entire appeal behind cryptocurrencies is blockchain technology, which provides a new way to store information. It is used to keep an online ledger of all the transactions that happened within the network and recorded everything in chronological order. In addition, the ledger is distributed among all the users in the blockchain network.

These ledgers are independent of one another, but they would arrive at the same conclusion thanks to the consensus algorithm. In other words, the ledgers are a way to check and verify each other’s validity so that to alter an entry, one needs to alter every entry on the network, which is nigh impossible.

All transactions are recorded and added to a “block”, which would be added to the blockchain after it is confirmed by a miner. After the block has been confirmed, it is impossible to change it unless you alter every block in the blockchain. That means, it is not possible to forge transaction histories.

Many experts, though divided on the future of Bitcoin and Altcoins, all agree that blockchain technology has a serious potential for other uses where anonymity is guaranteed and forgery or fabrication is impossible. That means online voting, crowdfunding, etc.

However, since cryptocurrencies are completely virtual, you can lose your expensive coins simply by hardware failure. If you store your coins in your hard drive without a backup copy, they are gone if your hardware fails. If you were to lose your credit card or forgot your PIN, your bank can restore your access easily. The same cannot be said for your crypto wallet since no one else can access it.

Criticism of Cryptocurrency

The price for every product on the planet is based on supply and demand. High supply and low demand mean low prices. Low supply and high demand mean high prices. Cryptocurrencies are designed to be scarce so that their price would go up exponentially over time if they are successful.

The perfect illustration of this is Bitcoin. Bitcoin experienced a “halving” back in 2016. This halving is a way to control how much Bitcoin would be released into circulation. You see, there is a hard cap on how many Bitcoins there would be in the end. That cap is 21 million Bitcoins. New Bitcoins are created to reward the Bitcoin miners and the amount changes after every halving.

As the name suggests, Bitcoin halving is when the flow of Bitcoin being released into circulation is cut in half. This occurs every four years or so and is a way to control the price of the coins. When the halving happens, miners receive only half the coins they used to get.

This, in turn, creates scarcity and drives the price way up. The 2016 halving saw the coins going as high as $19,000 each before dropping back down to $7,000. However, the price before the halving was much lower than $7,000. In other words, just because you receive fewer coins from mining, their worth is more than doubled, so it’s a win-win for everyone.

Other than that, cryptocurrency is controlled by the people. There is no central authority to oversee the flow other than what’s been hardcoded in the network. That also means that the crypto trading market is highly volatile.

In foreign exchange or Forex, the major financial institutions exert influence over a currency’s value trends. So, the retail traders, the ordinary people, generally trade alongside the financial institutions in hope that they can cash in on the trend. Retail traders only trade in small amounts, so they don’t have any influence over the trends.

In cryptocurrency, there is no central authority. So, people who trade cryptocurrencies directly influence their prices. And since there are plenty of people in the system hoping to make money off cryptocurrencies, they cause a lot of swings in the price.

In short, cryptocurrencies are highly volatile and experts agree that this is a risky investment option. This is exactly what happened in the 2016 halving where people would flock to Bitcoin, buy up every coin they could get, and hope to sell when the price is at its peak. However, most people who joined the craze were not savvy traders and they bought up the coins when the price was already at the highest. Many saw their investments going down the drain and refused to sell, hoping to catch the next big break that never came. For this reason, cryptocurrencies are often regarded as a short-term fad or little more than a speculative bubble.

Another concern surrounding cryptocurrencies is that they are not tied to any material goods. Before the gold standard was obsolete, gold was the store of value. Then came fiat currencies, which is money issued by the government and its value is tied directly to the government’s economic and political power.

Cryptocurrencies possess neither of those so their value remains questionable to this day. One possible “material good” that cryptocurrencies are tied to is the production cost, or the energy and hardware needed to mine cryptocurrencies.

But there is yet another problem. Mining cryptocurrencies is a power-intensive process and the upfront cost can be too steep for many people. You need to have a beefy computer or a specialized tool to mine Bitcoin efficiently. Mining Bitcoin is a race, as in whichever miner gets to verify the transaction first would be awarded. This is the proof of work system.

This competition leads to the creation of ASIC, which is a piece of specialized equipment that allows miners to outmine all other miners who do not have this hardware, and there have been controversies around this expensive device. Casual miners looking to make a few bucks with their computers end up losing money because they could not compete, and they would still have to pay for the high power bill every month.

If you’re interested in mining Altcoins such as Ethereum, you might have a better shot since some cryptocurrencies use a different method called proof of stake, where you stake some of your coins to show your willingness to verify the transaction. Everyone gets their fair share of work, even the small-timers, and everyone is happy.

Power consumption is a serious issue and mining crypto is not helping the issue here. At first, people used graphics cards and processors to handle the mining. But these tools are not optimized for the job since they are created for other purposes.

The Bitcoin craze created a market that arguably left everyone worse off. Certain tech companies such as Nvidia started creating dedicated GPU for miners. It is a GPU with no output port and is intended to be used for mining. The problem with this move is that it puts more strain on the global supply of silicone and chips, which is already stretched thin as it is. This affected even car manufacturers.

Then there is the problem of security. Of course, the blockchain network behind every cryptocurrency is highly secure, but they are not the only thing within the cryptocurrency ecosystem. There are crypto exchanges where you can buy and sell cryptocurrencies and you can store your coins in digital wallets. While the blockchain network is almost impervious to hacking, the exchanges and wallets are not.

Crypto exchanges are online platforms where you can buy and sell cryptocurrencies. These websites are usually the target of hackers and there have been successful cyberattacks that resulted in millions of dollars lost. Crypto wallets are not 100% safe either unless you use hardware wallets, which in itself brings some other risks.

Regardless, many observers believe that cryptocurrencies have the potential to revolutionize the financial world. Even if it fails to do so, blockchain technology would be beneficial, nonetheless.

WHY INVEST IN CRYPTOCURRENCY

The biggest question people ask is why they should bother investing in cryptocurrency. The 2016 Bitcoin halving taught people a lesson already and they put their feet down and say “never again”. Now, there is a sudden interest in the subject again. What is up with that? Why is everyone talking about cryptocurrency again?

It is Different This Time

There are many reasons why you should invest in cryptocurrency. One main reason why people have reservations about cryptocurrencies right now is because of the 2016 Bitcoin halving, which saw a huge hike in price soon followed by a crash that left many people worse off.

But this time is different because many companies, including the big players such as Tesla and PayPal, are getting in on the action. These players were absent during the 2016 halving. The fact they are now coupled with the widespread adoption of cryptocurrency means that cryptocurrency is flourishing.

You can make a lot of money by utilizing your cryptocurrency. You can treat it like a stock by buying and holding onto the coins until selling it off at a later date at a higher price.

Alternatively, you can invest in various projects and startups that accept cryptocurrencies. These projects utilize blockchain technology to create innovations that would bring a huge leap forward in many industries. So, although you may have reservations about cryptocurrency, you should at least consider the use case of the blockchain technology that powers cryptocurrencies in the first place. And there are many applications for this technology.

Blockchain Use Cases

Blockchain technology allows for data storage on a peer-to-peer network that is very resilient against cyberattacks. This seemingly simple function gives blockchain technology a wide range of applications that we did not know we needed until now.

Decentralized Finance

One of the most common uses for blockchain technology is decentralized finance, which is a breath of fresh air from traditional and centralized financial systems. Decentralized finance (DeFi) uses peer-to-peer finance that is enabled by decentralized technologies created on a blockchain network.

This is where all the cryptocurrencies come from. Interestingly enough, it does not have to apply to cryptocurrencies alone. Banks and other financial institutions can make use of blockchain technology to enable the transfer of fiat currencies with all the benefits that blockchain technology provides.

The most cited use case for DeFi would be on the Ethereum network where you could run smart contracts to automatically execute certain protocols exactly as programmed, without the need of a third party overseeing the whole operation. DeFi has no centralized infrastructure and is instead powered purely by codes running on decentralized infrastructure on a blockchain network.

There are a number of benefits that DeFi provides that centralized financial systems just cannot. For one, it makes use of smart contracts that automate certain processes. The blockchain network is also tamper-proof, and security is something everybody wants when it comes to money.

The blockchain network also allows for interoperability as protocols and applications on the network can interact with one another. Therefore, we are looking at a robust ecosystem of apps and smart contracts on a given blockchain network.

It is also very accessible. Another label for it would be “permissionless”, but this does not mean people can access your data without your permission. This just refers to the fact that anyone with a crypto wallet and internet connection can access the blockchain network, oftentimes without any minimum amount of crypto in their wallet, from anywhere across the globe. Some contents are not accessible because of government censorship or it is just locked to certain regions, so blockchain allows you a greater level of accessibility. Plus, you can access it without giving anyone permission. No one can bar you from accessing the blockchain network.

The way the blockchain network is designed allows for a great level of transparency as well. Every transaction is broadcasted and verified by the miners and recorded on the ledgers that anyone can access. Moreover, DeFi protocols are created with open-source that anyone can see, so that people can have a complete understanding of what really is going on behind the scene for apps on the blockchain network.

Non-Fungible Tokens

Non-fungible tokens (NFTs) are tokens that are used to represent ownership of items with unique qualities. That means, intellectual properties or real estate. Only one person can hold this token and this ownership is recorded and protected by the blockchain network. No one can alter the ownership record or fabricate a new NFT into existence.

On the flip side, fungible items are defined by their value, not their unique properties. Suppose that a single Bitcoin is worth 1,000 USD. Both Bitcoin and USD are fungible because one Bitcoin or 1,000 USD is exchangeable for another one Bitcoin or 1,000 USD.

Each NFT has a unique identifier. That means, it is digitally unique and so no two NFTs are the same. Every NFT has to have an owner, and the ownership record is on the public ledger that anyone can check to verify. On the Ethereum network, NFTs can be used with apps on the platform. You can sell an NFT ticket to an event in exchange for another NFT. Plus, content creators can sell their work and retain ownership rights over their own work. They can claim royalty over resold work directly because they have the tokens to verify their ownership of the content. Another interesting application for NFT is that you can use it as collateral if you use a decentralized loan service.

Each NFT has a special identifier and it is not interchangeable with another token, at least at a 1:1 ratio. One Bitcoin is exactly the same as another Bitcoin, but 1 NFT is not equal to another NFT. You can buy and sell NFT, but the original creator would claim some resale royalties. Alternatively, you can hold onto your NFT forever. Your digital or physical asset that is tied to the NFT would be yours forever because you can use your NFT to prove your ownership.

That said, NFTs are primarily used for digital content. Ownership over digital content outside of blockchain is stored on servers that are controlled by various centralized institutions and you have no choice but to hope that they keep their words. With NFTs, you have full control over your intellectual property.

The list of applications goes on and on. But you can easily see how useful NFTs could be in protecting content creators’ revenues and intellectual properties. It also has interesting applications for physical items as well, namely real estate. The possibility is quite literally endless for NFTs.

Video Streaming

Another interesting application for blockchain technology is in the video-streaming industry. The issue with the current video-streaming methodology is the file size. High-quality videos take up a lot of space and you would need servers to store everything. This has continued to increase costs that have trickled down to content creators.

However, blockchain technology seeks to cut down on those costs using smart contracts, which we will get to in a minute. Blockchain technology works here by allowing you to store video content on decentralized servers. The content is heavily encrypted and secured.

What are these decentralized servers? Those are miners’ devices. Miners can load the software onto their devices and basically rent out their excess computer space to store the video files and stream the content with their excess bandwidth. Miners would then be rewarded in the form of tokens or cryptocurrency.

Theta is doing just that and their project has seen steady growth. Over time, perhaps we will see a new crypto mining method in the future. So far, we have CPU, GPU, cloud, ASIC mining, all of which we will discuss in Chapter 3.

Smart Contracts

Smart contracts are essentially programs slapped onto a contract. It is created to take in certain data and execute certain clauses of the contract should certain conditions be met. At its core, smart contracts are the if-then Excel function. Nothing more, nothing less.

Again, this application is very simple in nature. But its utility is very versatile. You can use it to trade product movement in a supply chain, to confirm identity digitally, for voting, healthcare, food safety, intellectual property, the list goes on and on.

The reason why smart contracts are so desirable is that it operates on the blockchain network. That means, tampering with the data is nigh impossible unless you break the whole thing. It takes away a third party who has to oversee the whole thing. It just automates the entire process for you. With a bit of tinkering, you just need to feed it some input and it will spit out the right output. It is that simple.

HOW CRYPTOCURRENCIES WORK

In this section, we will get into the details of the workings behind cryptocurrencies.

We will start with the ledger. As the name suggests, the ledger records all transactions that happen within the blockchain network and organizes them all into chronological order. The entries are completely public, meaning that anyone can view them. Anonymity here comes in the form of username or pseudonyms. So, while you can see which user sent how many coins to whom, you do not know the real person behind those usernames. This gives you the perfect balance between anonymity and transparency.

The entries on the ledger cannot be changed, either. The only way to do that would be through a 50% + 1 confirmation from other ledgers in the system. Having a majority of ledgers that agree would automatically update all ledgers to have the same entries. This is difficult to pull off since each user has their own independent ledger that uses a consensus algorithm. While all ledgers agree on updating their entries after a transaction has been verified by a miner, fabricating a transaction is nigh impossible.

We also need to talk about transaction verification. Suppose that you want to dabble in cryptocurrency. You buy some Bitcoins through a crypto exchange. Then, you decide to spend it. The entire process is completed within minutes, but a lot happened behind the scenes.

When you decided to spend your coins, the transaction requires verification. In other words, the transaction is not official yet and it is up to the miners to verify it. The miners’ computers or devices have to solve an algorithm problem to complete the verification. They are looking for the key to confirm the transaction. Once that is completed, the transaction is recorded in the ledger and becomes part of the blockchain. The miners would be awarded a modest sum of Bitcoin for their work.

WHERE CAN YOU USE CRYPTOCURRENCY?

The biggest question surrounding cryptocurrency right now is whether it will advance beyond being a vehicle for speculation. The goal for Bitcoin is to become a global currency that is accepted worldwide. It is a lofty goal, but given its current value, it has a good chance of becoming the currency that transcends borders and jurisdictions.

Given its price volatility and the fact that governments across the globe are still on the fence about the matter, many companies are still waiting. Adopting cryptocurrencies into their payment method is easy enough. Plus, they have every incentive to do so. The transaction cost is lower. The processing speed is faster. Plus, it is impossible to forge transactions. The only thing stopping everyone from accepting cryptocurrencies is the law.

That said, there are plenty of stores out there that accept cryptocurrencies as a payment method. It’s a high-risk high-reward move. If the government suddenly decides to outlaw cryptocurrencies, then all the coins they’ve made would go down the drain. However, if the government would officially accept cryptocurrencies, then these stores would be well ahead of their competition when it comes to adopting cryptocurrencies.

Then there is the problem with its volatility. Again, the price swings wildly and so pricing your products or services based on the number of coins is just asking for trouble. Let’s say that a coin is worth $5 and so your $50 product would require 10 coins. Now, if the price goes down to $4 per coin, then you would already lose $10 from last day’s sale.

That is unless you cash out on the coins the moment you get them. This is a viable solution since some crypto exchanges allow you to convert your coins into fiat currencies at a flat rate. That means, if you route your payment via the crypto exchange, they would convert the coins and give you the cash and you don’t have to worry about the fluctuating rate.

But therein lies another problem. What if you route your coins through the exchange, but then the rate goes up? Then you would lose out on the extra profit you can make. If the coin price goes up to $8 per coin, then you miss out on $30 per sale because you sold all your coins yesterday.

In fact, many business owners rely on the bullish trend of Bitcoin. A bullish trend simply means an upward trend. In other words, they hold onto the coins and hope that the price will go up so they can cash out when the price is much better. It’s riskier than converting the coins at a flat rate, but there is a lot of money to make even if the price difference is only $10. If you hoard enough coins, the numbers will start to add up very quickly.

For this reason, companies are slowly adopting cryptocurrencies into their payment methods. This also expands their reach to the cryptocurrency community. Elon Musk tweeted that Tesla would be accepting cryptocurrencies in the near future at the moment of writing and many tech giants are jumping on the cryptocurrency train.

Among them is Microsoft. In fact, Microsoft started accepting Bitcoin for its Xbox store in 2014 but stopped for a while due to price volatility. Now, you can buy Xbox store credits using Bitcoin once again. Bill Gates said that Bitcoin is just better than fiat currencies on several occasions.

Overstock is another early adopter. They accept Altcoins as well as Bitcoin and it is clear that they support the cryptocurrencies since they are holding onto the coins. They do not just cash in on the coins at their earliest convenience. Instead, they are holding onto this precious digital asset.

Home Depot, one of the biggest hardware store chains in the U.S., also accepts Bitcoin. Since they are the biggest hardware provider, you can buy enough material to build an entire house using nothing but Bitcoin. Geminis partnered up with Flexa to create the checkout systems for Home Depot so that the payments go as smoothly as possible.

You can also pay for your coffee at Starbucks using Bitcoin. Though, they would not be accepting direct payment via Bitcoin. Instead, you would have to use the Bakkt app, a crypto exchange, for that.

Whole Foods also jumped on the train by partnering up with the Winklevoss Flexa app called “Spedn”. They accept Bitcoin, Litecoin, and Gemini dollars. So, you’ve got your grocery expenses covered with Whole Foods. But, through the Spedn app, you can also find products from Baskin Ribbins, Gamestop, Jamba Juice, and Regal Cinemas.

Newegg is known for buying hardware to mine cryptocurrencies and they have all kinds of computer hardware for sale, so if you’re interested in mining cryptocurrencies, consider checking out what they have to offer.

The list goes on and on. Chances are that a few weeks after this book has been published, more shops would accept cryptocurrencies as a payment method.

ON THE SUBJECT OF COINS AND TOKENS

When you talk about cryptocurrencies, you might hear the term “token” being used now and again, and it can be confusing when you learn that you can buy and sell these tokens as well. It’s even more baffling when people sometimes use the term “coins” and “tokens” interchangeably. However, both are totally different things. So, what’s the difference?

Let’s start with coins. Coins or cryptocurrencies are digital assets that have their own blockchain. So, each cryptocurrency, or coin, has its own blockchain network that they operate in. Bitcoin only works on the Bitcoin blockchain. Litecoin only works on the Litecoin blockchain. Each blockchain is independent of one another.

You can send and receive coins but there is nothing physical about it other than the computers working to send the data around a gigantic global database. This database is the blockchain network that keeps track of all transactions.

You can use your coins the same way you spend money. You can send and receive it from someone. You can also hold onto it and sell it off at a later date and pocket the profit. Or, you can also buy products and services using cryptocurrency.

While Bitcoin is intended to be used as money, Ether and other cryptocurrencies are created for other purposes.

For instance, Ether is used to facilitate transactions on the Ethereum network. This is important since most tokens are developed on Ethereum. So even though you are sending or receiving tokens, the sender still has to spend a bit of Ether, which goes to the miners.

NEO is a stake in a wallet to earn a dividend. This is also known as GAS. Tokens built on NEO can be sent and received the same way on Ethereum, and the GAS is a transaction fee.

Then there’s DASH. It’s a coin, but if you own enough of them, you can vote on important decisions for the entire Dash network. Let’s say that someone posted an idea to upgrade the DASH network. So, those who own a large amount of Dash coins can decide whether to upgrade or not. So, owners of Dash coins can decide what direction the project goes.

So, how about tokens? Tokens are different from coins. They are created on an existing blockchain. Thanks to smart contracts, the most popular platform for tokens would be Ethereum. All tokens created on Ethereum are called ERC-20 tokens.

Smart contracts are computer programs or transaction protocols created to automate the execution, control, or documentation of legally relevant events and actions. This is all based on the terms and agreement of the contract.

As mentioned before, Ethereum is not the only platform for creating tokens. You have NEO, Lisk, Waves, and Stratis. They have different names for their tokens. For instance, tokens created on the NEO platform are known as NEP-5 tokens.

Creating tokens is very easy, although you still need some coding experience. You just have to have some native coins to get started so the miners can facilitate the transaction process.

Most tokens are created to be used with decentralized apps such as dApps. Developers creating the tokens have complete control over how many tokens they want to have and where they would go after they are created. Upon creation, they would need to spend some native coins.

After the tokens are created and distributed, token owners can use them to activate features on specific apps. For instance, Musicoin gives you the ability to access their library of music, videos, and songs.

A crypto exchange called Binance also has its own token which gives users a 50% discount on their fees.

Tokens can also be used to represent a physical object. Suppose you want to use a smart contract to sell your house. Of course, you cannot put your house into the smart contract, at least not physically. So, an alternative would be to use a token to represent the house.

WePower or WPR reflects that usage perfectly. Their project is a dApp and their tokens represent electricity. It’s not entirely physical, but you get the idea. Users can buy and sell electricity using smart contracts. Each WPR token represents a certain amount of energy.

The question then is, why use tokens?

First of all, it’s the simple fact that developers do not have to create an entire blockchain. They can piggyback on an existing blockchain and use their limited resources on something else. They benefit from the security and resilience of the native blockchain they are on, such as Ethereum.

If they were to create their own blockchain, it would be expensive and it can take forever. You see, if you create a blockchain, you would need miners. Lots of them. And since miners flock to the more popular cryptocurrencies, you can see how that is not happening.

Plus, a blockchain’s resilience against cyberattacks depends entirely on the number of users. You want to have many computers connected to a blockchain rather than having thousands of weak and centralized blockchain networks. Overall, it’s just expensive, tedious, and nigh impossible.

This problem can be solved if you bite the bullet and pay a bit of coin of the native blockchain network and work on your tokens from there.

With all that said, how does a token work in practice? Let’s take Civic. Civic uses CVC tokens and is an app that contains your private information. This information is fully encrypted so that even if your device is stolen, your private information remains safe.

The Civic app is intended for those who would have their identities checked often. It can be a hassle having to hold onto the paperwork and other things, so the Civic app puts everything in one convenient place for you. It is cheaper, more reliable, and efficient.

Suppose that you go to South-East Asia on a holiday, then you might need to confirm your identity more than once. The first time would be the airport. If Civic happens to be a partner of that airline, you would have the option to scan a QR code. The app would then send all the necessary details to the company. No more fumbling with papers. It’s all inside your phone. Of course, to confirm that the information indeed comes from you, you are required to do a fingerprint or an iris scan.

You can do this whenever you need to verify your identity. So long as the destination is a partner with Civic, then the entire process is a breeze. Your information can be verified via the blockchain network.

You might be thinking that not many companies would partner up with Civic, and you would be correct. It is still rather small in scope right now, but as the app continues to be used, the more trust it gathers. Eventually, it will reach a point where everyone has heard about the app.

But that’s not the entire system behind the CVC tokens. They have another role to play. CVC is used up in the verification process. The verifiers of IDs such as governments, banks, and other trusted sources would be paid CVC tokens for their KYC (know-your-customer) checks. The records would be stored on the blockchain.

The user also receives a certain number of CVC when that happens. The idea is to encourage you to continue using Civic as the companies that need to confirm your identity need to buy more tokens from you, the user. So, this system creates an interesting economy where everyone is rewarded for their participation.

Since CVC tokens are developed on the Ethereum blockchain, the transaction requires some Ether to fuel the transaction process. That said, the Civic app uses CVC tokens exclusively. You cannot use Bitcoin, Ethereum, or NEO to access their services.

CRYPTOGRAPHY MADE SIMPLE

Cryptocurrency got its name as a cross between currency and cryptography. Although cryptocurrency boasts bulletproof encryption to protect data, not many people understand what cryptography is and how is it linked to cryptocurrency.

Let’s start with the basics. Cryptography is the practice of encrypting information in a way that only the people “in the know” would be able to read it. That way, even if the information falls into the wrong hands, no one from outside would know what it means. It’s an ancient technique that is still used today, from Julius Caesar communicating with his generals using ciphers, to the Allied forces using encrypted messages so the Axis could not read it, even if they could intercept the message.

Cryptography is needed to protect sensitive information or data from being stolen by someone who isn’t supposed to see it. The data can be anything from finance to military information. There are plenty of reasons why people would want to keep their private information private.

As you can probably tell, cryptography can take on many forms. There are many ways to scramble data or information in a way that the recipient can decipher it. Before modern technology, people came up with creative ways to encrypt information. For instance, BSELUEA can go through a cipher and give you BLUE SEA. It’s a simple task of rearranging the letters in a specific way. Some would go as far as to create an entire alphabet.

Nowadays, cryptography becomes much more complex thanks to computers. They can apply very complex mathematical technology to encrypt data. These technologies are now used for cryptocurrencies. But no matter how complex it gets, it always works on the same basic principle. That is to hide the actual information so that only authorized people can access it.

So, how does cryptography apply in cryptocurrency?

Cryptography is used in the blockchain network to protect the transactions, control the registration of new transactions, and verify the transfer of assets. To accomplish these goals, cryptocurrencies rely on public key cryptography.

Public key cryptography gives users two separate keys. One is called “public key”, which is used to receive the coins. To access the coins, you would need a “private key”. Think of it like one of those blue mailboxes you find in public places. The public key would be the box itself since anyone can put mail in there. However, to access the mail in the box, you would need the key, and only the mailman has that key. This key to the box is the private key.

The keys are normally around 30 characters long and are a jumbled mess of letters and numbers. It does not mean anything. The public key is also called the public address since that jumbled mess serves as an address so people can send you coins.

These keys are linked to your digital wallet, where your coins are actually stored. So, anyone can send you coins into your wallet using your public key. However, to access the wallet, you would need a private key, which you should not share with anyone.

Public key cryptography is a simple concept, but pulling it off is an impressive technological advancement. Even if cryptocurrency fades into obscurity in the near future, the technologies it leaves behind would benefit everyone. This cryptography is one of those technologies that will change the online payment industry.

Though there are vulnerabilities with public key cryptography, it is all just human errors and there is not much technology can do to compensate for that. For instance, people could lose their private keys. If so, they might have no way of recovering the funds in their crypto wallet. Sometimes, they might accidentally reveal their private key to someone else and they could just access the wallet and steal everything.

Again, this is just human errors but there are ways to prevent this from happening by setting up two-factor authentication, backing up the digital wallet, and keeping spare private keys. You can write your private keys on a piece of paper stick it somewhere that you know will be safe. Have a couple of copies in different places just to be safe.

INVESTING CRYPTO AT YOUR OWN RISK

Before you invest in cryptocurrencies, you need to understand the associated risks.

Price Volatility and Manipulation

As mentioned before, cryptocurrencies experience very wild price swings. There are major price surges that are soon followed by crashes. These seemingly random events leave experts and investors alike amazed and baffled. Your profit today could be your loss tomorrow. The price swings so wildly that it is difficult for anyone to have the same confidence as the stock market or Forex. The volatility is oftentimes random and unpredictable, so even the strategies you meticulously planned out might not work because of factors outside your control.

Speaking of factors, the volatility in crypto prices comes from three sources: market manipulation, speculation, and sentiment. Since the cryptocurrency market is largely unregulated and anonymous, the price becomes very volatile. Feelings run high. Influencers can manipulate prices just by tweeting. Price manipulation hasn’t been widely proven yet because of the anonymous nature of cryptocurrency, but it is not hard to see that it is there. The most common price manipulation tactics would be pump and dumps and shilling.

Lack of Regulations

The lack of a regulatory framework contributes to the problem. Governments across the globe are on the fence about the issue. Some outlawed cryptocurrencies whereas others are starting to accept them.

For one, having a legal framework would be a sign that the government views cryptocurrencies favorably. It is the missing link that Bitcoin and Altcoins need to achieve widespread adoption. However, in the business world, you want to have as few regulations as possible so you can trade and make a profit easily. Too many laws mean more restrictions that could cut into your margins.

At the same time, if there’s a lack of regulation, then the legal outlook would be uncertain. It means that the government does not officially support the coins and they may remain obscure and function as little more than speculative vehicles.

The current legal framework for cryptocurrencies is complex, haphazard, and disorganized. The biggest concern right now would be taxes, as expected. It is all about the money, after all. The lack of regulations in this area or a “regulatory greyness” means that investors are uncertain of what tax obligations are required. They want to know how much they should set aside for taxes as well as what records they need to keep. Failing to meet these obligations comes with a very hefty price and investors do not want to make the IRS unhappy. As the Joker once said, he’s more willing to fight Batman rather than fight the IRS.

Though regulations are slow to catch up to technology, when there is a lot of money on the line, the pacing is much better. In this case, regulations are catching up quickly to cryptocurrencies. Authorities in many countries are taking appropriate steps to introduce regulations. Research papers are being written, standards are set, etc.

The first country to start building up a robust regulatory framework for cryptocurrency is Switzerland. Their idea is to create as few rules as possible so that the market remains free, but provide enough rules that go with other legal frameworks so that companies know what obligations they need to fulfill through a “sandbox” so that startups can play around with their innovations within a controlled environment.

Singapore and Britain are also starting to experiment with their blockchain and crypto regulatory environment. This environment allows companies to experiment under relaxed regulations and licensing requirements.
