For the United States, there is a study on exchanges created by the New York Attorney General’s Office.

Market Adoption

Bitcoin strives toward widespread adoption, but there are a number of things that are holding it back such as regulatory concerns, market volatility, the lack of understanding from the public, among other things. That and the fact that cryptocurrencies and the blockchain technology that powers them are still new, there is a chance that cryptocurrency would not be broadly adopted. By that point, cryptocurrencies would lose all of their value. While no one really knows when the deadline would be, cryptocurrencies need the law, technological improvements, and institutions to back them up as soon as possible if they were to grow in scale.

Security, Custody, and Consumer Rights

Although the blockchain network is secure, there are other points of vulnerability in its ecosystem. Users need to take all measures to store their cryptocurrencies and other crypto assets or risk losing it all.

Crypto exchanges are viewed as one of the most unsafe places to store your cryptocurrencies. Most exchanges give you a wallet to store your coins, but there have been cases where they have been hacked and all the coins were stolen. Of course, most exchanges you see today would talk about their top-notch security and that your coins would be safe with them. But it is better to be safe than sorry and store your coins in a hardware wallet.

To make matters worse, lost or stolen coins or assets cannot be recovered. Tracking down where your coins went is nigh impossible. If you send your coins to someone by mistake, there is no way to reverse the transaction either. The best you can do is to ask them nicely to send you back the coin. That is, assuming that you know who got your coins in the first place.

Because there is no intermediary, cryptocurrencies do not have central or official safeguards or insurances. How much you get from rebates on lost investment depends entirely on the organization you are doing business with.

Thankfully, there are now custody solutions that allow financial institutions to act as an intermediary in such a transaction. This is one of the things that cryptocurrencies desperately need to gain trust and receive widespread adoption.

The system works by having a third party holding onto the cryptocurrencies on behalf of the trading clients. Once the products or services are given and all are satisfied, then the coins would be sent to the provider. The basic system includes three parties. The client, the provider, and the mediator. The client purchases goods or services from the provider, but place the coins with the mediator until the provider delivers the goods or services. Once done, it takes two out of three approvals from the parties involved to release the funds. If there are any disputes, then the mediator may step in and resolve the conflict.

This gives investors an added level of security when investing their expensive coins. If things go south, they can get their coins sent back to them. One may argue that the client can abuse their power and demand their coins returned to them after they got the product or service. Of course, the mediator has to be trusted by both parties to make a sound judgment of the situation.

The custody solutions are believed to be the leap cryptocurrencies need to kickstart the entry of institutional capital into the cryptocurrency industry. Once institutions start to invest using cryptocurrencies, it will start to gain trust and approval and retail investors would soon follow suit.

Coinbase and a few other crypto exchanges are starting to offer their custody products. A multinational investment bank, Citigroup, also said that they would provide crypto custody solutions to institutional investors. They created Digital Asset receipt, which allows institutional investors to invest in cryptocurrencies while still following regulations and have a safe way to pull out if the investment goes awry. Fidelity and their subsidiary Fidelity Digital Asset Services also aim to do the same thing.

Exiting the Market

Of course, when you enter a market, you have to think of your exit strategy well in advance. You want to know when you want to get out so you can cash in on your profits. The crypto market’s off-ramps pose another problem for many investors. It is with the exchange rate. Most exchange only allows you to withdraw in USD. If you’re in the United States, that is fine. Other exchanges might offer JPY, GBP, or EUR, but your options are nonetheless limited.

The problem is that while blockchain allows for cheap and fast transactions, you are still subject to the inefficiency and slow speed of major financial institutions once you are out of the system. Most exchanges have a high minimum amount if you want to withdraw your fiat currencies.

To make matters worse, they also only accept major cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin or Ethereum. Once you get past that hurdle, you also need to wait on the exchange to send the money over to your bank. This is outside the blockchain network, so you have to put up with the tedious process that can take up to months to complete.

To this day, some exchanges are allegedly withholding funds without a clear reason and most banks are still wary of accepting cash from the sale of cryptocurrency. All this factor means is that investors have to put up with the exchange rates, fees, and other risks if they happen to be using exchanges that lack transparency.

Of course, if they were to invest using fiat currencies directly, they would still have to deal with similar hurdles, but they won’t have to worry about the initial steps they had to do with cryptocurrencies.

2

BEFORE YOU INVEST, THINK

With the crypto basics out of the way, let’s talk about investment. Or rather, before you do, you should stop and think of a couple of things before you dive in. Cryptocurrency can serve as a lucrative investment option if you know what you are doing. As always, investing in cryptocurrencies is not for everyone. So, this chapter will help you determine whether you should set aside some cash and try to make more money. Even if it does not stop you from jumping into the game, it at least gives you something to think about when you do.

CAN YOU AFFORD IT?

First thing first, can you actually afford to invest in cryptocurrencies? That might sound silly. Of course, anyone can set aside a hundred bucks and buy some coins to get started. The real question is intended to access your financial situation.

It should go without saying, but investors know to invest only as much money as they are willing to lose. In other words, the money they put forward in an investment is the money they are willing and can afford to lose.

Your financial situation could be different. Perhaps you are struggling and barely get by every month and you want to make some more money by investing in cryptocurrencies. You can argue that the price volatility could help you turn a profit here and there when the price swings favorably. You might be right and you could theoretically catch these price swings.

However, chances are that you can also make a bad call and end up losing all of your money. Again, the cryptocurrency market is unpredictable. Even if you are being careful, factors outside your control can ruin everything for you. So, if you lose all that money, then that’s it. You just have to make do with whatever money you have left to get by until the next payday.

Even more dangerous still, maybe you think that you can catch the next big break. It’s not an uncommon thing to do especially in the crypto trading market. Feelings run high and your gut is telling you that profit is right around the corner. So, you put forward the money you can’t afford to lose and try your luck. If you succeed, good for you. If not, then it’s game over. It’s clear how this vicious cycle can continue.

So, the golden rule in investment is to only invest money you are willing to lose. Potential investors should first access their current financial situation. Your investment should not influence your financial health, at least not directly.

With that initial assessment out of the way, how much do you need to really start investing in cryptocurrency?

One of the things that attract many people to cryptocurrency trading is the fact that the barrier of entry is quite low. You do not need a lot of upfront investment to start. Though you can invest less, you should start with at least $50.

You can start by buying cryptocurrencies at an exchange and then start to sell or exchange the coins. All these actions have their associated fees, so bad trades or trades that give too small a profit margin will not cut it.

Because of how volatile the market gets, you can turn your $500 into $5,000 and vice versa depending on your strategy and a bit of luck, or lack thereof. Just like any other trading vehicle, doing your research and having a solid strategy will get you very far.

As for the minimum amount you need to start trading Bitcoin or Altcoins, that depends on the exchange. Each cryptocurrency is almost infinitely divisible. Bitcoin, for instance, can be divided up into a hundred million pieces. A hundred millionth is the smallest unit, which is referred to as one Satoshi to pay homage to the original creator of Bitcoin, Satoshi Nakamoto, who started it all.

That said, the minimum amount would be set by your crypto exchange platform, so make sure to check that out. Long story short, you can start trading Bitcoin even when you do not have much money to spare. It just takes more time to build up your wealth.

The next big question people ask is whether you can make a living off trading cryptocurrency. The short answer is yes. Forex traders and stock traders could make a living off their games, and you can too with cryptocurrency. The only difference is that you need some luck and some skills to get started.

There are people who are completely financially independent through crypto trading. High volatility is a high-risk, high-reward scenario, and if you play your cards right, you can turn a hefty chunk of profit.

Your success may also depend on your patience. Again, it is a highly volatile market, so price swings can really make you doubt your strategy. But if you stick to your game plan and practice patience, and most important not get greedy, then you should come out on top most of the time. Again, you will need a bit of luck here. Seeing a trade turn sour now and again because of factors outside your control can be frustrating. But keep your head cool and understand that this happens from time to time.

How Much Does It Cost to Trade Bitcoin?

Other than the price of the coins themselves, you also have to consider the fee you have to fork over when you trade on a crypto exchange. There is a fee when you buy or sell coins. This isn’t some sinister way to make money off of you or anything, but these fees go into making sure that the crypto exchange can continue to operate.

These fees include the Maker and Taker fees. Maker fees are the fees that add to the order book liquidity via limit orders. Taker fees are the fees for subtracting liquidity from an order book via market orders. Sometimes, you can incur both maker and taker fees if the limit order is already there in the order book.

Normally, cryptocurrency exchanges calculate fees as a flat rate per trade or as a portion of the 30-day trading volume for your account. In either case, they have a structure to determine how much you would be charged based on the amount you are trading.

Four Things to Consider While Trading on Crypto Exchanges

Before you start trading, there are four things you need to keep in mind. For one, cryptocurrency exchanges are mostly unregulated in many countries. They have a very hands-off approach to regulating cryptocurrencies. For instance, the crypto exchanges in the United States are only regulated by a patchwork of law. Though some cryptocurrencies are banned in China, major Chinese exchanges are still operational today and they changed their location to Malta or Seychelles to avoid government crackdown.

Next, you need to understand how the fees are the way they are. The idea is to encourage you to make frequent trading in large amounts, which are at least above a thousand dollars. Most exchanges often lower their fees if you trade frequently and in large portions. In other words, if you make small and infrequent trades, the fees would cut into your profit margin pretty quickly. Some crypto exchanges do not charge you at all if you trade over $10 million.

In addition, crypto exchanges encourage their users to trade with coins. Fiat currencies have withdrawal and deposit fees on them, depending on your choice of payment method. Of course, when you first set up your account, you may have to deal with a deposit fee and also a small fee to create your wallet. When you want to withdraw your money, you have to pay the withdrawal fee as well. But once you are in the system, you are better off buying cryptocurrencies with other cryptocurrencies, which is mostly free.

Finally, keep in mind that even the biggest and most popular crypto exchanges do not offer all the coins in existence, even the popular ones. However, you can import coins from your wallet for a small fee without having to switch your crypto exchange.

For instance, suppose that you are using Coinbase and you want to try trading Cardano (ADA) with the Bitcoins you own in your Coinbase wallet. Unfortunately, Coinbase does not list ADA. You can look for another crypto exchange, say Binance, that offers ADA. You don’t have to put in fiat currencies into your Binance account to buy Bitcoin and then trade for ADA. Binance gives you the option to import your wallet from Coinbase, so you can buy ADA directly from Binance with your Bitcoin. Of course, there will be a small fee, but it beats having to pay the deposit fee when you create an account on Binance since you can just import your wallet.

FINDING THE IDEAL CRYPTOCURRENCY

Next up, you need to set a target. What cryptocurrency should you invest in? That depends on your goal. Most people want to make more money, so they believe that investing in Bitcoin would be the way to go. They have a point. Bitcoin is the biggest and most popular cryptocurrency right now. You can just buy some Bitcoins and then hold onto it until their price goes up, then sell them off and pocket the profit.

But there are more ways to invest and profit from crypto investment than that. The above method is viable, though rather slow. Plus, some coins have more utility than that and you can use them to invest in startups or ICOs.

Bitcoin (BTC)

Bitcoin is released in 2009 by Satoshi Nakamoto. It is the biggest and most popular cryptocurrency in the market today as it is the first cryptocurrency to ever be created. Although the technology behind it is rather complex, paying via Bitcoin is becoming easier for an average user. They would use digital wallets to send and receive payments and the coins themselves are listed on virtually every crypto exchange. To date, more and more merchants are accepting Bitcoin such as Microsoft, Subway, etc.

That said, though Bitcoin is recognized as the first cryptocurrency that pioneered blockchain technology and Altcoins, they do have their shortcomings. For instance, due to the block size limitations, it can only handle so many transactions simultaneously. On average, it can handle seven transactions per second. However, there are far more transactions to be processed than just seven in a given second. In the end, it can take up to nine or ten minutes to verify one transaction.

On the other hand, Visa can take on thousands of transactions every second. Bitcoin is considered pretty slow in the transaction processing department. Other cryptocurrencies make it a habit of increasing their block size and other clever methods to improve the transaction speed.

Bitcoin’s prices are currently on the rise following the 2020 halving. However, there is no telling if the price will continue to go up. It could just follow the 2016 trend and plummet very soon. Although cryptocurrencies are definitely here to stay, no one can say for certain that Bitcoin can be the biggest cryptocurrency around. Ethereum, Zcash, and Dash boast greater anonymity, faster transaction speed, and more functionality than Bitcoin.

Ethereum (Ether, ETH)

Ether or ETH is a cryptocurrency that is based on the Ethereum blockchain. It comes as a close second when it comes to popularity compared to Bitcoin. Some people believe that Ethereum would overtake Bitcoin and become the dominant cryptocurrency in the market thanks to its versatility. Of course, you can invest in Ethereum as if it is a digital asset like Bitcoin, but the Ethereum platform is also home to token developments.

The Ethereum platform allows you to create smart contracts and tokens to fuel your ICOs. Ether would be used to fuel the transactions on the platforms. A quick refresher on the function of smart contract, it is mostly an if-then function on your excel spreadsheet. For instance, when a predetermined condition is met, the smart contract executes the corresponding contractual clause.

For instance, suppose that you are a company that manufactures and sells smartphones and you partnered up with suppliers and shipping companies. You want to make sure that your phones are manufactured properly and punctually, and that there are no labor violations (aka no slacking off during working hours), and that all parties get paid on time. For this to happen, you need a lot of contracts with your workers and the companies you work with.

But, if you use smart contracts with blockchain, it speeds up the entire process and makes everything convenient for all parties without compromising reliability. So, how does it work in this situation?

Let’s start with the contract between your company and the shipping company. It starts when the trucks come over to pick up the smartphones from the factory. The company would scan the boxes before hauling the box onto the trucks. What the scanning does is update the entry on the blockchain network, telling both parties that a certain number of products is picked up. This activates a clause in the smart contract which would release the fund from your company and send it to the shipping company. No invoice is needed.

When it comes to the labor contract between you and your worker, the system works similarly. When they come into work, they’d scan their cards and a third party would verify it so that no labor policies are violated.

Other Popular Cryptocurrencies

There are other cryptocurrencies that offer various features and it is impossible to cover every single one of them. For this reason, we will only cover some of the most popular coins to date. It is best that you do your own research and look for the coins you want to invest in yourself.

The first is Litecoin, which is launched in 2011. Litecoin is basically the lite version of Bitcoin, hence the name. It is an open-source, decentralized, and cryptography-backed cryptocurrency. The developers claimed that Litecoin would be the silver to Bitcoin’s gold. Litecoin has a faster transaction confirmation rate than Bitcoin.

Dash is released in 2014 but under the name of Darkcoin. It soon changed its name and gives more anonymity to users thanks to its decentralized mastercode network. It achieved this thanks to a Masternode, a node on its blockchain network, that forms a more robust foundation than Bitcoin.

Zcash is launched after Dash. It is quite new to the whole cryptocurrency market, but some people say that Zcash provides complete anonymity to its users since it utilizes zero-knowledge SNARKS. In simple terms, it does not record transactions at all. It does verify transactions, encrypts information, and prevents double-spending.

Monero has a unique take on privacy. For instance, it allows for complete anonymity by using a special technique called “ring signatures”. It works well enough that it became popular among users on the Dark Web.

Ripple was launched back in 2012 and offers almost instant transactions internationally at a very low cost. Instead of the conventional mining techniques that many other cryptocurrencies use, Ripple uses a consensus ledger which allows transactions to be verified without mining. This means less computational power is required to verify the transaction.

GET YOURSELF A CRYPTO WALLET

We have mentioned this time and again since the start of the book: digital wallet/crypto wallet. What is it? The wallet is an app that allows you to store your coins. It also doubles as a way to receive coins.

Cryptocurrency wallets are apps that you can run on your phone or computer. But if the idea is too abstract for you and you want to have a little something more tactile, you can get a hardware wallet or a device that runs the wallet app for you.

Digital wallets do not work the way you think they do. You might think that your coins would be stored in your crypto wallet as if they are files on a USB drive. But that is not the case. The data stored on the wallet just points to where the actual coins are in the blockchain network and the public ledger.

That said, why do you need a wallet? The whole idea behind Bitcoin and Altcoins is to act as a decentralized currency so that the common people have full control over their money. The money you put into the bank would be invested into something else to turn a profit. Basically, your bank is borrowing money from you so they can do other businesses. Except, this time, they control how much they have to give back in interest.

So, what you get is an IOU or a promise that the bank would give back your money by demand. The only problem is that’s not always the case. There were instances when the economy reaches a point where banks just do not have enough money to give back to their customers. So, a lot of people ended up losing money.

Bitcoin and Altcoins give you more power over your money. Since they are also steadily increasing in value, investing in cryptocurrencies might not be a bad idea. Plus, with such a valuable digital asset in your hands, you want to make sure that you have a safe place to store your coins.

You might be wondering that since your crypto exchange would give you a wallet anyway, would it be adequate enough. You can store your coins there, but it is best if you do not. Exchanges can be hacked and all the coins there could be whisked away in an instant.

Ideally, you want to have two wallets: hot and cold. A hot wallet is connected to the internet and a cold wallet is usually offline. Having a wallet on an exchange is fine, but you should only have coins in there when you want to use them. Once done, you should move all your coins to a cold wallet.

A cold wallet is a hardware wallet and is disconnected from the internet. This is perhaps the safest way to store your coins. Hackers cannot hack what is not connected to the internet, after all. That said, maintaining a hardware wallet can be difficult. You need to keep your private keys safe and accessible only to you.

Other than that, you need to practice all the online safety procedures such as activating two-factor authentication and not clicking on suspicious links. We have covered this extensively in the first book.

Another thing to keep in mind is that certain wallets can only hold a certain cryptocurrency. A Bitcoin wallet, for example, can only hold Bitcoins, not Ethereum. So, you can only send and receive Bitcoins. There are wallets out there that allow you to store different coins, but make sure that they are compatible with the coins you intend to own.

UNDERSTANDING CRYPTO TRANSACTIONS AND EXCHANGE

In this section, we will quickly go over how you can send and receive cryptocurrencies. We have already talked about private and public keys. The public key is what you share with others so they can send coins to your wallet. The private key is what you use to access your wallet and your coins.

So, to send your coins, log into your wallet and choose to send your coins. Keep in mind that coins transfer only works within the same blockchain network. In other words, you can only send and receive the same coins for your wallet. For instance, you can only send or receive Bitcoin since the transaction takes place on the Bitcoin blockchain network.

From there, you just put in the recipient’s public key or public wallet address and choose the amount you want to send. Keep in mind that you may have to pay a bit of a transaction fee to grease the engine depending on the cryptocurrency you are dealing with. Once you get the amount and address right, you can send your coins away.

This is the most dangerous part of the transaction. So, make sure to triple-check the address before you hit “send”. A QR code or straight-up copying the address instead of typing it in would help you avoid sending the coins to the wrong address. You may have the option to write a short note to the recipient as well to let them know why they just got some coins from you.

Receiving coins from other people is a lot easier. You just have to share your public wallet address with the sender. Some crypto wallets generate a QR code that the other person can scan so they don’t have to go through the hassle of typing out your entire address.

Other than that, keep in mind that it might take some time for the transaction to go through. The coins are usually sent almost instantaneously, but there have been cases when the transaction can take hours if the traffic is that congested.

CRYPTO EXCHANGE BASICS

In this section, we will talk about crypto exchanges. They are basically where you would buy, sell, or trade your coins for other coins or for fiat currencies. So, they are the intermediary that facilitates the buying and selling process and charge some fees for their work.

There are two types of crypto exchanges. You have the centralized crypto exchange and the decentralized exchange. Both have their strengths and drawbacks. Look for the cryptocurrency that suits your needs.

Centralized Crypto Exchanges

Centralized crypto exchanges are the most popular option. They function as the third party when you buy or sell cryptocurrencies. These exchanges are operated and controlled by a company. They are generally more reliable and user statistics prove just that. 99% of all crypto transactions take place on centralized exchanges. These include Coinbase, Gemini, Kraken, and GDAX.

There are a few advantages of using a centralized crypto exchange. For one, it is more user-friendly both in terms of interface and its inner workings. It works very much like a Forex trading platform. So, it presents a familiar face and allows beginners to ease into crypto trading faster.

Users just need to log in, take a look at their account balance, and then head straight to making transactions via apps or websites. No additional setup is needed from there.

Plus, centralized exchanges are just as reliable. Ironically, having a centralized exchange for a decentralized currency gives users another layer of protection and reliability for transactions. A highly developed and robust platform gives users higher levels of comfort.

That said, centralized crypto exchanges have their fair share of problems.

For one, there is the hacking risk. These exchanges are operated by companies that are responsible for their customers’ coins. They usually have billions of dollars worth of coins in one place, which makes them a prime target for hackers.

Then there are the problems with transaction fees. Again, these crypto exchanges operate entirely against the reasons why cryptocurrencies are created in the first place. They are meant to phase out the intermediary, cut down on the transaction cost, improving transaction speed, and offer anonymity by being a decentralized digital asset.

Crypto exchange is a prime target for hackers since they hold all the coins in one place, they act as an intermediary, and they have transaction fees. The rate at which they charge you depends on the amount you are trading with, so make sure to read up on that before you decide.

Some of the most popular centralized crypto exchanges include Binance, Coinbase, Bithumb. Bitfinex, Huobi Global, Bitstamp, FTX, bitFlyer, and KuCoin.

Decentralized Crypto Exchanges

On the other hand, there are decentralized cryptocurrency exchanges. These are not controlled by any companies. They function on peer-to-peer technology and allow for transactions without the need for a third party. The 1% of crypto users prefer this form of exchange since there are certain issues associated with centralized exchanges. Blocknet, io, Barterdex, and AirSwap are some examples of decentralized crypto exchanges.

Though unpopular, decentralized exchanges offer something that centralized exchanges just cannot.

One of its biggest strengths is the hacking risk, which is virtually none for a decentralized exchange. In this exchange, there is no third party that the coins have to go through. It’s just the sender and the recipient. So, there is no company or organization that hackers can target. It’s just a bunch of people trading coins among themselves.

Decentralized exchanges also prevent market manipulation. Since it operates on a peer-to-peer basis, it protects users from wash trading and fake trading.

Then there is the anonymity that makes cryptocurrencies so great. On a centralized exchange, you have to tell them your name, banking details, ID documents, etc. Users do not have to fill any form to get started on a decentralized exchange. No private information is shared.

So, what are the drawbacks?

For one, decentralized exchanges are not user-friendly. You have to remember the keys as well as passwords to your wallets or risk losing everything in there. You also need to get comfortable with the platform and the process, which can take some time. Centralized exchanges make everything easy and convenient even for someone who is not tech-savvy.

Decentralized exchanges also do not give the users the ability to convert their coins into fiat currencies. So, unless you already have cryptocurrencies in your wallet, getting into a decentralized exchange is less convenient.

This also presents another issue when you want to cash out on your coins. You would need to transfer them to a centralized exchange anyway to convert your coins into fiat currencies. This process incurs service fees. That said, at least this is better than having to be charged every step of the transaction.

Finally, there is the problem with liquidity. This is due to the fact that centralized exchanges are just so much more popular. So, you can have a hard time finding buyers or sellers of your coins of choice. Given how many coins there are right now, it’s clear how this can become an issue.

If you can, it is probably better for you if you familiarize yourself with centralized crypto exchanges first before transitioning to a decentralized exchange. If you are looking for one, some of the most popular exchanges include Venus, Uniswap, Tokenlon, ox Protocol, Sishiswap, BurgerSwap, Compound, Curve Finance, 1inch Exchange, and PancakeSwap.

ON THE SUBJECT OF BROKERS

Finally, there are crypto brokers. You can expect to see these people wherever there is a way to trade anything. Since cryptocurrency has proven itself to be a very popular trading vehicle, it makes sense that there would be brokers in this area as well.

Cryptocurrency brokers are another party in the ecosystem and they act as an intermediary between you, the customer, and the crypto market or exchange. So, how is it different from a crypto exchange?

Well, there isn’t too much that is different. It is just another party in the system. Sometimes, these brokers would buy up coins and also offer them to their customers at a set price. In other words, they mostly function the same way as a crypto exchange and it is another transaction fee you have to deal with in addition to that of your crypto exchange.

Why Consider Using Crypto Broker?

Again, since cryptocurrency is all about decentralization and cutting down transaction costs, why should you consider signing up on a brokerage platform? After all, they would charge you premiums for using their platforms.

Suitable for Beginners

First of all, crypto brokers put together the most user-friendly environment. Even those who are not very tech-savvy can understand the interface and get started trading cryptocurrencies easily.

Your broker may also offer cryptocurrencies at a relatively stable price that is set by the broker. They are also a better option if you only trade in small volumes. They also offer their users the ability to convert coins into certain fiat currencies.

The registration process is simple. You just sign up, verify your identity and account, select a payment method, and you can get started immediately.

Wide Range of Services

When it comes to facilitating trades, your crypto broker can hold onto the coins for you in case you do not want to deal with third-party wallets and such. This is one of the biggest reasons why people prefer to trade via a broker as they can trade without having to deal with security problems. Unlike crypto exchanges that are prone to hacking and theft, crypto brokers store their customers’ coins inside their Trezor and Ledger hardware wallets, which grants protection against cyberattacks and promoting user trust.

As always, when you are looking for which crypto broker to work with, always start with the most popular and competent platforms. Your broker should have top-notch security while also being compliant with the current national regulations. They should also be available 24/7 so that you can trade whenever you want, wherever you want, and receive support when you need it. Also, they should have a mobile platform in addition to their desktop counterpart as well.

Do You Need a Broker?

The short answer is no. No, you do not need a crypto broker to trade cryptocurrencies. You can do so directly at an exchange by yourself and they offer better trading tools to assist you in your trading game. The most you will need is a crypto exchange where you can buy and sell cryptocurrencies easily.

Your broker may have a different interest when they offer their services to you. This is perfectly illustrated by the ongoing problem with GameStop stocks. Long story short, some broker platforms stop people from buying up the stock to protect the hedge funds above them. Robinhood is guilty of such malicious practice.

Since crypto trading is all about decentralization and anonymity, you need to have a platform that you can trust. Trust is very important when there are no third parties to regulate and reverse transactions, which is simply not possible with cryptocurrencies.

You just need to use a crypto exchange with good features to help you. No more, no less. There are many crypto exchanges out there that offer you powerful tools that you can trust.

There are a number of ways you can determine whether the exchange is worth your time. They should offer high levels of liquidity, low spread fees, low purchase and withdrawal limits, high trading volume, top-notch security, insurance, as well as usability.

Spread here simply means the difference between the buying and selling prices. This is how your exchange makes money. When you sell your coins, your exchange would buy them off of you and then turn around and sell them off at a slightly higher price and pocket the price difference. Conversely, when you buy the coins, your exchange would sell them to you at a price higher than what they spent buying them, and they make a small profit out of that as well. Ideally, you want the spread to be as small as possible to maximize your profit.

Features are very important here. Some exchanges offer you invaluable tools such as stop-loss and take-profit. These tools help you bail out of the market if the price swings unfavorably to minimize loss and also let you cash out at a certain price point. It’s mostly automated so you do not have to sit there and stare at your screen all day.

Once you find the exchange you want to use, just sign up, buy the coins, and start trading. Rinse and repeat and you might just leave with more money than when you went in. It takes time and patience, but it is well worth it in the end.

The problem with crypto brokers is that they also access crypto exchanges to relay your orders. So, they also have to deal with the fees from the exchange. Since they have to make a profit, they would bump up the fees to cover costs. In the end, you end up spending more on fees if you were to trade via a broker. You still need to pay fees at an exchange, but they are much smaller.

But if you are a beginner and aren’t very familiar with crypto exchanges, you can try out a crypto brokerage platform first. Once you are comfortable, you can then move on to crypto exchanges where you can really start to make money. Of course, you might be interested in forex or the stock market if crypto trading is not working for you. In that case, some crypto brokers also allow you to trade in other markets as well.

3

HOW TO MAKE MONEY WITH CRYPTOCURRENCIES

Due to the unique properties of cryptocurrencies, there are a number of ways you can make money with cryptocurrencies. It can function both as a speculative vehicle and a currency, meaning that you can trade them as if you are in the stock market or forex market. You can also use cryptocurrencies to invest in startups or projects and get various benefits. If you’re not confident in your trading skills, you can just let your computer do the work for you and straight up mine coins and sell them off yourself.

In other words, anyone can get started earning coins and making money. It is just up to you to decide which path you want to go. In this chapter, we will go over various ways to make money in cryptocurrencies in detail.

MAKING MONEY WITH CRYPTOCURRENCY

You have several options here. The three most popular methods would be to invest, trade, and mine cryptocurrencies. They all have their strengths and drawbacks.

Trading Cryptocurrencies

Let’s start with trading cryptocurrencies. This is largely similar to Forex, or foreign exchange. If you are good at looking at charts, then you can make profits here. Forex works by trading between a pair of currencies. Since the price changes constantly, you can ride the waves. If you time it right, you can make a profit by the price differences.

Trading cryptocurrencies work on the same concept. You just swap between different cryptocurrencies and hope to make more coins at the end of the day. But, making a living off trading cryptocurrencies is unreliable at best due to the unpredictable price swings. You need to time your trades just right. If you are just starting out, you are better off getting some practice in before you get started. That means spending time going over technical analysis methods and charts. This, of course, takes time.

Another way to trade is to speculate on the price movement. We have talked about this before, but to quickly summarize, you simply bet on whether the price would move up or down.

If you believe that the price would go up, you just buy the coins and sell them off when the price rises high enough. If you believe the price would go down, you do what is called “shorting” by borrowing the coins from your exchange, sell them off, buy the same number of coins again after a predetermined period and give them back to the exchange, and pocket the price difference.

Investing

There are two ways to go about this. You can go the speculative way by buying some coins, hold onto them until the price goes up, and sell them off. This is simple, relatively safe if it is a popular coin, but it will take a long time to see any tangible benefit.

You can also use your coins to invest in projects or startups. What you get in return are tokens that can grant you special access to various services or even a share of the company’s profit. You can even sell off the tokens to make more money.

Mining

Mining is considered to be a pretty slow way to generate cryptocurrencies, but it does so at a constant rate and requires little effort on your part. The best part is that miners form a vital part of any blockchain network since they are the ones to verify transactions and add them to the blockchain. In other words, there is always a demand for miners.

You just need a beefy computer to get started. If you are a gamer, then your device should be sufficient in mining cryptocurrencies. It is a power-intensive process, though, and so your electricity bill will spike. Therein lies the risk.

You want to mine valuable coins. Only those are worth your time. There have been stories of miners who spent more on electricity bills than they earn money, so you want to maximize your profit.

INVESTING IN CRYPTOCURRENCY

A decade ago, around the time when Bitcoin started to make an appearance, if you were to tell someone that part of your investment portfolio consists of cryptocurrency, people would just laugh in your face. They may even tell you that you have no clue how to invest and probably say bad things behind your back.

After all, you would need around ten thousand Bitcoin to buy a pepperoni pizza at Papa John’s. Of course, it would not pass as a viable investment option. But that was a decade ago.

Fast forward to today, and people say that you are late to the game if you haven’t invested in cryptocurrency already. They may laugh in your face and say nasty things about you if you don’t even know cryptocurrencies or that you have never traded Bitcoin, Ethereum, or any coins in your life.

Whether you want it or not, cryptocurrency is here to stay. It is everywhere and more businesses are incorporating it into their payment system. It is no longer a conversation among geeks and day traders anymore.

This is clearly illustrated when PayPal made it possible for their customers to buy, sell, and own cryptocurrency, and to use it to buy things at 26 million different merchants. Some may ask why, and others may say that it took them long enough.

According to PayPal, the adoption was hindered by their limited potential as an instrument of exchange, citing volatility, cost, and transaction speed. That said, they also mentioned that their platform can give people a means to make cryptocurrency more accessible as a payment method.

The CEO of PayPal Dan Schulman said in a press release that the move to digital forms of cryptocurrency is inevitable. It is going to happen, but it is just a matter of how and when it is going to happen. It will bring various advantages that the current financial system could not, such as accessibility, efficiency, speed, and resilience.

This move alone is not insignificant. This push immediately addresses three of the major concerns people had with cryptocurrencies for over a decade such as legitimacy, a clearly defined and accessible marketplace, and everyday purchases.

As of now, there is a number of crypto savings accounts to choose from. Soon, you could sign up for a Bitcoin rewards credit card, which works mostly similar to the reward systems for your bank-issued credit cards.

This world’s first Bitcoin Reward Credit Card would be provided by BlockFi. They said that the reward system here is different in the sense that you get 1.5% back on the purchase in Bitcoin. In other words, you effectively get a 1.5% discount on all purchases in Bitcoin while the merchant still gets the full amount. This would encourage users to start buying Bitcoin and use it to make everyday purchases.

To top it all off, merchants can also introduce additional discounts on their products to encourage users to pay via cryptocurrency. Since the transaction cost is lower, merchants have a little more wiggle room to allow special discounts, which further encourages people to start paying with cryptocurrencies.

As of now, BlockFi’s credit card is still on the waiting list. We do not have an exact date for its eventual release yet, but this is another push into getting cryptocurrencies widely adopted.

So, what does all of this mean? It means that more and more businesses are finding more ways to make cryptocurrency useful. In other words, cryptocurrency could become mainstream in the near future. That said, should you invest in cryptocurrency? Opinions are divided still and the answer varies depending on who you ask.

Why You Should Consider Investing in Crypto

The Associate Director of Product Management at Gemini Claire Lovell said that Bitcoin has reached an all-time high, just like it did in 2016. The difference that suggests the price will not crash this time around is the fact that financial institutions and businesses are now working to find ways to incorporate cryptocurrencies into their payment systems.

Lovell said that cryptocurrencies give people higher levels of independence, opportunity, and choice in their finances. Cryptocurrency provides a decentralized and open-source financial system that addresses many shortcomings that our current financial systems have by making it accessible directly to consumers. In simple terms, it means it is easier than ever to buy, sell, store, and trade.

The fact that cryptocurrency is still in its infancy works as an argument for and against it. Critics will say that it has yet proven itself to be a reliable store of value and that it would fade into obscurity very soon. They would say that it is just a fad. On the other hand, this presents a unique opportunity for investors. If you invest in cryptocurrency now, even though the price is high, you would be among the first adopters. The price could still go up even higher, so you can cash in at the top and make bank.

Investing in such a new investment vehicle the like of Bitcoin has great potential as it could become something very big in the future. The loss? Though some would say that the loss would be equally massive, keep in mind that you are not going to lose more money than you put in since the price cannot go into the negative. The most it will go is zero, and the loss incurred then would be the entire amount you put into Bitcoin or Altcoins.

The Case Against Cryptocurrency

That said, not everyone believes that investing in cryptocurrencies is a good idea. At least, they do not think an average investor should concern themselves with crypto investments. There are a number of reasons for this.

The biggest one, again, is price volatility. While it is fun to ride the waves, the price swings are definitely not for the faint-hearted. There are yet to be sufficient regulations to prevent fraud or theft that we see in other markets.

Hacking remains a major threat in the cryptocurrency ecosystem. The blockchain network is virtually impenetrable, but other parts of the ecosystem such as the digital wallet and crypto exchanges remain particularly vulnerable. Hackers can get in, steal all your coins, and there is little you can do about it once that happens.

Plus, there is the fact that cryptocurrency is not the only investment option you have out there. Unless you really, really hate the current financial system, there are other ways to make use of your money without having to deal with the aforementioned threats.

For one, if you want to invest in something and hope the price will go up, there is the stock market. Its speculations are all the same here, except the prices are a lot more stable. If you are looking to invest in startups, having some tokens to your name seems appealing, but it’s just a more convenient way to verify your contributions and to enjoy the promised perks. There are other ways to do that without the use of tokens.

MINING CRYPTOCURRENCY

If you are not into trading cryptocurrencies or investing in them, then you can always try to dabble in cryptocurrency mining. As mentioned before, miners are always in demand as they are the ones who allow the whole blockchain network to function. Since there is no central authority to verify transactions or anything, the task falls to them.

Every blockchain network has to have miners. They use their computers to solve complex mathematical problems to verify transactions. As a token of appreciation, the miners are awarded a small amount of cryptocurrency. How many coins you get from mining depends on the coin.

We have mentioned Proof of Work before, but there are two types of mining. There is the Proof of Work (PoW), and Proof of Stake (PoS). These two types of mining verify transactions and update the ledger using consensus algorithms.

Proof of Work

To put it simply, the Proof of Work consensus model requires miners to solve mathematical problems. This is the system for Bitcoin, but it requires a lot of computational power. What are they calculating for? Miners generate what is called “hash”, which turns an input into a random string of letters and numbers. The idea is to find the right string to match the target.

As you can see, the probability is pretty low. However, given how fast computers perform these calculations in a given second, it is not impossible. In fact, miners’ rigs can generate trillions of computations every second. So, it takes them an average of nine to ten minutes to find the right one.

The problem is that it only awards the people who completed the verification first. That means, whoever generates the right one first takes the coins, so not everyone is a winner here. And you can say that the competition is fair, but it isn’t between humans and their abilities. It depends completely on people having better and more expensive hardware.

For instance, there are ASICs in the Bitcoin network and they are the point of controversy. ASICs are specialized devices that do a very good job in mining coins. They are expensive, sure, but they mine coins at a rate with which no other machine can really compete. So, their presence in the ecosystem means that no one other than the people who own ASICs can make a living off mining coins.

Then, there are other problems with the system. The first and most obvious one would be energy consumption. Mining Bitcoin consumes the same amount of energy on average as that of Switzerland because of the PoW system. As more miners join the ecosystem to mine for coins, the energy use is only going to go up.

Then there is the centralization element of this system, despite the fact that cryptocurrencies are all about decentralization. For one, if a mining entity can take over 50% +1 of the entire blockchain network’s hash rate, then the owner can double-spend, block transactions, or bend the rule temporarily. That is highly unlikely for larger cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin, however.

Sometimes, the problem isn’t concentrated on one individual. The system is somewhat centralized because a few mining pools can control up to around 50% of Bitcoin’s computational power already. As of now, developers are working to alleviate this issue.

Proof of Stake

Proof of Stake works by having the miners, in this case “validators”, stake some of their Ether. That amount would be frozen and cannot be used for anything while the validators participate in the proof-of-stake process. When they stake their Ether, they make themselves available to be chosen to propose a new block to be added to the chain.

The selection process is entirely random, of course. Once chosen, other validators attest that they have seen the block. Once enough attestations for the block are gathered, the block is added to the blockchain.

Validators are awarded when they attest to a new block and say that it is accurate and it follows the rule. That is it. Then, they would be awarded a small number of coins. The one chosen to propose a block would also be awarded.

Of course, one can easily see how this system can be abused. This is why there are punishments in place for breaking the rule. If the validator proposes a block with false data history, or transaction, their stake would be slashed and they would be banned from the network. But for smaller infractions such as going offline when they are chosen to propose a block, there are equally smaller penalties.

The biggest advantage of the PoS consensus model is the fact that it consumes less energy. In fact, you do not have to worry much about the initial investment in hardware or the power bill. This makes this mining method a lot more accessible to an average person. You just need a working computer and a solid internet connection.

The only investment you need to make is to invest a certain amount of crypto to be qualified to be a validator. But if it is still too much for you, there are even staking pools. These are groups of people who put together their coins and this pool would stake on everyone’s behalf. The reward would then be distributed proportionally to the percentage of their contribution to the pool. So, you can just chip in a little bit of coin and you can get started with them. Just don’t expect to get paid much if you only contribute a little bit of coin to the pool.

This also opens up the door for participation and allows many people a new way to generate revenue. Considering that you do not need much to get started in PoS-based cryptocurrency, many people would jump into mining here. Since everyone is chosen to propose and attest to new blocks at random, everyone has an equal chance to earn their keep.

Another thing worth mentioning is that it is scalable, meaning that you stand a higher chance of being chosen if you stake more of your coins. Of course, if something goes wrong, you would lose your coins, but you shouldn’t run into this problem if you follow the rules.

Increasing how many coins you stake is also interesting. For Ethereum at least, the starting cost would be 32 ETH or Ether. Staking this many Ethers would give you “one” validator. If you want to stake more, you need to dump in another 32 ETH, which would give you another validator. You cannot stake 33, or 34, or 63 ETH. You need to do it in multiples of 32, each of which gets you one validator. If you have multiples of validators, your chance increases accordingly. Having 10 validators boosts your chance of being picked tenfold compared to having only one validator.

This is also another reason why staking pools are popular. People chip in bits of Ether and the aggregate Ether the pool has can get them many validators. This means, a staking pool can have tens or even hundreds of validators running at the same time, which massively improves the chance for the pool to get picked.

Mining Hardware

We have mentioned mining hardware before, so what is that? Actually, it can be a simple computer that you already have at home or a purpose-built machine that can churn out coins at an alarming rate. We went over this in the previous book, so check that out for more details. But here is a quick refresher.

There are a number of ways to mine cryptocurrency. You have CPU mining, GPU mining, cloud mining, and ASIC mining. The first two just utilize computer hardware that you already have and the last two make mining a bit more interesting.

CPU Mining

Let’s start with CPU mining. CPU mining simply uses your computer processor to mine cryptocurrencies. It was initially the best way to mine cryptocurrencies. But, as the technology improved and a better understanding of cryptocurrencies was established, this quickly became obsolete.

This is for two main reasons. The first is that CPU mining is just super slow. You can actually leave it running for months on end without making any tangible revenue. That’s how bad it is. Then, there is the problem of electricity and cooling. Your CPU can heat up very quickly though, and if you subject it to crypto mining without some beefy cooling, you can cook an egg on it after leaving it running for 15 minutes.

That said, at least CPU mining is the most accessible option since every computer comes with a CPU. So, if you really want to, and it is highly ill-advised to do so, you can start crypto mining using your CPU. You just need some software to get started and you are set. CPU mining will get you started, but chances are that it won’t get you off the ground any time soon.

GPU Mining

GPU mining is possibly the most popular crypto mining method today. This is because GPU rigs can churn out coins at a respectable speed without spending too much on them. A quick Google search for cryptocurrency mining and chances are that you will find GPU rigs among the top results.

GPU mining is far more efficient and cheaper than CPU mining. Of course, the initial investment in a GPU mining rig can be costly, but it can process a lot more than your computer’s processor alone.

GPU rigs consist of a motherboard, a processor, a rig frame, a cooling system, and some graphic cards. Some could have a modest number of two GPUs whereas others pack eight of them inside the frame.

A decent GPU mining rig would set you back around $3,000. This might be a hefty investment, but keep in mind that it will pay itself a lot faster than a CPU rig. If you are looking to get serious about crypto mining, this is a great place to start.

It is also worth pointing out that certain graphic card companies such as Nvidia are pushing out graphic cards that are built for mining alone. You can tell if it is a mining graphics card if it does not have a port for display output.

The introduction of a new line of mining graphics cards caused outrage among gamers who are looking to upgrade their computers. The problem here is that, at the moment of writing, there is a global shortage of chips that are needed to build GPUs.

Nvidia’s 3000 series of graphics cards are already snatched up by scalpers who would resell them at marked-up prices. So, the supply for those graphic cards is already running low. Instead of putting more supplies into manufacturing more graphics cards, Nvidia decided to create mining graphic cards instead with the little supply they had. This is seen as a move to latch onto the latest cryptocurrency trend and people are not very happy about it.

Nvidia is also working to discourage people from using their latest 3000 series graphic cards to mine by simply reducing the hash rate, which basically slows down the mining speed of those cards. So, if you are in the market for mining graphic cards, make sure to look at the hash rate.

Cloud Mining

But let’s say that your computer sucks. Your Intel processor from the early 2000s can’t run the latest Windows Media Player, Google Chrome, and Microsoft Word at the same time without it reaching boiling temperature. You also happen to be in a bad spot and don’t have enough money to spare for a mining rig. Yet, you want to get into the crypto mining game. What are your options?

There is cloud mining. It is the most popular way to mine cryptocurrencies with little to no effort on your part. The idea is simple. Some people who have a lot of money decided to make a business out of mining cryptocurrency. So, they bought up a bunch of GPU mining rigs and let people borrow them to mine cryptocurrencies. It’s that simple.

Many cloud mining companies normally have massive mining facilities with multiple farms located in areas where power is cheap. Their farms usually have hundreds of GPU rigs stacked and working simultaneously. The people maintaining the farms and offering cloud mining services are tech-savvy and know full well how to mine cryptocurrencies.

Given the fact that you can just borrow their rig, it is not hard to see why it is as popular as it is today. If you don’t have enough money, your computer is just inadequate, or you are not interested in owning a crypto mining rig, then cloud mining is your best bet. All you really need is some cash and an internet connection.

Cloud mining can be free if you use certain providers. You might think that it would be a better deal, but it usually comes with very slow mining speeds, extra conditions, etc. Plus, they are designed in a way that you would be familiarized with their platform and is more willing to pay to get better service from them.

Your cloud mining provider should provide plans going from the cheapest to the most expensive one, with different perks. Some hosts might give you the ability to tinker with your cloud mining plan and get a custom price. You are looking to pay from anywhere from $500 to $5,000.

It might sound expensive, and it kind of is. You do not own the mining rig permanently. There is a term and it usually lasts a few years. You might get a lifetime deal with your provider too, but you might have to fork over some extra cash for that to happen. Such is the price for convenience.

Speaking of price, how would you get paid? You just have to register an account with your provider, pay them, and put in your cryptocurrency wallet code, and you are good to go. Throughout your rental period, all the earnings that your rig generates would be sent to your wallet. At least, the remaining value after electricity and maintenance costs are deducted.

On average, it is expected that cloud mining would pay itself in about six months to one year. From that point, you start to rack up profit. But this is just an average estimate since the prices for coins tend to fluctuate.

ASIC Mining

Finally, we have ASIC mining. ASIC is Application-Specific Integrated Circuit. These are special devices created specifically to mine cryptocurrency. These are the most powerful rigs to mine cryptocurrencies. They are so fast that they leave GPU and CPU rigs in the dust.

Now, you might be wondering why aren’t they are popular as GPU rigs if they are so good. That is because it is just too good.

When the ASIC company announced the introduction of their mining rig, there was an uproar among those in the crypto community. They wanted these things banned because they are so powerful that other miners just have no way to compete. That means ASIC owners can hoard all the coins because other miners just cannot keep up with the high hash speeds.

Think of it this way. If the majority of earnings just go to a few miners who have enough money to build an ASIC farm, what kind of chaos do you think would ensue? Not only that they have all the coins to themselves, but they can also potentially control the cryptocurrency blockchain network as well since they can verify over 50% of all transactions on the network and therefore could technically control the entire network.

Although, nowadays, the general sentiment around ASICs isn’t that harsh anymore and it is slowly becoming more affordable. In fact, more and more people are migrating to using ASICs. There are many ASIC devices out there with specific tasks. This is its strength and also weakness.

You see, ASIC devices are built to mine a specific coin. Depending on how they are designed, they can either mine a number of different coins or only one coin. It all comes down to the algorithm it uses. So, if your ASIC miner can only mine a cryptocurrency, but then there is an update to the mining algorithm, then you end up with a very expensive paperweight with no resale value.

ASIC miners also consume quite a lot of power, even the most efficient device, can run pretty hot and loud as well. The upfront expense, niched application, and lack of resale value make it a difficult recommendation as a mining rig.

For GPU mining rig, you can at least use the graphic cards for your computer when you are done mining or resell it to gamers out there looking to upgrade their computers. You cannot say the same for ASIC miners.

There is another danger on the horizon, though. Some people are starting to mine cryptocurrencies other than Bitcoin. More specifically, they are flocking toward Ethereum because of its updated consensus model, to a Proof of Stake (PoS) system, which does not require much computing power to mine. That means, less power is consumed and less money is spent on a pricey mining machine.

But perhaps this would create an opportunity for Bitcoin miners who have ASIC mining rigs because there are fewer competitors and they can start to rake in profit when people start to migrate. Only time will tell.

What Cryptocurrency to Mine?

As mentioned before, you want to squeeze as much value out of your mining rig as possible. That means mining the right coins so you can sell them off and make bank. But what coins to mine?

Well, that’s easy. You go for the most valuable coins to mine. That means Bitcoin, Dash, Ethereum, Litecoin, etc. Just go to Coinbase or Binance and look at the most valuable coins on the list. That should give you a good idea where to start mining.

Another interesting thing to note is what rig you use to mine cryptocurrency. For instance, if you have a mining rig or got yourself a deal with a cloud mining service provider, you can definitely start mining Bitcoin as they use the PoW consensus model. That said, mining Bitcoin is probably going to be difficult since it is so popular that everyone wants a piece of that Bitcoin pie. Miners across the globe would go into the few mining pools on the network and try to snatch a small amount of coin. You might have to wait for hours until you start to see the first Bitcoin trickle in.

However, if you are not interested in owning and maintaining a rig or cannot afford one, you can look for coins that use the PoS consensus model. Ethereum would then be the perfect option, but you want to hold onto that thought for now since they are still undergoing a renovation. Ethereum 2.0 would incorporate the PoS consensus model into their network and it is set to fully launch in 2021 or 2022, so keep an eye out and jump in once everything is set up. This is probably your best bet.

Now, you might be wondering what if you go for the less popular coins and start mining them since there would be fewer competitors. You can definitely do that and you can perhaps churn out quite a number of coins without using up too much energy. The problem then would be the coin prices.

Bitcoin alone takes up over 50% of the total market cap and is worth over $55,000 for each coin. Ethereum comes in second place with only over $2,000 for each coin. You can clearly see the price disparity between the two coins and it only gets lower the further you go down the list.

And maybe you can tolerate the low price. So long as you make a lot of coins, you can still rake in all the profit anyway. But then there is the problem of power consumption, time, and security to consider, depending on whether the cryptocurrency uses the PoW or PoS consensus model.

In a cryptocurrency that uses the PoW consensus model, you would need to consider power consumption since your device would need to perform a lot of calculations. The problem with mining small cryptocurrencies with this consensus model is that there aren’t many transactions here, so chances are that you won’t be making a ton of coins every second.

You might still be making more coins than Bitcoin, but their cumulative value would be very small and you might have problems selling them off. Because, again, the coin you are mining is not popular so there will not be many people who would want to trade these coins.

Again, you might be fine with that. So long as you get to dump all your coins off eventually, that would be okay. But then there is the power bill to consider. Unfortunately, although the coins are cheap and you can mine a lot of them, your miner would still run at full power and consume a lot of energy. The coins you make are most likely not going to cover even the power bill, let alone generate a profit for you. For this reason, you want to make the most out of your power consumption by mining more popular cryptocurrencies.

But what about the cryptocurrencies that use the PoS consensus model? For that, power consumption will not be an issue. Time could be an issue since everyone is chosen randomly, but you can also increase your odds of being chosen by getting more validators. Since there aren’t many people around and that the cryptocurrency is cheap, you do not have to spend much in order to stand a good chance of getting picked. But then there is the security problem.

We need to understand that the blockchain networks that power popular cryptocurrencies are virtually impenetrable because of the sheer number of users on the network. As mentioned before, each user and miner would be given a ledger that updates itself independently from the rest. These ledgers check and validate each other’s data to ensure that there won’t be any counterfeiting. The alteration of data is only possible when someone controls a majority of the node.

In popular PoS cryptocurrencies, this is not possible because of the number of users and validators on the network. It would require way more money than you think to take over the blockchain that way. It is very much possible with not-so-popular cryptocurrencies, however.

You can buy many cheap coins and use them to stake so you have a lot of validators to your name. Since the coin is not so popular, there won’t be many users on the network, so you will eventually reach a critical point where you have a majority of control over the network. Then, you can just alter data and double-spend at your leisure. That is fine for people who want to watch the world burn, but not so fun for miners.

To be clear, this threat still applies to small PoW cryptocurrencies, except it is even more expensive to carry out that attack. You would need to have a lot of money and have the mining rigs powerful enough for the attack. It is expensive and really not worth it for the attacker, most of the time. In PoS cryptocurrencies, you just need to have money.

For all the reasons above, it is not recommended that you mine smaller cryptocurrencies. It’s just not worth all the trouble.

Crypto Mining Legality

One of the most common questions people ask is whether mining cryptocurrency is legal. Generally, yes. But that really depends on your government and your mining method. Unfortunately, governments across the globe view cryptocurrency differently, so the answer won’t be a solid “yes” across the board.

Certain governments ban the possession of cryptocurrency, let alone mining or using it. They are usually quite vocal about it, so a quick search on the internet should give you a pretty solid idea of what your government’s stance is on the coins.

When it comes to the mining method, it means you should not use illegal means to mine coins. You see, some hackers would use Javascript on browsers or any other means to install malware on your computer. They are not after your data, but they want your computer’s processing power. This is called cryptojacking, which is highly illegal.

Then, there are taxes to consider. After all the fees are cut by your broker or exchange, and the bank transfer, you may have to deal with another tax from your government. It is money, after all, so you can guess that your government would be interested in having a share of your spoils.

Crypto Mining Pool

The reward for verifying the block is granted to the miner who found the right hash first in the PoW system. However, given how large the Bitcoin blockchain network is, your chance of getting there first is highly unlikely.

Your probability of finding the valid hash is equal to the portion your mining rig contributes to the overall mining power on the network. So, if your machine only contributes 0.001% to the total mining power, that is your chance of finding the hash first. That is not a good probability.

So, miners with weak rigs stand a very small chance of actually getting any coin. Therefore, they band together to create a mining pool. That means, miners would pool their computational power over a network to mine the coins. In this case, the aggregate computational power might go up to 15% share if you band together with a few thousand others, and suddenly your odds of making money are pretty good.

That said, the pool mines for every member. So, the reward is divided proportionally according to the amount of computational power you contribute to the pool. If there are a few thousand people in the pool, everyone gets their fair share. For you, that means you won’t get a lot, but it is better than your 0.001% probability. So, you should definitely join a crypto mining pool.

CRYPTO TRADING

Cryptocurrency trading wasn’t the hottest thing when Bitcoin just launched. That is because there wasn’t any other cryptocurrency in the market yet. Right now, that is a different story. There are over a thousand cryptocurrencies in the market, so the possibilities are endless here.

Trading cryptocurrency is simple. You exchange one cryptocurrency for another and hope that the coins you have increase in value. You can do this at your crypto exchange and they will charge you a small fee for doing this. Crypto trading is probably the next hottest thing right now as billions of dollar’s worth of coins are being traded every day.

Savvy traders, with a little bit of luck, can make bank just by trading cryptocurrency. There are plenty of people who do this full-time from the comfort of their own homes. But to do so would require a lot of skill and knowledge of the market.

For this, traders use a number of tools to monitor the market and price movement. This is so that they can choose to grab the right coin at the right time. Among their repertoire of tools, they have software to monitor previous price trends among others.

This might sound intimidating to you already, but worry not. Everyone has to start somewhere, after all. So long as you trade with the money you are willing and can afford to lose, there is no danger in giving this a shot.

There are two ways to trade cryptocurrency. You can put together a short-term strategy or a long-term strategy.

Short-Term Trading

As the name suggests, short-term trading involves the purchase of cryptocurrency that you plan on holding onto for a short time before selling it off again. How long? That depends on you. It can be from minutes to a few months.

A short-term strategy is effective when you expect a temporary rise in price in the near future. If that is true, then you can sell the coins for a quick profit before the price drops again.

Short-term trading has a number of advantages. For one, it provides a good opportunity to have a high return on investment. In forex, or foreign exchange, the prices for the currency pair hardly move by more than 1% every day. So, the only way to make a lot of money from that would be through leveraging, which is basically a multiplier that amplifies your profits (and losses).

In cryptocurrency, their prices can double (or cut in half) overnight. With over 1,000 currencies to choose from (compared to 100 something of fiat currencies), you have a lot of options. That also means that there are large trading volumes for many coins.

This is important because you can usually find buyers or sellers for a certain cryptocurrency. There are a lot of coins flowing in and out in the exchange across all the cryptocurrencies.

Of course, you have a few other things to consider as well. Again, volatility rears its ugly head. It’s a double-edged sword. If you time it right, you can win big. If not, you would lose a lot of money. The key here would be to carefully analyze the market, have a solid entry and exit strategy and maybe a couple of contingency plans, and commit through it without panicking.

Keep in mind that you need to have a cool head if you are to pull this off successfully. Remain calm as you weather through the choppy sea of the crypto market. One thing you will learn right off the bat is that you do not always win. There are factors outside your control that no one can anticipate. It gets very stressful very quickly when you see how prices move against you.

So, you sometimes just have to bite the bullet, take the L, and pull out before losses mount. Bail out and try again later. Even the best trader on the market do not make a profit 100% of the time, so chances are that you won’t either.

Also, understand that short-term crypto traders are only after the small price movement. So, you have to see the subtle details on the charts to make the most out of it. If you are a complete beginner, this can be tricky.

Another drawback is that you need to make large investments if you want to see a chunky return on investment. This is something that many people aren’t very comfortable with, especially beginners. You either put in a bit of money and make almost no profit or put in a fair chunk of your money and risk losing it over a couple of bad trading decisions.

Long-Term Trading

Long-term trading involves a lot of waiting time, which might also involve a lot of nail-biting moments. The term “HODL” is pretty common in this context and only the beginners do not know what it means.

HODL stands for “Hold On for Dear Life”. As you might have guessed, this simple plan involves holding onto the coins for over a year and weather the volatility storm. That said, the price swings are to be expected, but you are expecting to see a slow increase in value over a long time. It will take a while, but you are going to get there and you can cash in. The whole plan hinges on you doing absolutely nothing, which is easier said than done.

A good example of this would be the early investors in Bitcoin. Sure, people would laugh in your face if you invested in Bitcoin back when you needed over 10,000 of those to get a pizza. Around 2011, the price was just $0.35 a pop. If they held onto the coins until 2017, they would be able to sell them all off for around $20,000 each. In other words, you would get around 57,000 times of your initial investment. You have to wonder what those people are doing right now.

So, what kind of benefits does this long-term strategy bring? For one, all it takes is time. You buy some coins and sit on them for a while. It is that simple. That means, you do not have to understand the charts or graphs since you just hold onto the coins for a long time.

Plus, you do not need to waste time anxiously checking the price movement every five minutes. You can do it when you are free or whenever you want. It does not really matter much since you are not doing anything else anyway.

You can also treat this like investing in stock, not to grow wealth, but rather to develop good habits. The idea is simple. You start off with whatever cash you can afford to give away and put that forward to buy a couple of coins. Whenever you have some cash to spare, put that into buying more coins. You can even say that you would spare just $100 every month and you would put that into a cryptocurrency. You do that every month over a long period of time until it is time to reap your harvest when you are near retirement or when you need some emergency cash. This strategy lets you avoid the stress of market volatility since there is no need to fuss about short-term price movements.

Of course, no plans are without their own flaws. The biggest one is that you will miss opportunities that would otherwise allow you to make short-term profits. Because of price volatility, sometimes it may jump up momentarily before settling back down again. Short-term traders would wait for this moment to make a quick profit.

You may also notice another major flaw in this strategy. It is the fact that you are betting on the price going up in the future.
